# Changelog

*June 2026 — history rewritten from a single 211-commit main branch into
7 feature-phase branches with per-commit surgical splits, preserving
all file content, release tags, and GPG signatures. Original history
preserved at `backup/2026-06-06-original`.*


All notable, consumer-facing changes to Aqueduct (`aqueduct-core`) are
recorded here.

Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/);
versioning follows [SemVer](https://semver.org/). The stability contract
applies from v1.0.0 — during alpha/RC, breaking changes may land in any
release and are marked **BREAKING**.

## [Unreleased]

### Removed

- **BREAKING: agentic (tool-calling) heal mode is removed outright, with no shim.** `agent.mode` no longer exists at either level, so `agent.mode: agentic` (and the explicit `oneshot` spelling) now fails schema validation naming the key instead of being accepted; every heal is the single prompt to PatchSpec turn that `oneshot` always described. Removed with it: `agent.max_tool_calls` and `agent.supports_tools` (both levels, plus the per-tier `cascade[].supports_tools` override), the per-heal `ToolBox` (`aqueduct/agent/toolbox.py`), the internal read-only diagnostics registry it called (`aqueduct/tools/`, `ToolRegistry`, `call_tool`, and the reserved `aqueduct.tools` entry-point group), the provider-side tool-use loops for both Anthropic and OpenAI-compatible endpoints (`ToolCallState`, the tools payload builders, the `tool_use`/`tool_result` and `tool_calls` round trips, and the one-time `[agent_tools_unsupported]` degrade warning), the prompt's "Tools available" addendum (`PROMPT_VERSION` bumped), the `AttemptRecord.tool_calls` counter and the transcript's per-call tool rendering, and the optional `sample_source_rows` executor-protocol hook with both engines' implementations of it. The `heal_attempts.tool_calls_json` column is dropped from the DDL and from the migration list, and nothing writes it any more; a pre-upgrade store keeps the column with its historical rows and needs no migration. `read_source_schema` is UNAFFECTED and stays a supported protocol hook: `aqueduct drift` is its consumer, not the deleted toolbox. The decision was measured, not assumed: an A/B over the full `gallery/aqscenarios/` suite on one model had agentic losing on pass rate (17/19 vs 18/19), costing about 2.17x the input tokens, and running about 24% slower, recorded in `docs/benchmarks/agentic-vs-oneshot-2026-09-04.md`. (`aqueduct/agent/toolbox.py`, `aqueduct/tools/`, `aqueduct/agent/{loop,cascade,providers,prompts,budget,transcript}.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/{ddl,surveyor,scenario}.py`, `aqueduct/cli/{__init__,run,run_setup,heal,benchmark}.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/{schema,models,parser}.py`, `aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/{engine,schema_reader}.py`, both engines' `capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/templates/default/`; tests: `tests/test_tools/`, `tests/test_agent/{test_agentic_loop,test_providers_tools,test_toolbox,test_toolbox_engine_routing}.py`, the `tools-tests` CI lane; docs: `docs/specs.md` (8.10 and 8.12 deleted), `docs/observability_guide.md`, `docs/extending.md`, `docs/threat_model.md`, `SKILL.md`, `AGENTS.md`)
- **BREAKING: the signature-keyed heal cache (`aqueduct/agent/memory.py`) is removed outright, with no shim.** Pending-patch reuse, exact replay, and signature-matched coaching-example retrieval — the three zero-token paths the LLM heal loop consulted before calling the model — are gone. `aqueduct/agent/memory.py` (`find_pending`, `find_replay_candidate`, `find_coaching_examples`, `PendingHit`, `ReplayCandidate`, `CoachingExample`, `contains_set_engine_config`) is deleted; `aqueduct/patch/index.py` keeps its lifecycle functions (`PatchIndexRow`, `ensure_schema`, `upsert`, `set_status`, `get`, `recent_applied`, `list_by_status`) but loses the three cache queries (`find_pending`, `find_replay`, `find_coaching`). `agent.memory` is no longer a valid `aqueduct.yml` block — `AgentMemoryConfig` is gone and setting `agent.memory` now fails schema validation instead of being silently accepted. The coaching-examples prompt section ("## Past validated fixes for similar failures") never renders any more; every heal now composes the chronological "Previous patch attempts (do NOT repeat these)" section instead — `PROMPT_VERSION` bumped `1.13` → `1.14`. `healing_outcomes.resolution` no longer takes the value `replayed` (or `cached`) going forward — `llm` is the only value a run writes; a pre-2.3.0 store may still carry historical rows with the old values. Repeat failures now cost LLM tokens like any other failure — see the pending-patch short-circuit below, which replaces the reuse behavior for the one case that mattered operationally (an unreviewed patch already sitting in `patches/pending/`). (`aqueduct/agent/memory.py`, `aqueduct/patch/index.py`, `aqueduct/agent/prompts.py`, `aqueduct/agent/loop.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_memory.py`, `tests/test_agent/test_coaching.py`, `tests/test_agent/test_heal_cache_index.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_heal_cache.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §8.1/§8.2, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `docs/cli_reference.md`, `AGENTS.md`)
- **BREAKING: `aqueduct mcp serve`, the `aqueduct/mcp/` package, and the `[mcp]` extra are gone, with no shim.** The stdio MCP diagnostics server that exposed the read-only `aqueduct/tools/` `ToolRegistry` over the Model Context Protocol (for MCP clients such as Claude Desktop or an IDE) has been removed outright: `pip install aqueduct-core[mcp]` no longer resolves, `aqueduct mcp serve` no longer exists as a CLI command, and there is no compatibility shim or deprecation warning. The underlying `ToolRegistry` (`aqueduct/tools/`) is unaffected and continues to back the agentic-heal `ToolBox`; only the MCP transport on top of it is removed. Diagnostics remain available through the existing CLI equivalents (`aqueduct report`, `aqueduct runs`, `aqueduct lineage`, `aqueduct doctor`). (`aqueduct/mcp/`, `aqueduct/cli/mcp.py`; tests: `tests/test_mcp/`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_mcp.py`; docs: `docs/cli_reference.md`, `docs/specs.md` §8.10)
- **BREAKING: the `aqueduct blueprint` command group and its `history` subcommand, the `blueprint_history` diagnostics tool, and `stores/queries.py::blueprint_history`/`git_blueprint_commits` are removed, with no shim.** The blueprint-history feature merged the store-side remediation timeline (patch apply/reject, heal run starts, outcomes) with the blueprint file's git commit history into one chronological view, exposed as both `aqueduct blueprint history <id|blueprint.yml>` and the `blueprint_history` registry/agentic-mode tool. All of it — `aqueduct/cli/blueprint.py`, the two query functions and the `BlueprintHistoryEvent` dataclass in `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, and the tool registration in `aqueduct/tools/registry.py` — is deleted outright, with no compatibility shim or deprecation warning. (`aqueduct/cli/blueprint.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `aqueduct/tools/registry.py`, `aqueduct/agent/toolbox.py`; tests: `tests/test_stores/test_blueprint_history.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_blueprint_history.py`; docs: `docs/cli_reference.md`, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `docs/specs.md` §8.10/§8.12)
- **BREAKING: `agent.regression_artifact` and post-heal `.aqtest.yml` generation are removed, with no shim.** Both the `aqueduct.yml` engine-wide default (`AgentConnectionConfig.regression_artifact`) and the Blueprint `agent:` block's per-pipeline override (`AgentSchema.regression_artifact`, the `None` = inherit engine default field) are gone, along with `aqueduct/agent/regression_artifact.py` (`generate`, `RegressionArtifactResult`) and the opt-in call site in the heal loop that invoked it after a successful heal. A successful heal no longer optionally emits a CI regression `.aqtest.yml` under `aqtests/` next to the Blueprint; `agent/signature.py` (the error-signature engine) is unaffected. Setting `agent.regression_artifact` in either `aqueduct.yml` or a Blueprint now raises a schema-validation error (`extra`/unknown-field rejection) instead of being silently accepted. (`aqueduct/agent/regression_artifact.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_regression_artifact.py`, `tests/test_parser/test_regression_artifact_schema.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §8.9)
- **BREAKING: the `ci` approval mode, the `webhooks.on_ci_patch` event, and `agent.ci_webhook_url` are removed, with no shim.** `agent.approval` (engine-wide `aqueduct.yml` default and Blueprint `agent:` override) no longer accepts `"ci"` as a value — a Blueprint or config setting it now fails schema validation naming the field, instead of the run loop silently POSTing a staged patch to `agent.ci_webhook_url` and firing `webhooks.on_ci_patch`. Both config fields (`AgentConnectionConfig.ci_webhook_url`, `WebhooksConfig.on_ci_patch`) and the `effective_mode == "ci"` staging branch in the run loop are deleted outright, along with `docs/templates/ci-heal-workflow.yml`. `aqueduct patch pr`, `aqueduct patch import`, `aqueduct patch commit`, and the `on_patch_pending` webhook are UNCHANGED and remain the supported path for handing a staged patch to CI: stage under `approval: human`, receive `on_patch_pending`, then call `patch import` (or `patch pr` for a one-shot branch+PR) exactly as before. `aqueduct/patch/ci.py` itself is unaffected — it is the shared heal-as-PR / patch-import kit backing those three commands, not part of this removal. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/patch/apply.py`, `docs/templates/ci-heal-workflow.yml`; tests: `tests/test_parser/test_approval_rename.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_guardrails_rollback.py`, `tests/test_surveyor/test_agent.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_exit_code_wiring.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §8.3, `docs/production_guide.md`, `docs/cli_reference.md`, `SKILL.md`, `AGENTS.md`)
- **BREAKING: the plan-regression gate (formerly Gate 4) and `aqueduct/patch/explain_gate.py` are removed, with no shim.** The post-patch Spark `explain()`-plan regression check (`Exchange`/`BroadcastExchange`/`BatchEvalPython` node counts vs. a captured baseline) is gone outright, along with both places it could be tuned: the `aqueduct.yml` engine-wide default (`AgentConnectionConfig.block_on_explain_regression`) and the Blueprint `agent:` block's per-pipeline override (`AgentSchema.block_on_explain_regression`). The `observability.explain_snapshot` table is no longer written by any run; existing tables are left in place (they may be dropped manually) and there is deliberately no migration. `stores/queries.py::plan_metrics` and its `PlanMetricRow` dataclass, the `explain` value of `patch_simulation.gate`, and the `explain_capture` optional kwarg on the executor protocol (`ExecutorProtocol`/`OPTIONAL_EXECUTE_KWARGS`, `run_polyglot`, both engines' `execute()`) are all removed with them. The gate pyramid is now guardrails → compile-check → lineage → sandbox → resolvability, with resolvability renumbered from Gate 5 to Gate 4; setting either config field now raises a schema-validation error instead of being silently accepted. (`aqueduct/patch/explain_gate.py`, `aqueduct/patch/preview.py`, `aqueduct/patch/gate_status.py`, `aqueduct/patch/resolvability_gate.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, `aqueduct/executor/orchestrator.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/executor.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/{engine,executor,probe}.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/{ddl,surveyor}.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/{schema,models,parser}.py`, `aqueduct/cli/{run,run_setup,patch,__init__}.py`, `aqueduct/agent/gate_validation.py`; tests: `tests/test_patch/`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_patch_gates.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_sandbox_default.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_sandbox_mode.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_doctor_new.py`, `tests/test_parser/`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §6/§8.7, `docs/threat_model.md`, `docs/cli_reference.md`, `docs/extending.md`, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `docs/compatibility.md`)
- **BREAKING: the observability-store retention/pruning feature, `aqueduct report-prune`, and `aqueduct stores migrate` are removed, with no shim.** The `observability.retention:` `aqueduct.yml` block (`ObservabilityRetentionConfig`, `ObservabilityConfig`) and the throttled automatic end-of-run prune it drove (`aqueduct.surveyor.retention.maybe_prune_store`/`prune_store`/`vacuum_store`, called from `Surveyor.record()`) are gone outright — `run_records`, `failure_contexts`, `healing_outcomes`, `heal_attempts`, `patch_simulation`, `column_lineage`, and `probe_signals` rows now grow unbounded, and pruning/reclaiming disk space is the operator's own responsibility (e.g. manual `DELETE`s against the DuckDB/Postgres store). The `store_maintenance` marker table existed only to throttle the auto-prune to once/day/store; nothing else read it, so it is no longer created either — an existing table is left in place with no migration to drop it. The on-demand deep-clean CLI verb `aqueduct report-prune` (and its `--vacuum` flag) is deleted; `aqueduct report-costs` is unaffected. `aqueduct stores migrate` (copying depot KV rows from a DuckDB file into a configured Postgres/Redis backend) is also removed; `aqueduct stores info` is unaffected. The `sample_rows` Probe signal's per-probe write-time cap is UNCHANGED in behavior — it still keeps only the most recent rows per `probe_id` — but is no longer configurable: `ProbeSampling.sample_rows_keep_last_n` keeps its fixed default of 20 as the single source of truth, and the `aqueduct.yml`-driven override in `aqueduct/cli/run_setup.py` is gone. Setting `observability.retention` in `aqueduct.yml` now fails schema validation (`extra`/unknown-field rejection) instead of being silently accepted. (`aqueduct/surveyor/retention.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/ddl.py`, `aqueduct/cli/observability.py`, `aqueduct/cli/stores.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run_setup.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/handoff.py`, `aqueduct/executor/probe_sampling.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`; tests: `tests/test_surveyor/test_retention.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_run_retention_wiring.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_probe_retention.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_stores.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_env_resolution.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_funnel_migration.py`, `tests/test_config.py`; docs: `docs/cli_reference.md`, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `docs/specs.md`)
- **BREAKING: OpenLineage RunEvent emission and the top-level `lineage:` config block are removed, with no shim.** Aqueduct no longer emits OpenLineage `START`/`COMPLETE`/`FAIL` RunEvents (with the column-level `columnLineage` facet) to an OpenLineage-compatible backend such as Marquez, DataHub, or Atlan — `aqueduct/surveyor/openlineage.py` (`OpenLineageEmitter`, `build_run_event`, the column-lineage facet builder, the run-id UUIDv5 mapper) is deleted outright, along with every call site in `Surveyor` (`__init__`'s conditional emitter construction, and the `START`/`COMPLETE`/`FAIL` `emit()` calls). The top-level `lineage:` block (`LineageConfig`, fields `openlineage_url`/`openlineage_namespace`) is no longer a valid `aqueduct.yml` key — setting it now fails config validation naming the key, instead of being silently accepted. **COMPILE-TIME column lineage is entirely unaffected**: the `column_lineage` table, `aqueduct lineage`/`aqueduct lineage --chain`, and the patch-preview lineage gate (Gate 2) keep working exactly as before — they never depended on OpenLineage emission. (`aqueduct/surveyor/openlineage.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run_setup.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`; tests: `tests/test_surveyor/test_openlineage.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §7.4, `docs/extending.md`, `AGENTS.md`)
- **Dead surfaces left behind by the removals above are gone too.** `Surveyor.successful_patch_ids()` (its only caller was the deleted replay path), the heal-transcript `cache_status` argument and its `replayed (zero-token)` / `pending (zero-token)` / `coached (zero-token)` verdict labels (no code path can produce those states any more), and the `zero_token_heals` / `zero_token_coverage` fields of `aqueduct runs --heal-coverage --format json` (they could only ever report zero once the cache was removed, which is a misleading number rather than a useful one). `aqueduct runs --heal-coverage` still prints the `healing_outcomes.resolution` breakdown, and a store written by an older version still shows its historical `cached`/`replayed` counts there. (`aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `aqueduct/agent/transcript.py`, `aqueduct/cli/observability.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`; docs: `docs/cli_reference.md`, `docs/observability_guide.md`)

### Changed

- **BREAKING: a depot mount with no `path` now gets its own per-blueprint file, and `shared: true` requires an explicit `path`.** `stores.depots.<name>.path` is optional on the `duckdb` backend, and its implicit `default` mount no longer carries the hardcoded `.aqueduct/depot.db` value. A pathless DuckDB mount is routed per blueprint to `<routing root>/<blueprint_id>/depot.db` (the same routing base the observability store uses, and the same base `--store-dir` replaces), which is its OWN file next to that blueprint's `observability.db` and never a table inside it. Because the file is already per blueprint, such a mount skips the `_NamespacedDepot` key prefixing entirely: its keys are stored raw, where the old shared-file default stored them as `<blueprint_id>:<key>`. A mount that DOES name a `path` is unchanged in every respect, prefixing included. Two config-load errors are new: `shared: true` with no `path` (a per-blueprint file cannot be shared, so the operator must name the shared file), and a `postgres`/`redis` mount with no `path` (there the value is a DSN or URL, not a file that can be derived). NO back-compat shim: a project that relied on the old implicit `.aqueduct/depot.db` sees an empty depot on its next run unless it sets `path: ".aqueduct/depot.db"` on the mount, which restores the previous location and prefixing exactly. Preview reads (`aqueduct compile`, `aqueduct drift`, patch-preview Gate 3, `aqueduct lineage --chain`) resolve the new location through the same `build_depot_mounts`, so they keep matching a real run. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/stores/base.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`; tests: `tests/test_stores/test_depot_mount_routing.py`, `tests/test_parser/test_config.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_compile_cmd_depot.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §3.2/§10.4.4, `docs/observability_guide.md`)

- **BREAKING: `agent.progressive` and `agent.max_chain` are gone; chained multi-patch healing is now the only heal-loop behavior.** Setting either key in a Blueprint's `agent:` block or in `aqueduct.yml` now fails validation naming the key, rather than being accepted. The loop that used to be opt-in is now the loop: when a candidate patch validates in memory but the re-run still fails in a DIFFERENT module, the candidate is carried forward and the new failure is diagnosed against the already-patched manifest; when the SAME module fails again the candidate was wrong, so only that candidate is discarded (the links that already proved themselves are kept) and the same failure is retried. This is itself a fix: the old `run_progressive_chain` threw away every accumulated link when a chain got stuck and restarted from the original Blueprint. `agent.max_patches` is now the SINGLE total-attempt cap for the whole chain — every diagnosis call spends one unit of it — which means chaining requires raising `max_patches` above its default of 1 (and, as before, `max_patches > 1` needs `danger.allow_multi_patch`); the old separate `max_chain` default of 3 no longer exists. The two invariants are unchanged: nothing is written to the Blueprint until the full accumulated patch passes end to end, and exactly one combined `PatchSpec` is ever staged or applied per heal. Cascade tiers still never chain. One further consequence: a candidate that fails to apply or compile is now discarded and retried against the attempt cap like any other wrong candidate, where it previously ended the heal after a single attempt. The sandbox guard that used to key off `agent.progressive` now keys off the attempt cap: `agent.sandbox_mode: off` is refused only when `max_patches > 1` (a single-attempt heal has no mid-chain candidate to validate, so it stays legal there, gated by `danger.allow_skip_sandbox` as before). (`aqueduct/agent/progressive.py` deleted, `merge_patch_specs` moved to `aqueduct/agent/merge.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run_setup.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`/`models.py`/`parser.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_chained_healing.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_chained_healing_loop.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §8, `SKILL.md`)

- **`aqueduct run` now short-circuits before calling the Agent when the current blueprint already has a patch pending review.** Replaces the removed signature-keyed pending-reuse path: `patch_index` is checked for a `pending` row on the run's `blueprint_id` (`patch/index.py::list_by_status`) before every heal attempt; if one exists, the run stages nothing new, names the existing patch id and how to review it (`aqueduct patch pull <id>` / `aqueduct patch list`), and exits `HEAL_PENDING` (`aqueduct/exit_codes.py`) without an LLM call. No `heal_attempts` row is written for the short-circuit — the run's failure is already recorded by the ordinary per-iteration `Surveyor.record()` call that runs before this check. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_heal_cache.py`)
- **BREAKING: `aqueduct run --resume <run_id>` now fails closed on a manifest-hash mismatch.** Previously a `--resume` against a checkpointed run whose Manifest had since changed either silently proceeded with a suppressible warning (module checkpoints) or silently re-executed from scratch with no signal at all (cross-engine handoff spill). Both paths now refuse up front (`CONFIG_ERROR`), before any engine session is built, naming both the checkpoint's stored manifest hash and the current run's — unless the new `--force` flag is passed, which restores the prior (permissive) behavior exactly. `--force` is only valid together with `--resume`; using it alone is a Click `UsageError` (exit `USAGE_ERROR`, see below). (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spill.py::find_run_under_other_hash`; docs: `docs/cli_reference.md`, `docs/specs.md` §10.4.2/§10.4.3; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_run_resume_force.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_spill.py`)
- **BREAKING: `USAGE_ERROR` exit code changed from `5` to `64` (sysexits `EX_USAGE`), and Click's own usage errors (unknown command/flag, missing argument, bad `click.Choice` value) now exit `64` instead of Click's hardcoded `2`.** Previously a Click-level usage mistake (e.g. a typo'd flag) and a genuine runtime pipeline failure were indistinguishable to a wrapper — both exited `2`, colliding with `DATA_OR_RUNTIME`. `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py` now repoints `click.exceptions.UsageError.exit_code` to `exit_codes.USAGE_ERROR` at import time, unifying Click's usage errors with Aqueduct's own `sys.exit(exit_codes.USAGE_ERROR)` call sites under one code. `DATA_OR_RUNTIME` (`2`) is unchanged. `5` is retired and never reused. (`aqueduct/exit_codes.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/integrations/airflow/operator.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §10.7, `docs/cli_reference.md`, `aqueduct/integrations/airflow/README.md`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_exit_codes.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_run_resume_force.py`)
- **BREAKING: `@aq.depot.get(...)` and `@aq.run.prev_id()` now raise `CompileError` when NO depot backend is configured at all**, instead of silently returning the default (`""`) with only a `logging.warning`. A Blueprint referencing either token with no depot wired was turning an incremental pipeline into a silent full re-read every run, with no signal reaching the user. The error names the calling token and points at `stores.depots` in `aqueduct.yml` as the fix — the same message shape `@aq.depot.<name>.get` already used for a missing named mount. A depot that IS configured but simply lacks the key is unaffected: that is a data question, not a config bug, and still returns the default exactly as before. A Blueprint that never references `@aq.depot.*` / `@aq.run.prev_id()` is unaffected regardless of depot configuration. Any workflow that relied on the silent `""` fallback now sees a loud `CompileError` instead. (`aqueduct/compiler/runtime.py::AqFunctions.depot_get`/`run_prev_id`; tests: `tests/test_compiler/test_runtime.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §5.3)
- **BREAKING: `aqueduct drift` is now report-only — it no longer runs the agent or stages a patch.** The synthetic in-memory `FailureContext` it used to build for a breaking change and drive through the agent + apply-gate machinery (`aqueduct/drift/context.py`: `build_synthetic_failure_context`, `PREDICTED_DRIFT_ERROR_CLASS`) is deleted outright, along with the staging call site and `--patches-dir` flag in `aqueduct/cli/drift.py`. `drift` still reads each Ingress's live schema, diffs it against the self-owned baseline in `drift_checks`, and prints the same breaking/benign report as before; it now exits `DATA_OR_RUNTIME` on a breaking change and `SUCCESS` otherwise (benign drift, no drift, or a fresh baseline), and can no longer exit `HEAL_PENDING`. Healing remains entirely reactive: a real `aqueduct run` still self-heals when it actually fails, exactly as before — `drift` is only ever the early warning ahead of that run. (`aqueduct/drift/context.py` deleted, `aqueduct/cli/drift.py`; tests: `tests/test_drift/test_drift_unit.py`, `tests/test_drift/test_drift_command.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §8.8, `docs/cli_reference.md`, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `AGENTS.md`)

### Fixed

- **The heal budget's stuck-signature axes no longer merge two distinct failures that share a 240-character prefix.** `make_signature` normalized the failure message, truncated it to 240 characters, and hashed the truncated form. Every axis in `aqueduct/agent/budget.py` (`same_error_consecutive`, `same_signature_overall`, `progress_stalled_window`) keys on that hash, so two genuinely different long failures counted as one repeat and could stop healing with `stuck_signature` while the agent was still making progress. The hash now covers the full normalized message; `ErrorSignature.normalized_message` stays capped at 240 characters for display and for the `healing_outcomes`/`patch_index` signature columns. Volatile content (paths, digits, quoted and backticked identifiers, ANSI codes, whitespace runs) is still collapsed by normalization before hashing, so a rerun of the same failure with different line numbers still hashes identically. Signature hashes for messages longer than 240 normalized characters change value; nothing keys a lookup off them, so no migration is needed. (`aqueduct/agent/signature.py`; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_signature.py`)
- **`aqueduct lineage --chain` now builds a preview depot like the other three preview compile paths.** It was the fourth path that called `compile()` with no `depot=`/`depots=` argument, so every `@aq.depot.get(...)` / `@aq.run.prev_id()` in the Blueprint hit the loud `_depot_get_or_raise` `CompileError` and the command was unusable on exactly the Blueprints that use them — the same defect `aqueduct compile`, `aqueduct drift` and patch-preview Gate 3 already had fixed. It now loads the config (which it previously never did at all) and builds its depots through the same `preview_depots` helper, so a chain trace resolves the identical namespaced value a real `aqueduct run` would; a depot build failure is caught and falls back to `depot=None` so it can never crash a trace that would otherwise succeed. Parse errors and compile errors are now reported from separate `try` blocks, since the depot has to be built between them. (`aqueduct/cli/observability.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_lineage_chain.py`)
- **Read-only observability and depot reads no longer fail intermittently against a live run, and a locked store is no longer reported as an empty one.** A read-only DuckDB open takes no writer lock, but DuckDB still refuses it while a writer is mid-transaction — exactly the situation these reads exist for (inspecting a run while it runs) — and the read-only branch of `_DuckDBRelational.connect()` had no retry at all, so reads failed sporadically under concurrency. Read-only connects now go through `_connect_read_only_with_retry`: five attempts with 50/100/200/400 ms backoff, about 0.75 s worst case. It is deliberately short and bounded, unlike the writer's ~40 s `_connect_with_retry`, because a reader is interactive and the contention it hits is a brief commit rather than another long-lived writer. The two remaining bare read-only connects — `stores/queries.py::run_sql_readonly` and the run-id search loop in `stores/read.py::resolve_duckdb_obs_path` — go through it too; the latter also no longer swallows a failed open with `except Exception: continue`, which could silently route a read to the wrong store file (or to "no store at all") purely because a pipeline happened to be mid-commit. **No silent fallbacks:** a store that stays locked past the retry budget now raises the new `StoreLockedError`, a subclass of `StoreConnectionError` that distinguishes "another process holds this file right now" from "this store cannot be opened at all" (access denied, corrupt, missing driver). `_RelationalDepotMixin.kv_get`'s broad `except Exception` — which previously turned a lock error into a silently-returned default, so a preview could compile against fabricated depot values — now re-raises `StoreLockedError` while keeping the historical non-fatal behaviour for a store written by an older version or a driver-level fault. A genuinely missing key still returns the default, via the pre-existing file-existence guard and the `row[0] if row else default` path. (`aqueduct/stores/duckdb_.py`, `aqueduct/stores/base.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `aqueduct/stores/read.py`; tests: `tests/test_stores/test_depot_store.py`)
- **The DuckDB engine now populates `module_metrics.records_read`, and a Probe writes a `module_metrics` row at all.** `records_read` was NULL for every DuckDB module and a Probe wrote no row whatsoever, so `aqueduct report --profile` and `run_detail()` showed a permanent gap where the same Blueprint on Spark shows numbers. Spark reads `records_read` for free off a `DataFrame.observe()` that rides along on a scan already happening; a DuckDB relation is a lazy plan re-executed at each consumption point, so there is nothing to piggyback on. The engine now spends one explicit `COUNT(*)` over each module's input — the cheapest scan available — on Ingress, Channel, Junction, Funnel and Probe; a Channel or Funnel with several inputs sums across them, and a count that fails for any reason yields NULL rather than a misleading `0`. `records_read` is the module's INPUT row count, so a filtering Channel's `records_read` is deliberately larger than the row count it passes on, and a Junction reports the whole relation it fanned out rather than any one branch. Probe rows carry `records_read` (the relation the Probe observed) and `duration_ms`; `module_metrics` has no status column, so a Probe's outcome continues to live in its `ModuleResult` and its `probe_signals` rows. `bytes_read` is unchanged and stays NULL wherever DuckDB has no cheap source for it — a mid-pipeline relation has none at all, and a remote path has no cheap stat — which `docs/observability_guide.md` now states explicitly in a per-engine column-by-column table rather than leaving the reader to infer it. (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py`; tests: `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_module_metrics.py`; docs: `docs/observability_guide.md`)
- **A Junction branch port now feeds any downstream module, not just an Egress.** Both engines resolved a module's main input with an include-list (`edge.port == "main"`), so a Channel, Assert, Regulator, or Arcade wired to a Junction `<branch_id>` port failed the run with `[<id>] <Type> has no main-port incoming edges` — only Egress (and the synthetic Handoff, already fixed for exactly this reason) accepted a branch port. `docs/specs.md`'s port table has always said a `<branch_id>` port is consumed by "Any downstream module", so the spec was right and the code was wrong; no spec change was needed. Main input is now classified by what it EXCLUDES: any incoming data edge that is not a `signal` edge and not a `spillway` edge, which covers every Junction branch id without enumerating an open set of author-chosen port names. Resolving the edge was only half the bug — every call site then looked the value up in `frame_store` by `edge.from_id` alone, which is the `main`-port frame key, while a branch's frame lives at `<from_id>.<branch_id>`; all eight call sites (Channel, Junction, Assert, Regulator on both engines) now resolve through `_effective_frame_key`, which also keeps resolving through a synthetic Handoff. The port-classification helpers were triplicated across the two executors, so they are now defined once, engine-neutrally, in the new `aqueduct/executor/edge_ports.py` and imported by both; each executor keeps its private `_incoming_main`/`_incoming_data`/`_is_data_edge` names as thin aliases, and `aqueduct/compiler/islands.py`'s separate copy is untouched. On Spark the fix needed a third piece: `_run_sql` registers every upstream as a temp view named by its frame key, and Spark rejects a dotted name outright (`TEMP_VIEW_NAME_TOO_MANY_NAME_PARTS`), so a branch upstream is now left unregistered and reached through the existing `__input__` alias when it is the Channel's only input; a Channel with SEVERAL inputs where one arrives on a branch port has no name to write in its SQL and now raises a `ChannelError` naming the key and pointing at the single-input workaround, instead of surfacing a raw Spark `AnalysisException`. (`aqueduct/executor/edge_ports.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/executor.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/channel.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py`; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_executor_orchestration.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_executor.py`)
- **The preview compile paths now build the same default depot the run path builds, instead of compiling with no depot at all.** `aqueduct compile`, `aqueduct drift`, and the patch-preview sandbox gate (Gate 3) each called `compile()` without a `depot=`/`depots=` argument, so every `@aq.depot.get(...)` / `@aq.run.prev_id()` resolved against nothing — silently returning the default before the loud-fail change above, and hard-failing with `CompileError` after it, even with `stores.depots` fully configured. All three now construct their depot mounts through the same `build_depot_mounts` helper `aqueduct.stores.get_stores` uses (factored out of it, so there is exactly one implementation of the `_NamespacedDepot` `<blueprint_id>:` prefixing rules), which means a preview resolves the identical namespaced value a real `aqueduct run` would. Depot construction failure is caught and falls back to `depot=None`, so a depot problem can never crash a preview that would otherwise succeed — the loud `CompileError` remains the backstop for a genuinely depot-less compile. (`aqueduct/stores/base.py::build_depot_mounts`, `aqueduct/depot/depot.py::preview_depots`, `aqueduct/cli/compile_cmd.py`, `aqueduct/cli/drift.py`, `aqueduct/patch/preview.py::run_sandbox_gate`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_compile_cmd_depot.py`, `tests/test_drift/test_drift_command.py`, `tests/test_depot/test_depot.py`)
- **Depot reads on preview paths open DuckDB read-only, so a preview never contends with a live run's writer lock.** `_DuckDBRelational` gained a `read_only` flag (default `False` — existing construction is unchanged) that connects via `duckdb.connect(path, read_only=True)`, skips the `CREATE TABLE` DDL a read-only connection would reject, and never creates the database file or its parent directory. `kv_put`/`kv_delete` on a read-only store raise `StoreConnectionError` rather than silently no-opping. `kv_get`'s pre-existing missing-file guard still returns the default without ever calling `connect()`, which is what keeps a preview against an unwritten depot from crashing on DuckDB's read-only-open-of-a-missing-file error. (`aqueduct/stores/duckdb_.py`, `aqueduct/stores/base.py::_RelationalDepotMixin`; tests: `tests/test_stores/test_depot_store.py`)
- **Patch-preview Gate 3 now flags a depot value that moved since the failure it is healing.** A failing run may itself have written to the Depot (an Egress with `format: depot`) before it failed, so the patch was being validated against different inputs than the failure saw, with no signal. `AqFunctions` now records every resolved `@aq.depot.get`/`@aq.depot.<name>.get`/`@aq.run.prev_id` key during Tier 1 resolution (`depot_reads`), `compile()` gained an optional `depot_reads_out` sink to expose it without touching the `Manifest`, and the run path carries it through `_CompileResult.depot_reads` into `run_sandbox_gate`'s `depot_reads_at_failure` parameter. For every depot key present in both the failure's reads and the sandbox recompile's reads whose value differs, Gate 3 prints `depot key 'X' changed since failure: 'old' → 'new'` to stderr and folds the same line(s) into a `pass` result's `detail`. Purely informational — it never changes gate `status`, never blocks auto-apply, and needs no snapshot/versioned-depot store; a key present on only one side (the patch added or removed a depot reference) is not treated as staleness. (`aqueduct/compiler/runtime.py::AqFunctions`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py::compile`, `aqueduct/cli/run_setup.py::_CompileResult`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py::_run_patch_gates_inline`, `aqueduct/patch/preview.py::run_sandbox_gate`; tests: `tests/test_compiler/test_runtime.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview_gate3.py`; docs: `docs/specs.md` §8.2)

## [2.2.1] — 2026-08-25

### Added

- **`aqueduct doctor` DuckDB session/extension preflight (`check_duckdb`).** A `check_java`/`check_spark(preflight=True)` analog for the embedded DuckDB engine: whenever `deployment.engine == 'duckdb'`, doctor now connects the way `_make_session` would (`engine.duckdb.database_path` if set, else `:memory:`) and runs `SELECT 1`, reporting the DuckDB version. Runs unconditionally (a bare `duckdb.connect` is near-instant, unlike a SparkSession) — never gated behind `--skip-spark`. Under `--preflight`, it additionally exercises `_make_session`'s httpfs/S3-secret wiring (`resolve_s3_secret_from_config` + `configure_s3_secret` + `ensure_extension(con, "httpfs", ...)`) when S3 credentials or an explicit `extension_repository` are configured; when neither is configured the ok detail says so explicitly (`no s3 config — httpfs check skipped`). A non-`duckdb` deployment gets an honest `skip` naming the resolved engine instead of a probe attempt. `tooling.doctor.session_preflight` (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`) flips `unsupported` → `supported`. (`aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_doctor_new.py::TestCheckDuckDB`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_doctor_engine_routing.py::test_run_doctor_skips_duckdb_check_for_spark_engine`)
- **`aqueduct doctor` real DuckDB table-existence probe.** A `table:`-addressed Ingress/Egress resolved to the DuckDB engine now gets a real check instead of an unimplemented-for-this-engine skip: `_table_exists_check` opens a READ-ONLY connection to `engine.duckdb.database_path` and queries `information_schema.tables` (matches base tables AND views, unlike `duckdb_tables()`) for the module's 1/2/3-part `table:` name, using the same catalog-defaulting rule as the DuckDB executor's own `table:` resolution. When `database_path` is unset (the default `:memory:`), the check is an honest `skip` — that run's catalog only exists inside its own session, so a fresh doctor connection would have an empty catalog and falsely report every table missing. A configured `database_path` file that does not exist yet reports `fail` ("table not found"), never a crash. `tooling.doctor.table_exists` (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`) flips `unsupported` → `supported`. (`aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_doctor_engine_routing.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_tooling_leaves.py::test_duckdb_table_exists_is_supported_with_a_hint`)

### Changed

- `ProbeSampling` hoisted to an engine-agnostic `aqueduct/executor/probe_sampling.py`; `aqueduct/cli/run_setup.py` no longer imports a Spark module to build every engine's sampling config. Per-engine copies (`aqueduct/executor/spark/probe.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/probe.py`) are now re-exports — existing import paths are unchanged.

## [2.2.0] — 2026-08-24

The 2.2.0 milestone (Phases 87 + 90): heal-as-PR becomes a first-class,
git-native flow; the trust boundary around the healing loop is closed
(deny-by-default auto guardrails, untrusted-data prompt framing, a written
threat model); and the project front door is rewritten to the AI-harness
positioning with a truth-pass across README, gallery, and docs.

### Changed

- **BREAKING: `agent.approval: auto` now denies file-touching patch ops when `agent.guardrails.allowed_paths` is unset**, instead of allowing any path. `auto` is the only approval mode where a patch applies with zero human review, so an unconfigured allowlist previously meant a patch could write anywhere without anyone checking. Gate 1 (`aqueduct/patch/apply.py::_check_guardrails`) now refuses `set_module_config_key`/`replace_module_config`/`insert_module`/`add_probe`/`add_arcade_ref` ops that write a `path`/`output_path` value when `allowed_paths` is empty, naming the offending value and pointing at `agent.guardrails.allowed_paths` (or a switch to `human`/`ci`, where a human reviews the patch before it applies either way). `human`, `ci`, and `disabled` are unaffected. See `docs/specs.md` §8.3/§8.7 and `docs/threat_model.md`. (`aqueduct/patch/apply.py`; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_guardrails_rollback.py::TestAutoModeDenyByDefault`)
- **Untrusted-data framing in the composed healing prompt.** The runtime data a heal attempt embeds (the failure's error message, and its structured root-cause block or raw stack trace) is now wrapped in `<<<UNTRUSTED_DATA>>>` / `<<</UNTRUSTED_DATA>>>` sentinel markers, and the system prompt scaffold gains an "Untrusted data" instruction block, placed before the failure-report description, telling the model that delimited content (and any tool_result from an agentic-mode tool call) is data, never instructions, and that instruction-like text inside it is a prompt-injection attempt to be ignored. `PROMPT_VERSION` bumps `1.12` → `1.13`. (`aqueduct/agent/prompts.py`, `aqueduct/agent/loop.py`; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_untrusted_data_framing.py`)

### Added

- **`aqueduct patch pr <patch_ref>` (Phase 87, heal-as-PR).** Applies a staged patch on a fresh `aqueduct/heal/<patch_id>` branch, commits through the identical path `patch import` uses (same gates, same `---aqueduct---` trailer), pushes, and opens a PR via the `gh` CLI. Mode-agnostic: works on any pending patch regardless of the Blueprint's `approval` mode, so a `human`-mode reviewer can use it in place of a local `patch apply`, and a `ci`-mode runner can use it instead of (or alongside) the webhook + `docs/templates/ci-heal-workflow.yml` flow. `--dry-run` runs every check and prints the plan without touching anything. A repo-root pre-flight guard hard-refuses when the resolved git work-tree root does not match the project root, naming both paths; a new `git.expected_root` config key pins the intended root explicitly for a monorepo layout. New `aqueduct.yml` blocks: `git:` (`expected_root`, `remote`) and `pr:` (`base_branch`, `title_template`, `draft`); no Blueprint influence over either, and no labels/reviewers field, by design: reviewer routing stays with repo-level CODEOWNERS/branch protection. A new `aqueduct doctor` check reports `gh` presence and auth, soft/skippable when the PR flow is unused. GitHub-only in 2.2.0 (`gh` CLI shellout; the transport is a single internal seam, no provider abstraction shipped). PR body renders purely from existing PatchSpec fields; no new prompt section, no `PROMPT_VERSION` bump. (`aqueduct/cli/patch.py`, `aqueduct/patch/ci.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_patch_pr.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_ci.py`, `tests/test_config.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_doctor_new.py`, `tests/test_meta_patch_grammar.py`)
- **`docs/threat_model.md`**: the healing loop's trust boundaries, assets, the FailureContext → prompt → PatchSpec injection surface and its mitigations (the gate ladder, Gate 1's allowlist including the new auto-mode deny-by-default, the sandbox, the untrusted-data prompt framing, redaction chokepoints), and threats explicitly out of scope.

### Removed

- **BREAKING: the `aqueduct dashboard` command and the `dashboard` extra.** The Streamlit/Plotly observability viewer (`aqueduct/dashboard/`) never ran in CI, so its smoke test never once executed against real code before this release. `pip install aqueduct-core[dashboard]` no longer resolves. `stores/queries.py`, the query layer it shared with `aqueduct report`/`runs` and the MCP tool registry, is unaffected. It returns if dogfooded usage justifies it and a CI lane lands in the same release that adds it back.

## [2.1.3] — 2026-08-24

Phase 89 — the perf-polish workstream: session keep-alive across polyglot
islands and spill-storage bounding.

### Added

- **Polyglot session keep-alive (`execution:` block).** `run_polyglot()` no longer builds and tears down a fresh engine session for every island: when the next island in execution order runs on the same engine, the live session is handed over, so a `spark → duckdb → spark` Blueprint now builds ONE Spark session instead of two (a cold local Spark build measures ~3.9 s; the tear-down/rebuild it replaces, ~0.5 s each — numbers from the Phase 89 measurement pass). Reuse is observationally identical to fresh sessions by default: a new optional `ExecutorProtocol.cleanup_reused_session` hook drops the `register_as_table` catalog objects the finishing island registered (tracked from the compiled sub-Manifest, never a catalog-wide sweep) before the next island sees the session. Two new top-level `execution:` config fields govern it: `session_keep_alive` (default `true`; `false` restores the old close-every-island behavior exactly) and `share_island_state` (default `false`; `true` skips the boundary cleanup so a later same-engine island deliberately sees the previous island's registered tables/views). Cross-ENGINE boundaries are untouched — storage-mediated handoff, close-then-build ordering, and the heal-retry rebuild invariant all hold; every session is still closed by run end on success, failure, or exception. One quiet `-v` narrative line reports each reuse boundary. (`aqueduct/executor/orchestrator.py`, `aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, both engines' `engine.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_orchestrator.py`)
- **Same-run eager spill pruning (`handoff.prune_eagerly`, default `true`).** A handoff edge's spill directory is deleted the moment the island that reads it finishes successfully, instead of waiting for end-of-run cleanup — bounding peak spill storage on long chains. A spill feeding a pending or failed island, or one this run resumed from a prior run, is never touched, so `--resume` after a mid-run failure finds exactly the spills it needs; `keep_on_failure`, the orphan sweep, and supersession-release are unchanged. `false` defers every deletion to the run's own end (which remains the backstop either way). One `-vv` narrative line per pruned edge. (`aqueduct/executor/orchestrator.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spill.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`)
- **`aqueduct run --sandbox` is engine-agnostic.** The dev dry-run no longer refuses `engine=duckdb`: the guard was historical (the block hardcoded `make_spark_session()` while the Gate 3 sandbox machinery it reuses had long been engine-agnostic). The sandbox session now builds through the target engine's own `ExecutorProtocol` with the same config layering a real run resolves, so a DuckDB blueprint dry-runs end-to-end with Egress skipped exactly like Spark. A new `tooling.sandbox_dry_run` capability leaf (declared `supported` by both engines, consulted before the dry-run proceeds) forces every future engine to take an explicit position instead of failing mid-run. The polyglot refusal is unchanged. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`, both `capabilities.yml`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_sandbox_engine_agnostic.py`)
- **`aqueduct handoff sweep --older-than <duration>`** (`7d` / `24h` / `90m`) — the explicit operator/watchdog action `docs/specs.md` §10.4.3 recorded as missing: additionally reclaims a kept-failure spill whose run finished longer ago than the given age even though no later success has superseded it. Fires only inside the existing kept-failure branch after the supersession check; without the flag the sweep is byte-identical to before. Reclaimed-by-age candidates carry a distinct reason in the text table and `reclaimed_by_age: true` in `--format json`. Never automatic, never config-driven. (`aqueduct/cli/handoff.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spill.py`)

### Changed

- **`docs/roadmap.md` removed.** Deferred-feature tracking moved to the issue tracker; every pointer to the page (docs, `checkpoint_root` error messages, the config template, the DuckDB capabilities comment) now states its fact directly. The docs set also received a house-style pass (em-dash removal, register cleanup) and a scrub of internal planning references from user-facing pages.

## [2.1.2] — 2026-08-23

Phase 88 — the domains workstream: dependency healing (Domain 3) and the
typed defer slice (Domain 6).

### Added

- **A top-level `dependencies:` Blueprint block** — a flat list of PEP 508 requirement strings (`"holidays>=0.40"`), sibling of `udf_registry:`. Its entire runtime semantics are a **compile-time preflight**: every declared requirement is checked against the installed environment via `importlib.metadata`, and anything missing or version-conflicting raises the new typed `DependencyError` (a `CompileError` subclass) naming each failing requirement and the copy-pasteable `pip install` command — instead of the author meeting a bare `ImportError` halfway through a run. Satisfied requirements are silent. The block is **not** engine-scoped, carries no capability leaf (top-level Blueprint fields are outside the leaf walker's `_SCHEMA_BLOCKS`, exactly like `udf_registry`), and has no allowlist and no `aqueduct.yml` surface: **Aqueduct never installs anything**, so the installed environment is the only boundary there is. A malformed requirement string fails at PARSE time with the bad string named. `aqueduct/dependencies.py` hand-rolls the PEP 508-lite parser and reuses the existing PEP 440-lite comparator from `executor/capabilities.py` (`packaging` is not a declared dependency); a package installed at a version string that comparator cannot read passes rather than failing, because the preflight exists to catch definitely-unsatisfied requirements, never to reject an install it merely does not understand. (`aqueduct/dependencies.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`; tests: `tests/test_parser/test_dependencies.py`)
- **`declare_dependency` — a new PatchSpec op, and a fifth gate to judge it.** The healing agent can now propose the package a pipeline is actually missing. Applying the op appends one PEP 508 requirement to the Blueprint's `dependencies:` list (append-stable, deduped, creating the key when absent) and writes **nothing else** — never `requirements.txt`, never `pyproject.toml`, never the environment, and it never shells out to pip. The op needed no prompt-surface code: it joins the `PatchOperation` union, so `PatchSpec.model_json_schema()` discloses it automatically. The new **resolvability gate (Gate 5, `aqueduct/patch/resolvability_gate.py`)** decides what may happen to such a patch, reusing the existing `GateStatus` vocabulary with no new member: `not_applicable` (no such op), `pass` (already installed — auto-apply eligible), `warn` (**resolves on PyPI but is not installed — deferred to a human**, with the `pip install` command and the `aqueduct patch apply <id>` follow-up spelled out), `fail` (no such package, or no version satisfying the specifier — feeds the reprompt loop so the model finds another fix or defers honestly), and `unavailable` (the PyPI check itself could not run). Only `pass`/`not_applicable` permit auto-apply, decided by one predicate mirroring the sandbox gate's, fail-closed on `None`. Across several ops the worst verdict wins. `_run_patch_gates_inline` now returns a five-tuple, writes a `resolvability` row to `patch_simulation`, and the run's gate ladder gains its own numbered entry; `patch preview` renders the gate in text and JSON and exits non-zero on `fail` only — a `warn` is a deferral, not a rejection. (`aqueduct/patch/`, `aqueduct/cli/`; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_resolvability_gate.py`)
- **`webhooks.on_defer` — a dedicated event for deferrals**, so they stop overloading `on_patch_pending`. Its payload carries the new `defer_reason` bucket, the diagnosis and suggestions, and `confidence_reason` — which the agent collected from the model and then silently dropped before it ever reached a webhook body. When `on_defer` is unset, a defer falls back to firing `on_patch_pending` exactly as before, so nobody loses notifications on upgrade. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/agent/loop.py`; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_defer_webhook.py`)
- **`heal_attempts.defer_reason`** — a queryable column for why a heal deferred, added to both the `CREATE TABLE` and the idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` migration tuple (DuckDB and Postgres share that path). It is attached by `update_heal_attempt_stop_reason` rather than the per-turn `INSERT`, because a defer's reason is only known once the loop returns its final PatchSpec. (`aqueduct/surveyor/`, `docs/observability_guide.md`)

### Changed

- **`defer_to_human` now requires a `defer_reason` enum** — `infrastructure` | `upstream_schema_change` | `data_shape_change` | `insufficient_context` | `other` — **beside** the existing free-prose `diagnosis`/`suggestions`/`confidence_reason` fields, which are unchanged. The enum is the queryable bucket the prose could never be; an invalid or absent value is a pydantic validation error, which the loop already feeds into its normal reprompt path rather than hard-failing.

### Fixed

- **A defer-only patch in `approval: auto` no longer runs the whole gate pyramid as an expensive no-op.** A `defer_to_human` patch makes zero Blueprint changes, yet the `auto` branch ran sandbox replay, every gate, and the apply path on it anyway. It now short-circuits straight to the pending/defer staging path before the first gate, and says so in the run output. A mixed patch would still run the full ladder — the grammar's `_reject_mixed_defer_ops` already refuses to construct one. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_defer_auto_shortcircuit.py`)

## [2.1.1] — 2026-08-23

Phase 85 — the CLI workstream. A rendering foundation, then a narrative
redesign on top of it. No breaking changes.

### Added

- **A single CLI render funnel — `aqueduct/cli/render/`.** `cli/output.py` and `cli/style.py` were two competing output layers with 384 raw `click.echo` call sites bypassing both; they are now thin back-compat re-export shims over one package. `render/width.py` supplies TTY detection (`AQ_FORCE_TTY` override), `COLUMNS`-aware `terminal_width`, and an ANSI-stripping, East-Asian-aware `display_width` under which every glyph in the Aqueduct vocabulary (`✓ ✗ ⚠ ⓘ ◆ · │ ├ └ ┆ ▶ ↳ ⊘ ⇄`) measures exactly one column. `render/funnel.py` adds `echo`/`info`/`success`/`warn_line`/`error`/`result` plus a `gutter()` context manager so nested block output inherits its tree prefix. (tests: `tests/test_cli/test_render_wrap.py`)
- **One shared wrap/truncation primitive (`render/wrap.py::wrap_line`)**, replacing four ad-hoc char-count caps that had no knowledge of terminal width (`concise_error` 300, `_short_warning` 100, `TranscriptWriter._MAX_RAW_LINES` 40, `reprompt_reason[:300]`). Piped/CI output is never wrapped and never truncated — one logical record per line, full text, grep-safe. On a TTY it wraps to the measured display width with the gutter repeated on every produced line and continuations hang-indented so they never escape the tree; `-v` lifts truncation but not wrapping; a head cap emits `(N more lines)`; a `hint` renders the `ⓘ …: -v` escape hatch.
- **A width-aware table layer (`render/tables.py`), built on `rich`** — now a base dependency (it is neither a vendor leaf nor an engine leaf, so it takes no extra under the two-axis packaging policy). Fixed columns are sized to their content and exactly one flex column absorbs the remaining terminal width, ellipsising when narrow. Column widths are computed by Aqueduct and handed to `rich` with an exact `Console(width=…)`, because `rich`'s own auto-fit silently re-shrinks columns and would break the fixed-column contract. **When the stream is not a TTY, `rich` is not imported at all** — the non-TTY branch emits plain content-sized aligned text with an ASCII rule, full untruncated cells and zero ANSI. Migrated: `patch list` (all three hand-rolled tables), blueprint history, the benchmark diff table, and `report --profile` / `--trend` / runs. (tests: `tests/test_cli/test_render_tables.py`)
- **`--debug`** — a root flag carrying the Python `logging` DEBUG level, split out from `-v`, which previously conflated three unrelated meanings.
- **Golden-output tests** capturing the rendered clean-run, failure-plus-heal-unreachable, `doctor`, and `patch list` screens at a pinned width, so the look can be reviewed in the test source and cannot regress silently. (tests: `tests/test_cli/test_golden_output.py`)
- **`observability.retention:` — per-table age windows for the observability store**, with automatic pruning at the end of every run, throttled to once per day per store via a marker row so the common case costs one indexed `SELECT`. Defaults: runs/failures/lineage/simulations/probe-signals 90 days, healing outcomes and heal attempts 180 days, plus a `sample_rows_keep_last_n` count cap. Reclaiming disk is **never** automatic — `VACUUM` lives behind the new `aqueduct report-prune --vacuum` verb. Before this, every table except `explain_snapshot` grew strictly forever. (`aqueduct/surveyor/retention.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.1)
- **`aqueduct report-prune` and `aqueduct report-costs`.** `report-costs` aggregates LLM spend per blueprint per month — the `tokens_in`/`tokens_out` were already captured per heal attempt but no query existed, so the data was stored and unqueryable. `aqueduct runs --cascade` does the same for `healing_outcomes.model_cascade_position`, written in several places and never once selected. (Named with a hyphen, matching the existing `benchmark-diff`/`benchmark-stats` precedent, because `report` takes an optional `RUN_ID` positional and cannot also dispatch subcommands.)
- **The DuckDB executor now writes per-module `module_metrics`** (duration and row counts) for every real module type, at parity with Spark, which previously wrote them only for the synthetic Handoff module. `report --profile` and `--trend` returned nothing for a DuckDB blueprint, and "did this pipeline get slower over six weeks" was unanswerable for that engine. `records_written` is a real `0` for an empty result, so a zero-row run is now distinguishable from "no metric recorded". Row counts come off `COPY`/`CREATE TABLE AS`/`INSERT INTO`'s own cursor result — no extra scan. (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/`)
- **A `tooling.*` capability-leaf family**, declared by both engines: `tooling.test_runner`, `tooling.drift_schema_read`, `tooling.doctor.{table_exists,cloud_preflight,session_preflight}` and `observability.module_metrics.per_module`. Host/tooling capabilities were previously undeclared, so an engine could silently lack one; closure tests now force every future engine to declare all six. The family joins `all_leaves()` but is deliberately excluded from `execution_leaves()` — no Blueprint field spells one of these ids, so a Blueprint must never fail to compile because the host engine has no doctor probe. (`aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`, both `capabilities.yml`, `docs/compatibility.md`)
- **`-s/--set` on `patch preview`, `patch revert`, and `doctor`.** All three previously had no way to preview, revert, or diagnose under the same resolved config a `--set`-pinned `aqueduct run` would use. `patch revert`'s prior-values equality check — the safety property that stops a revert from clobbering an unrelated later change — deliberately stays fed by the UNPINNED resolution regardless of `--set`; the identical exemption now applies to `doctor`'s `healed-config` drift check, which enforces the same equality as a warning rather than a hard refusal. (`aqueduct/cli/patch.py`, `aqueduct/cli/diagnostics.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_set_override_wave5.py`)
- **`aqueduct handoff sweep`.** Every registered engine already sweeps orphaned cross-engine handoff spill directories automatically at the start of each polyglot run, but a Blueprint that failed and was never rerun kept its spill forever — nothing ever ran again to reclaim it. This is the explicit, on-demand counterpart, built on the same `run_records`-driven orphan-vs-live rule (`aqueduct/executor/spill.py::plan_orphan_sweep`/`sweep_orphan_spills`, now dry-run-capable). Defaults to `--dry-run` — this deletes materialized data with no undo — and discovers every observability store under `--store-dir`, since a shared `handoff.root` can carry spill from more than one Blueprint. (`aqueduct/cli/handoff.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spill.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_handoff_sweep.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_spill.py`)

### Changed

- **BREAKING: `-v` is now a repeatable output-detail tier, not a logging switch.** `-v` previously meant three unrelated things at once — the Python logging DEBUG threshold, Spark's `quiet_startup` toggle, and the heal transcript's terse/verbose switch — and `run`/`doctor` each carried their own independent `-v` that did not share `ctx.obj` with the root group. There is now ONE verbosity concept resolved through `aqueduct/cli/verbosity.py::resolve_verbosity()`: `0` clean narrative, `-v` the full Aqueduct-side story (untruncated errors and warnings, uncollapsed `doctor` rows, uncapped probe notes, transcript detail), `-vv` the raw layer (engine/Spark startup and log4j output, prompt text, streamed model text). `run` and `doctor` keep a local `-v` count purely so the postfix form (`aqueduct run -v bp.yml`) still works; the effective level is the max of both. **Scripts relying on `-v` to enable DEBUG logging must switch to `--debug`.** Spark `quiet_startup` now ties to `-vv` at all four call sites. (`aqueduct/cli/verbosity.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `docs/cli_reference.md`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_verbosity.py`)
- **The self-healing transcript no longer splits across both output streams.** Its `⚠` header and `◆ cascade` ceremony line were written to stderr while the tier ladder went to stdout, so `2>/dev/null` and `> run.log` each captured half a story. The settled rule — narrative to stderr, final results to stdout — is now explicit at every emit site in `run.py`, `cli/__init__.py`, `diagnostics.py` and `heal.py`: the whole heal block (including the streaming token writer, whose TTY check moves from stdout to stderr) is stderr, and the framed run screen — dividers, `▶` header, module tree, verdict — consolidates on stdout so it survives redirection intact. The failure footer moves to stdout to match the success footer.
- **`patch list` no longer truncates the rationale column at 60 characters in piped output.** Truncation is now a TTY-only concern, so scripts and CI logs receive the full text. `report --trend` promotes its slowdown flag from loose trailing text to a real `Note` column.
- **The run narrative was rebuilt.** The module tree gains right-aligned dim metadata and shows an Egress row's destination; the per-module engine tag is gone, replaced by a dedicated `⇄ handoff` line at a polyglot boundary — engine identity matters where data crosses engines, not on every row. A failed module now shows a short classified label with the detail wrapped beneath it and candidates on their own line, instead of one 300-character string. The heal block became a single visual object: a flat `│` gutter, `├─` only on a cascade escalation, `└` at close. Validation gates print as they run rather than only on failure, and the closing summary carries total elapsed time.
- **`⊘` now marks a heal tier that was never reached** (unreachable host, missing credentials), distinct from `✗`, which means a model ran and its patch was rejected. These previously rendered identically, so "the agent never started" and "the agent tried and failed" were indistinguishable at a glance.
- **`heal_attempts.tool_calls_json` stores only `{name, duration_ms}`.** Argument summaries and result previews — real, if truncated, payload content — were being written to the database on every agentic heal attempt. `signature_hash` and `escalated` are no longer populated; the columns remain in the DDL, so no migration is required.

### Fixed

- **The sandbox gate failed OPEN.** `sandbox_gate_permits_auto_apply(None)` returned `True`, so any caller that skipped Gate 3 — or any future path passing `None` by accident — could auto-apply a patch nothing had ever replayed. `None` now blocks, and a new `GateStatus.NOT_REQUESTED` distinguishes "never asked" from `NOT_APPLICABLE` ("asked, nothing to check") and `UNAVAILABLE` ("asked, could not run"). `patch preview`'s exit code deliberately uses a separate predicate, so omitting `--sandbox` — its documented default invocation — still exits 0. (`aqueduct/patch/gate_status.py`)
- **`warnings.suppress` silently failed for runtime warnings.** `_add_module_warning` appended straight to a list without consulting the suppress list, and the `runtime_retry_*` family bypassed both warning paths via bare `logger.warning`. All three now route through the shared suppression predicate, so a `rule_id` in `warnings.suppress` is honoured uniformly. (`aqueduct/executor/models.py`, both executors)
- **`column_lineage` grew without bound and was read unscoped.** Rows were appended on every compile whether or not any SQL changed, and `lineage()` read them back with no `DISTINCT` and no latest-run filter, mixing rows from many historical runs up to an arbitrary 500-row cap. Writes are now gated on the channel fingerprint actually changing, and the read is scoped to the latest run.
- **Probe `sample_rows` payloads are redacted.** The signal collected real data rows and inserted them into `probe_signals` every run, forever, with no redaction — while the failure path beside it already redacted. It now uses the same `redact()` and gains a keep-last-N cap.
- **Runtime warnings and notes are persisted.** `ModuleResult.warnings`/`.notes` were computed, displayed, and then dropped before reaching `run_records`.
- `run_records` gains a top-level `engine` column (migrated in place, idempotently, on both backends) so engine comparison is a `WHERE` clause rather than JSON-parsing an unindexed blob per row; `module_metrics` gains the `run_id` index its actual read pattern uses; and a `queries.py` fallback that selected a `failure_contexts.category` column which never existed now names the real one.
- A latent `UnboundLocalError` in `aqueduct benchmark`, where a redundant local import shadowed a module-level name for every call site above it.

## [2.1.0] — 2026-08-17

Final release. Four changes below are marked **BREAKING**; together with the
ten in 2.1.0rc1 this makes FOURTEEN breaking changes since 2.0.5. They ship
inside a MINOR bump per the stability contract stated at the top of this
file: the contract applies from v1.0.0, and during alpha/RC breaking changes
may land in any release provided they are marked.

### Added

- Four new gallery snippets: `45_schema_drift_check` (`aqueduct drift`), `46_agent_guardrails` (`agent.guardrails`), `47_agent_cascade` (`agent.cascade`), and `48_agent_progressive` (`agent.progressive`). Each of `46`–`48` ships `blueprint.yml` in its already-healed state (the `27_self_healing` convention) with a sibling `blueprint_bugged.yml` carrying the reader-facing deliberate bug, so CI's LLM-free snippet lane (`aqueduct run blueprint.yml`) exits 0; `45`'s `inspect_results.py` now tolerates a missing `drift_checks` table (only created by `aqueduct drift`, not a plain `run`) instead of crashing.
- **`agent.guardrails.deny_patterns` — a subtract-only denylist for config path values, evaluated after `allowed_paths`.** `allowed_paths` alone forces an operator to enumerate every permitted prefix before they can forbid one dangerous one; `deny_patterns` is a list of `fnmatch` patterns, checked over the same resolved path value (`${ctx.*}` values still resolved via `ProvenanceMap` first) after `allowed_paths` runs. A path `allowed_paths` permitted — or that was unrestricted because `allowed_paths` was empty — is still refused if it matches a `deny_patterns` entry; `deny_patterns` can never widen what `allowed_paths` allows, only narrow it further, consistent with AGENTS.md's "a permission surface below core may only SUBTRACT, never add." Gates `set_module_config_key`, `replace_module_config`, `insert_module`, `add_probe`, and `add_arcade_ref` — the same op shapes `allowed_paths` already gates. Additive and default-empty (`deny_patterns: []`); an existing Blueprint's behavior is unchanged unless it opts in. See `aqueduct/patch/apply.py::_check_guardrails`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py::GuardrailsConfig`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py::GuardrailsSchema`.
- **DuckDB Egress gains a real JSON writer** (`format: json`, previously unimplemented — `SUPPORTED_FORMATS` only had `parquet`/`csv`). Writes via `COPY ... TO '<path>' (FORMAT JSON, ...)`; `options: {array: true}` switches from the default newline-delimited JSON to a single JSON array, `options: {compression: ...}` passes through, mirroring DuckDB's own JSON `COPY` option vocabulary. The `mode: append` reader-selection branch (previously a two-way `parquet`-or-`csv` guess that would have silently read a JSON file back as CSV) is now a three-way dispatch across all three writable formats. (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/egress.py`; tests: `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_executor.py::test_write_egress_json_overwrite`, `::test_write_egress_json_append_to_existing_target_unions_rows`)
- **Five new `aqscenarios` benchmark scenarios** (15–19) covering shipped prompt rules with no prior coverage: an Ingress `schema_hint` type mismatch, a JOIN-condition field-not-found (suggested-columns extraction from a non-SELECT SQL position), a reserved-identifier backtick-quoting fix, a two-independent-bug blueprint that grades a surgical single-patch heal against unreported collateral edits, and the designated oneshot-vs-agentic-mode A/B comparison scenario.
- `benchmark_results` now persists `refusal` and `engine_config_gate` per row (both were already computed and exported in `aqueduct benchmark --format json`, but never reached the store, so `aqueduct benchmark-diff`/`benchmark-stats` history could not distinguish a config-heal refusal from any other failure). An existing benchmark DB is migrated in place — a new idempotent `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` pair runs on open, in both the DuckDB and Postgres backends — so a pre-2.1 store is not orphaned. (`aqueduct/surveyor/benchmark_store.py`, `docs/observability_guide.md`; tests: `tests/test_benchmark_store.py`)
- `format_benchmark_table` (the `aqueduct benchmark` terminal table) now shows `stop_reason` in each result cell when set, matching the field already persisted to `benchmark_results` and exported to `--format json` — a benchmark run's terminal output could previously not distinguish "the model gave up" from "ran out of attempts" without re-reading the JSON. (`aqueduct/surveyor/scenario.py`; tests: `tests/test_surveyor/test_benchmark_table.py`)

### Changed

- **BREAKING: `format: json`, `format: parquet` (Ingress and Egress), and `format: iceberg` (Ingress) are now gated capability leaves (F-9/F-12).** Previously these format strings were neither in `INGRESS_FORMATS` nor `EGRESS_FORMATS` (`aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`), so `compiler/capability_check.py` never emitted a leaf for them and no engine was ever asked whether it supports them — a Blueprint using an unsupported format compiled clean and only failed at runtime (DuckDB has no JSON writer and no Iceberg reader). Now: a DuckDB Blueprint using Egress `format: json` compiles (DuckDB gained a real JSON writer this release — see Added below); a DuckDB Blueprint using Ingress `format: iceberg` is REJECTED at compile time instead of failing at runtime with an unreadable-source error. Nothing that previously worked end to end regresses — every combination newly gated as `unsupported` had no working code path before this change either. (`aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`, both engines' `capabilities.yml`, `docs/compatibility.md`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/`)
- **BREAKING: the Probe signal `partition_stats` is renamed to `execution_partitions`, with no back-compat alias.** The signal reports `df.rdd.getNumPartitions()` — Spark's EXECUTION partition count, not Hive/table partitioning — and the old name misled readers into thinking it described table partitioning. A Blueprint using `type: partition_stats` now fails with a hard capability-check error at compile time (the same `extra="forbid"` grammar rejection any other unrecognized signal type gets); there is no reader that accepts both spellings. Update `config.signals[].type: partition_stats` to `type: execution_partitions` in any Blueprint that declares it. (`aqueduct/executor/probe_plugins.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/probe.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/probe.py`, both engines' `capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/dashboard/app.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `docs/specs.md`, `docs/spark_guide.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `SKILL.md`, `gallery/snippets/26_probe_signals_all/`; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_executor_probe.py`, `tests/test_surveyor/test_probe.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_gate_module_and_feature.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_probe.py`, `tests/test_compiler/test_compiler_warnings.py`)

### Removed

- **BREAKING: the Databricks remote-submit deploy layer is deleted.** `deployment.target: databricks`, the `deployment.databricks` config block, and the `[databricks]` extra are gone — they were never verified against a live workspace, their unit tests mocked `httpx` so vendor API drift was invisible, and the target-user overlap is about zero, since Databricks users run Workflows. There is no built-in replacement: wrap `aqueduct run` in a Databricks Workflows `spark_python_task` yourself.

- **BREAKING**: the `llm` extra is gone — `pip install aqueduct-core[llm]` now
  fails with an unknown-extra error. `json-repair` ships in the base install
  instead, so LLM patch-JSON recovery is always available.

### Fixed

- `docs/roadmap.md` and `docs/compatibility.md` no longer claim Iceberg and Hudi table formats are "planned, not started" — both shipped, are declared `supported` on Spark, and run against real Maven-provisioned Iceberg/Hudi bundles in CI.
- Removed the false claim that Aqueduct supports JDBC egress (`docs/roadmap.md`, `docs/production_guide.md`). JDBC is ingress-only, a Spark `DataFrameReader.format("jdbc")` passthrough; no `egress.format.jdbc` leaf exists on either engine and no JDBC writer exists.
- `config_contains`'s SQL comparison silently degraded from AST-normalized matching to lowercase-substring matching whenever `sqlglot` could not parse a scenario's SQL fragment — the fallback itself is unchanged (hard-failing would break scenarios that legitimately write partial SQL), but the degradation is no longer silent: it's now reported through `aqueduct.warnings.emit` (`config_contains_sql_degraded` rule_id), naming the config key and the unparseable fragment. (`aqueduct/surveyor/scenario.py::_normalize_sql`; tests: `tests/test_surveyor/test_scenario.py::TestNormalizeSql`)
- The `aqscenarios` benchmark grader's `has_guardrails` detection only tested the original four `agent.guardrails` fields, so a Blueprint declaring ONLY `deny_patterns` was scored as having no guardrails at all (`violated_guardrails=None` instead of `[]`). The field list is now derived from `GuardrailsConfig`'s dataclass fields instead of an inline literal, so a future guardrail field can't reintroduce this gap. (`aqueduct/surveyor/scenario.py::_try_apply_patch`; tests: `tests/test_surveyor/test_scenario.py::TestTryApplyPatch::test_only_deny_patterns_is_recognized_as_guardrails`)

- Non-ASCII text (accented names, CJK labels, …) is no longer mangled into `\uXXXX` escapes at the 13 reader-facing `json.dumps()` call sites an LLM or a human actually reads (healing prompts, tool results, `patch list`/`run --show`/`doctor` output, the dashboard, archived patch bodies). Hash/fingerprint/HMAC sites are untouched.
- `AqueductConfig.__eq__`/`__hash__` no longer compare the `_cli_engine_overrides` private attr, so two configs differing only by their `-s/--set` layer compare equal — closes a latent trap (no shipped call site compares config instances today).
- `aqueduct patch list` serves TEXT output from `patch_index` instead of reading every patch body, when an observability store is available.
- `.github/workflows/version-matrix.yml`'s Verify step now also regenerates `docs/compatibility.md`'s ENGINE_MATRIX region before diffing, so a stale table (an engine's `capabilities.yml` changed without a local `aqueduct dev capabilities docs` run) is caught instead of merging undetected.

## [2.1.0rc1] — 2026-08-15

**Pre-release.** PEP 440 spelling, so `pip install aqueduct-core` SKIPS this
version unless `--pre` is passed — that is the intended "unstable" marker.

Ten changes below are marked **BREAKING**. They ship inside a MINOR bump
rather than a major one, consistent with the stability contract stated at the
top of this file: the contract applies from v1.0.0, and during alpha/RC
breaking changes may land in any release provided they are marked.

### Added
- **A benchmark scenario can no longer pass while verifying nothing: the `.aqscenario.yml` reader is strict, the grader can express a config fix, and it can now say WHY a patch was refused.** Scenario 07 is the case that names the whole change.
