- **The DuckDB engine config surface + `httpfs` remote storage — `engine.duckdb.*` (Q4-follow-up task).** `DuckDBEngineConfig` (`aqueduct/config.py`) was an intentionally empty block since Q4 (any field would have been a silent no-op — `_make_session` read nothing from it). Seven real fields now, each wired into `_make_session` (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py`): `memory_limit`/`threads` (`SET` at session creation), `database_path` (a persistent file replacing the default `:memory:` connection — LOCAL PATHS ONLY, rejected at config-load like `checkpoint_root` — raising a receiving cross-engine handoff island's RAM ceiling AND letting large intermediates spill to disk, two independent motivations kept together per the owner's instruction not to split this task), `extension_repository` (`SET custom_extension_repository`, the airgapped-mirror escape hatch), and `s3_key_id_secret`/`s3_secret_access_key_secret`/`s3_region` (secret KEY NAMES — never literal credentials — resolved through the EXISTING `secrets:` block resolver, the same `aqueduct.secrets.resolve_secret()` `@aq.secret()` calls, and fed into DuckDB's own `CREATE SECRET (TYPE S3, KEY_ID ?, SECRET ?, REGION ?)` via parameter binding, verified against a real DuckDB connection to succeed with `httpfs` unloaded). `httpfs` itself is a DuckDB EXTENSION, not a Python package — no new dependency, no new extra; DuckDB's own `autoinstall_known_extensions`/`autoload_known_extensions` (both default `True`, duckdb>=1.0) already load it on first `s3://`/`gs://` touch with zero Aqueduct code. New `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/extensions.py` adds `ensure_extension()` (proactive `INSTALL`/`LOAD`, called only when S3 credentials or `extension_repository` are configured — a deliberate signal of remote-storage intent) wrapping any failure as `DuckDBExtensionError` (new `AqueductError` subclass) naming the airgapped-install cause and both escapes (a pre-populated `~/.duckdb/extensions`, or `engine.duckdb.extension_repository`) — never a bare `duckdb.IOException`/HTTP error from DuckDB's own loader; measured against a real unreachable-repository failure (`duckdb.IOException`, `IO Error: Failed to download extension ... Could not establish connection`) to confirm the wrap actually fires on the real exception shape. A new DuckDB session-startup warning rule (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/warnings/httpfs_availability.py`, rule id `duckdb_httpfs_availability`) mirrors the SHAPE of Spark's `jar_availability` rule (not the mechanism — a jar ships to Spark's executor fleet at session creation, a DuckDB extension installs per-connection in-process, deliberately NOT unified into one abstraction per the owner's explicit call) for the remaining case: a Blueprint reads/writes a remote path with no explicit S3/repository config at all, relying on DuckDB's own autoload. This closes a real pre-existing silent no-op found while wiring it in: `duckdb_/executor.py::execute()`'s `warnings_suppress`/`warnings_silence_all` kwargs were accepted but never read anywhere in the function. Also fixed a related latent no-op every session-construction call site carried: `SessionSpec.engine_config` was built unconditionally from Spark's merged conf dict regardless of the ACTUAL target engine (harmless only because DuckDB ignored `engine_config` entirely before this task) — a new `_resolve_session_engine_config()` helper (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`) now resolves each engine's OWN config sub-model via `model_dump()`, used at both the single-engine session build and the polyglot `run_polyglot()` call site (which also gained a `secrets_config` parameter threading the resolved `secrets:` block through `SessionSpec.engine_options["secrets"]` so DuckDB can resolve its `s3_*` secret names the same way `@aq.secret()` does). `aqueduct doctor`'s `handoff-access:duckdb` check no longer unconditionally reports `skip` for a remote `handoff.root` ("DuckDB has no httpfs wiring yet") — it now attempts the same real round trip a local root gets, using the configured `engine.duckdb.s3_*`/`extension_repository` if present, reporting a genuine `ok`/`fail` instead. Seven new `config.engine.duckdb.*` capability leaves, positionally owned by DuckDB alone (Spark's table is unchanged, 208 rows) — DuckDB's table is now 213 rows. **NOT part of this task, decided by the owner:** Delta on DuckDB (needs `delta-rs`, a real new dependency, and carries a second-Delta-writer value-drift risk — separate decision); JDBC/`postgres_scanner` (DuckDB has no JDBC client and never can; `ingress.format.jdbc` stays honestly `unsupported`, already recorded). (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/extensions.py` [new], `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/warnings/__init__.py` [new], `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/warnings/httpfs_availability.py` [new], `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/orchestrator.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.1/§10.9 → Version 2.41, `docs/production_guide.md`, `docs/extending.md`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`, `docs/compatibility.md`; tests: `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_engine_config.py` [new], `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_warnings_httpfs_availability.py` [new], `tests/test_config.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_doctor_handoff.py`)
- **Per-module `engine:` field, engine islands, and the per-island capability gate — grammar + compile-time gate only (cross-engine handoff Step 1).** A Blueprint may now span more than one execution engine. Every module gains an optional `engine:` field (`ModuleSchema.engine` / `Module.engine`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py` + `aqueduct/parser/models.py`) — a scalar engine NAME, deliberately the same word as the existing blueprint-level `engine:` BLOCK (Q4, per-engine session settings) but a different level; the two never collide (different key shapes, distinct error messages). Resolution follows four rules in strict precedence: (1) an explicit pin on the module wins; (2) unset inherits the SINGLE upstream parent's already-resolved engine — a Probe's `attach_to` target counts as its one parent, so an unpinned Probe naturally colocates with its target; (3) unset with multiple parents resolved to DIFFERING engines is a `CompileError` demanding an explicit pin; (4) unset with no parents (an Ingress) falls back to `deployment.engine` — the config default only ever supplies rule 4, an explicit pin always wins over it. New `aqueduct/compiler/islands.py` (pure, pyspark-free, no engine imports by name) derives islands — connected subgraphs of modules sharing one resolved engine, over the same `main`/`spillway` data-edge basis the executor's parallel-component detection uses — and the boundary edges between them (derived and validated only; synthesizing the handoff module itself is later, additive work). Two new compile-time gates: a Probe/Assert that resolves to a different engine than its target (`attach_to` for Probe, upstream parent for Assert) is a `CompileError` — neither module type may introduce an engine boundary; a spillway edge crossing islands is a `CompileError` in v1 (cross-engine quarantine routing isn't wired yet). Disjoint components pinned to different engines fall out of the same derivation with ZERO boundary edges — two independent single-engine flows run side by side in one Blueprint with no handoff at all. `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py`'s existing gate (Phase 78) now runs PER ISLAND against that island's own engine rather than once against the whole manifest and one engine — an island whose engine has no registered capability declaration is a `CompileError` (the same fail-closed `UnknownEngineError`); for a single-engine Blueprint this degenerates to the pre-existing single-gate call exactly. Manifest-scoped `feature.*`/`type.*` leaves (UDF language, UDF return type) are NOT owned by one module — a UDF is registered once and referenced from SQL by name — so new `aqueduct/compiler/udf_attribution.py::attribute_udfs_to_islands` attributes each UDF to the island(s) whose SQL actually references it (sqlglot, reusing the exact same parse `aqueduct/compiler/lineage.py`'s column lineage already runs — two new lineage helpers, `referenced_function_names`/`referenced_function_names_in_expr`, never a second SQL parser) before the gate filters each island's `udf_registry` copy. This is what makes the phase's flagship shape compile: a Python UDF used only inside a Spark island's Channel SQL no longer fails the moment an unrelated DuckDB island exists anywhere in the Blueprint — DuckDB's `feature.python_udf: unsupported` verdict is checked only against islands that actually reference the UDF. Attribution is fail-closed by construction: a SQL-bearing construct sqlglot cannot parse (or a genuinely dynamic reference) keeps that island in every UDF's checked set rather than dropping it, and a UDF attributed to zero islands with nothing unparseable anywhere falls back to every island — the same conservative behavior as before this attribution step existed. Scanned surfaces: Channel `op: sql`'s `query` (full statement), `op: join`/`op: filter`'s `condition`/`expr`, `op: deduplicate`'s `order_by`, `op: sort`'s `order_by`/`columns` (either spelling, a string or a list — a UDF call there is legal on both engines even though Spark's own sort implementation only honors a trailing `ASC`/`DESC` token and would fail such a call at runtime, since the same field genuinely invokes the UDF on a DuckDB-resolved island), any Channel's `spillway_condition` (all wrapped as a `WHERE` fragment), a Junction `conditional` branch's `condition`, and an Assert `sql`/`sql_row` rule's `expr`. Channel `op: select`'s column list and Probe/Assert `type: custom`'s Python callables are confirmed non-issues (column names not expressions; no SQL text at all) rather than tracked gaps. The new `module.field.engine` capability leaf is `supported` on both shipped engines (compiler-consumed metadata, not engine-executed behavior — no `tests:` key required, it's a `module.field.*` leaf, out of `execution_leaves()` scope). (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/islands.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/udf_attribution.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/lineage.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/models.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `docs/specs.md` §4.3/§10.9 → Version 2.34, `SKILL.md`, `aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`; tests: `tests/test_compiler/test_islands.py`, `tests/test_compiler/test_udf_attribution.py`, `tests/test_backlog.py` [one `@pytest.mark.todo` for the residual unparseable-construct over-rejection surface])

### Changed
- **BREAKING — the gate status `skip` is replaced by `not_applicable` and `unavailable`, and `unavailable` now blocks auto-apply.** One word was doing two opposite jobs. Gate 3 wrote `skip` both when there was nothing to replay (a config-only patch touches no module logic) and when it *could not* replay (the target engine's dependencies are absent, its session would not start, or the Blueprint is polyglot and the sandbox covers only one engine); Gate 4 wrote it both when no baseline existed yet and when plan capture does not work on the session at all. A patch that was **never verified** therefore rendered identically to one that **needed no verification**, and auto-approval accepted both — the same defect class already fixed twice this phase for Gate 2's false `pass` and Gate 3's over-claiming detail. The partition is now whether a check was *owed*: `not_applicable` means none was (no surface for this gate, or the operator declared none via `sandbox_mode: off`), stays informational and never blocks; `unavailable` means one was owed and the environment prevented it, so **nothing about the patch was verified**. For Gate 3 that blocks auto-apply and requires a human — a deliberate availability cost, since a heal on a machine without the target engine installed now stalls instead of applying a patch nothing replayed. Gate 4 stays warn-only: blocking there would refuse every heal on Spark Connect for a diagnostic that is advisory by design. The vocabulary moves out of bare literals in four modules — each carrying a comment claiming to "follow" another's — into `aqueduct/patch/gate_status.py`, where the auto-apply rule also lives as one function that both the heal loop and `patch preview` call rather than re-listing statuses. That set is written as the statuses which *permit* rather than those which block, so a status added later defaults to "a human decides". **`patch_simulation` rows written before this are not migrated and cannot be** — the distinction was never recorded, so a pre-2.1.0 `skip` row genuinely means "one of the two, unknown which"; `gate_rejection_rates` is unaffected (it counts only `fail`), but a rising `unavailable` count means heals stalling on missing engines rather than on bad patches. (`aqueduct/patch/gate_status.py` (new), `patch/preview.py`, `patch/explain_gate.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `cli/patch.py`, `cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `docs/specs.md` → Version 2.63, `docs/observability_guide.md`; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_gate_status.py` (new), plus updated assertions in `test_patch_preview_gate3.py`, `test_patch_preview_sandbox_engine.py`, `test_explain_gate.py`, `test_cli_sandbox_mode.py`)
- **BREAKING — a Blueprint's `agent:` block is policy-only; connection settings move to `aqueduct.yml`.** `provider`, `base_url`, `api_key`, `model`, `provider_options`, `timeout` and `cascade` are rejected in a Blueprint and accepted only at the engine level. This is a security fix: a Blueprint author previously chose the LLM endpoint the healing loop ships `FailureContext` to — pruned manifest, provenance, error text, and in agentic mode sampled data rows — so any pipeline failure was an exfiltration opportunity to a host the operator never approved, using the operator's runtime. Redaction scrubs secrets, not data. The repo already refused this trust shape for deployment concerns (`SparkEngineBlockSchema` omits `master_url`, `DuckDBEngineBlockSchema` omits `database_path`/`s3_*`); the agent block was the gap. `AgentSchema` now extends a shared `AgentPolicySchema` base with the connection fields absent, so `extra="forbid"` rejects one by name and the two levels cannot drift on the policy half. The `agent.model: [cheap, expensive]` cascade shorthand went with it — cascade tiers are bundles of connection settings, so there was no policy-only form to keep; write an explicit `agent.cascade:` in `aqueduct.yml`. Every Blueprint in this repo was already policy-only, so the only non-doc migration was `aqueduct doctor`'s cascade-tier credential check, which now reads the engine config directly instead of parsing a Blueprint. (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `parser/models.py`, `parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`, both engines' `capabilities.yml`, `docs/specs.md` → Version 2.59, `SKILL.md`, `docs/production_guide.md`, `docs/cli_reference.md`, both templates; tests: `tests/test_parser/test_agent_policy_split.py` and seven updated files)
- **BREAKING — the `set_spark_config` patch op is replaced by `set_engine_config`.** An engine-named op does not fit a grammar that is otherwise engine-agnostic, and DuckDB's Blueprint-level `engine.duckdb.memory_limit`/`threads` was unreachable by any op. `set_engine_config` carries a required `engine` field plus `key`/`value`, and addresses either shape an `engine.<name>:` block can take — a free-form `conf` bag (Spark) or typed fields (DuckDB) — by asking whether that engine's block schema declares a `conf` field, the same structural question the parser asks to read the block back. No engine name is hardcoded in the apply path, so a third engine works the moment its schema entry exists; addressing a key that is not a declared field on a typed block raises `PatchOperationError` naming the available fields, rather than writing a key nothing reads. **No back-compat alias:** the field sets differ, so an alias could not have worked, and one would be the accept-both-shapes reader this project bans. A stored patch body still naming `set_spark_config` raises `RetiredPatchOpError` (an `AqueductError` subclass) when re-parsed; the heal-cache replay path reports that candidate as unusable and falls through to the LLM rather than crashing or reporting a silent cache miss, and `patch apply`/`patch preview` surface it as a `PatchError`. The default-forbidden-in-auto-mode guardrail posture carries over. `PROMPT_VERSION` 1.9 → 1.10 (the composed op table changed). (`aqueduct/patch/grammar.py`, `operations.py`, `apply.py`, `provenance.py`, `preview.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/agent/loop.py`, `aqueduct/dashboard/app.py`, `templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`, `docs/specs.md` → Version 2.55; tests across `tests/test_patch/`, `tests/test_cli/`, `tests/test_agent/`)
- **BREAKING — the internal `spark_config` carrier becomes per-engine `engine_config`, so a Blueprint can override engine config for ANY engine (cross-engine remediation).** The `engine.<name>:` namespacing that landed two phases ago stopped at the YAML boundary: `Blueprint`/`Manifest` still carried `spark_config: dict[str, Any]`, and `resolve_session_engine_config` read the Manifest only on its Spark branch. Every other engine got its `aqueduct.yml` config and nothing else, so a Blueprint-level `engine.duckdb:` value had nowhere to go and was silently discarded — while the same override worked for Spark, as documented. `Blueprint.engine_config` / `Manifest.engine_config: dict[str, dict[str, Any]]`, keyed by engine name, replaces it, and every registered engine now gets the same precedence: its `aqueduct.yml` config, overridden by its Blueprint-level entry. The Blueprint-level bag keeps only keys the author explicitly wrote (`model_dump(exclude_unset=True)`) — a plain dump would emit every declared field at its default and let an untouched `None` clobber a real `aqueduct.yml` value. The parser derives the per-engine bags by walking `EngineBlockSchema`'s own fields rather than reading `engine.spark` by name, so a future engine needs no change here. Manifest JSON now serializes `engine_config`, not `spark_config`: this changes `manifest_hash` for every Blueprint, which invalidates existing checkpoints (a full rerun on next execution, correct for a changed manifest) and orphans cross-engine handoff spill directories under the old hash (the existing sweep collects them). No YAML, CLI, or patch-grammar surface changed. (`aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `parser/parser.py`, `compiler/models.py`, `compiler/compiler.py`, `executor/session_config.py`, `doctor/__init__.py`, `cli/hooks.py`, `cli/run.py`, `cli/drift.py`, `patch/preview.py`, `docs/specs.md` → Version 2.53; tests: `tests/test_parser/test_engine_block_resolution.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_session_config.py`, plus fixture updates across ~40 files)
- **BREAKING — `ModuleSchema` splits into a discriminated union, one typed `config:` per module type (Pass C, cross-engine remediation).** Before this, all 9 module types (`Ingress`/`Channel`/`Egress`/`Junction`/`Funnel`/`Probe`/`Regulator`/`Arcade`/`Assert`) shared ONE flat schema with `config: dict[str, Any]` — a freeform dict, so ANY key parsed regardless of whether any executor read it. That is a structural hole in the capability framework: a leaf is derived by introspecting pydantic models, so a key inside a freeform dict is invisible to it by construction — no gate can ever ask "does this engine support X" about it, and a Blueprint using a wrong/dead key compiles and runs as a SILENT NO-OP on every engine. This is the same root cause behind `materialize`/`watermark_column` (fixed in 2.40, entry below) and the still-open `config.maintenance`/`report` gaps this pass closes.

  `aqueduct/parser/schema.py` now declares `IngressSchema`/`ChannelSchema`/`EgressSchema`/`JunctionSchema`/`FunnelSchema`/`ProbeSchema`/`RegulatorSchema`/`ArcadeSchema`/`AssertSchema`, a pydantic discriminated union on `type`, each with a typed `config:` sub-model absorbing that type's real keys (`IngressConfigSchema.format`/`path`/`table`/`schema_hint`/`time_travel`/`on_new_columns`/`options`/…, `EgressConfigSchema.maintenance`/`merge_key`/`on_new_columns`/…, and so on for all nine). `config:` remains a nested YAML block — this is not the `materialize`/`watermark_column` "promote to module top level" shape — only its freeform-dict SHAPE is gone; `extra="forbid"` on each sub-model makes a misplaced or misspelled key a structural `ParseError` naming it. The one deliberate exception is `options:` (Ingress/Egress) — a freeform passthrough dict forwarded verbatim to the engine's reader/writer, since enumerating every Spark/DuckDB option is out of scope by design — plus two genuinely polymorphic fields (Ingress `schema_hint`, Channel `columns`) kept as an untyped container with their accepted shapes documented on the field.

  `Module.config` (the frozen dataclass field every executor/compiler consumer reads via `.get(...)`) is UNCHANGED — `aqueduct/parser/parser.py` dumps the validated per-type `config:` sub-model back to a plain dict (`model_dump(by_alias=True, exclude_none=True)`) before the existing anchor/context-resolve pipeline runs, so downstream layers are structurally untouched; only parse-time validation tightened. `aqueduct/executor/path_keys.py`'s per-type audit (needed to confirm which keys survive the split) found `data_dir`/`input_dir`/`output_dir` and `jar` (on Ingress/Egress specifically — `jar` is real only on `UdfSchema`) were DEAD registry entries nothing ever read; dropped, every module type now has an explicit row, the legacy blanket-fallback tuple is empty.

  **Capability leaves:** `aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py` keeps the pre-Pass-C `module.field.<name>` leaf id for the ~12 fields common to every type (`ModuleCommonSchema` — `id`/`label`/`engine`/`retry`/…); every type-specific field — both already-module-top-level ones (`attach_to`, `ref`, `materialize`, `watermark_column`) and every newly-typed former-freeform key — gets a new `<type_lower>.field.<name>` leaf (e.g. `egress.field.maintenance`, `channel.field.query`, `probe.field.signals`), plus a `<block>.field.<name>` leaf per nested/list-item block (`ingress_time_travel`, `egress_maintenance`, `junction_branch`, `probe_signal`, `assert_on_fail`, `assert_rule` — same mechanism as the pre-existing `agent`/`retry_policy`/… blocks). 149 new leaves, all given real per-engine verdicts (no default sweep) — `egress_maintenance.field.*` is the acceptance case this pass was for: DuckDB declares every one `unsupported` (no OPTIMIZE/VACUUM/compaction for the formats it writes), closing the `config.maintenance` silent no-op. Whole-module-type gaps (Probe, Assert — unsupported on DuckDB) cascade their new field-level leaves to `unsupported` too, for the same reason.

  **Real defects this exposed and fixed (not just typed around):** several gallery snippets and compiler test fixtures had been silently no-oping on wrong field names for a while, invisible until `extra="forbid"` started rejecting them — `24_assert_types_full`'s `min_count`/`max_count`/`max_rate`/`query`+`expect`/`columns` were never-read synonyms of the real `min`/`max`/`max`/`expr`/`expected` keys (every one of those rules had been a no-op); `43_probe_report_stdout`'s `method: exact` silently fell through to the `sample` branch; three snippets used `num_rows` (never read; real key `n`) and a dead `partition_column` on `partition_stats` (which takes no config at all); the `07_spark_oom_shuffle` aqscenario's already-catalogued dead `spark_config:` knob inside a Channel's `config:` migrated to the real `engine.spark.conf` block. `AssertRuleSchema`/`ProbeSignalSchema` gained a legitimate `id:` field — a human-readable label used pervasively across every shipped Assert/Probe snippet, never read by any handler, but a genuine authoring convention rather than a misspelled synonym.

  **Migration:** the error names the exact key and module (`module "m1" → Ingress → config → sql — Extra inputs are not permitted`); check that type's real `config:` fields (`docs/specs.md` §4.3/§4.4, or `SKILL.md`) for the correct spelling or the right place for the value (a freeform test/debug key belongs under `options:`, not loose in `config:`). No legacy-key validator, accept-both-shapes reader, or compatibility shim was added, per the standing breaking-change rule — `extra="forbid"` already reports the offending key by name. Migrated every affected gallery snippet, aqscenario blueprint, and test fixture found by a full-repo parse sweep. (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`, `aqueduct/executor/path_keys.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `docs/specs.md` §4.3/§4.4/§10.9 → Version 2.42, `SKILL.md`, `aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`, `AGENTS.md`; tests: `tests/test_parser/`, `tests/test_compiler/`, `tests/test_capabilities/`, `tests/test_patch/test_patch_apply.py`, `tests/test_gallery.py`, plus fixture updates across `tests/fixtures/audit/`)

- **BREAKING — Channel `materialize`/`watermark_column` move out of the freeform `config:` dict to declared module fields (Pass A item 2, cross-engine remediation).** Both used to live inside a Channel's `config:` block — a freeform `dict[str, Any]`, so anything inside it was INVISIBLE to the capability framework by construction: no gate could ever see them, no engine had to declare a verdict, and a Blueprint using `materialize: incremental` compiled cleanly on an engine that silently ignored it entirely (the exact root cause of the DuckDB `19_depot_incremental` bug fixed below). Promoted to declared `ModuleSchema`/`Module` fields — siblings of `config:`, same shape as Probe's `attach_to` — so they become capability leaves automatically (`module.field.materialize`, `module.field.watermark_column`, via `aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py::all_leaves()`) and every registered engine must give them an explicit verdict. Both engines declare `supported` (Spark already implemented this; DuckDB's implementation lands in the companion Fixed entry below), each with voluntary `tests:` links (not required — `module.field.*` sits outside `execution_leaves()` scope — added anyway since real per-engine tests now exist for both). **Migration:** move both keys from inside a Channel's `config:` block up to the module level:
  ```yaml
  # before                          # after
  - id: new_events                  - id: new_events
    type: Channel                     type: Channel
    config:                           materialize: incremental
      op: sql                         watermark_column: event_ts
      materialize: incremental        config:
      watermark_column: event_ts        op: sql
      query: ...                        query: ...
  ```
  No legacy-key validator, accept-both-shapes reader, or compatibility shim was added (per AGENTS.md's standing breaking-change rule) — a Blueprint that still nests them inside `config:` parses (the dict stays freeform) but the incremental behaviour silently stops, since neither engine reads them from there anymore; `extra="forbid"` was never going to catch this shape anyway (a freeform dict accepts anything), which is exactly why the freeform placement was the bug. Migrated every affected gallery snippet (`19_depot_incremental`, `31_depot_kv`) and test fixture. (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/executor.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `docs/specs.md` §4.4 → Version 2.40, `SKILL.md`, `aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_incremental.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_incremental.py` [new], `tests/test_compiler/test_compiler_warnings.py`, `tests/test_compiler/test_warning_rules_audit.py`, `tests/test_parser/test_resolver.py`, `tests/test_gallery.py`)
- **Config-leaf SCOPING — the `capabilities.yml` checklist narrows from ~105 to ~17 `config.*` leaves per engine (Q4 step 2).** Not a fourth `Support` verdict and not a behavior change for `aqueduct.yml` users: `deployment.engine`/`webhooks.*`/`secrets.*`/`stores.*`/`lineage.*`/most of `agent.*`/`danger.*` (~88 of the 105 `config.*` leaves `AqueductConfig` derives) run entirely in core code paths with no per-engine implementation to diverge from, so asking every engine to declare a verdict for webhook backoff was a category error the framework forced anyway because the closure test needed something to compare against. Every field in `aqueduct/config.py` now carries an EXPLICIT `Field(..., json_schema_extra={"engine_scoped": True})` or `{"engine_scoped": False}` tag — **there is no untagged/implicit state.** A field with neither key raises `CapabilityScopeError` naming the field and both resolutions, the moment the walker runs; an earlier version of this change let a missing tag fall back to core silently, which is the identical shape as the banned `all_leaves_default()` bug (a classification nobody decided) and was corrected before landing. The ~17 `True`-tagged leaves: `probes.*`, `metrics.use_observe`, `timezone`, `checkpoint_root`, `engine.spark.*`, `deployment.target`/`databricks.*`, `handoff.root`, `agent.sandbox_master_url`/`block_on_explain_regression`, `danger.allow_full_probe_actions`. `aqueduct/executor/config_leaves.py::all_config_leaves()` yields the `True`-tagged fields (the checklist); a new `core_config_leaves()` yields the `False`-tagged complement, derived from the SAME tag so the two cannot drift — reclassifying a key is now a one-word `True`↔`False` diff at the field itself. `explicitly_set_config_leaves()` is narrowed identically (`aqueduct/config.py::_warn_ignored_config_keys` loops it and calls `caps.verdict()` — once a core leaf leaves the checklist there is no row to ask). Per-leaf verification (grep each field's actual readers, not block-level guessing) corrected the working classification in two places found along the way: `config.danger.allow_full_probe_actions` is engine-scoped (the flag is read by `spark/probe.py`, an engine module, and gates a Spark-specific concern — avoiding extra distributed actions — that DuckDB accepts for signature parity only, per its own docstring, while never acting on it; same shape as `probes.max_sample_rows` — DuckDB's verdict corrected from `supported` to `ignored_with_warning` to match, since the flag is genuinely inert there), and `config.agent.block_on_explain_regression` is engine-scoped (Gate 4's explain-plan snapshot capture is implemented exclusively in `aqueduct/executor/spark/executor.py`; DuckDB has no plan-capture mechanism at all). A third correction, `config.lineage.*` → core, is recorded as its own entry below (a distinct finding, verified independently, that reverses an earlier classification rather than confirming one).

  **`engine.<name>.*` is positionally owned — new refinement, not previously optional.** A leaf under a per-engine namespaced block can only ever mean something to that ONE engine: `all_config_leaves(engine=...)` accepts an optional engine name and, when given, drops every OTHER engine's `engine.<name>.*` leaves. `config.engine.spark.master_url`/`config.engine.spark.conf` therefore appear ONLY in Spark's table (206 rows: 189 grammar + 17 config) and NEVER in DuckDB's (204 rows: 189 + 15) — forced by the sequenced-next DuckDB engine-config task, which would otherwise make Spark declare a verdict for every one of DuckDB's future extension knobs. Because a field namespaced to one engine has no coherent "core" reading, a field under `engine.<name>.*` tagged `False` (or left untagged) is ALSO a contradiction and raises the new THIRD error type, `CapabilityScopeError` (`aqueduct/errors.py`) — a SIBLING of `CapabilityDeclarationError`, deliberately NOT a subclass, so a shared `except CapabilityDeclarationError:` (`aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, now also catching this one explicitly) cannot swallow it and re-conflate two states with different fixes. Raised by the walker at every engine's real registration time (each engine's `capabilities.py` calls `all_config_leaves(engine=<name>)` at import) — never CI-only.

  **The safety net is one invariant test, not a committed snapshot.** A `core_config_leaves.yml` + CI `git diff` was considered and dropped: the per-field tag already is the one source of truth, sitting at the field it describes. What replaces it: `tests/test_capabilities/test_config_scope_invariant.py` asserts `core_config_leaves() ∩ {leaves any engine declares non-supported} == ∅` — a leaf some engine already declares `unsupported`/`ignored_with_warning` has a live `engine_key_ignored` warning path, and reclassifying it to core would silently delete that path with nothing else noticing (the existing orphaned-row check in `dev capabilities check` catches every OTHER way to get this wrong — see the enforcement table in `docs/specs.md` §10.9 and AGENTS.md). Proven falsifiable both ways per AGENTS.md's unfalsifiable-meta-test rule: (1) a temporary untagged field raises `CapabilityScopeError` naming itself; (2) flipping `config.probes.max_sample_rows` from `True` to `False` by hand and rerunning turns the invariant test red, naming the leaf and the offending engine (`duckdb`), in both cases before being reverted.

  **`aqueduct dev capabilities sync` now prunes orphaned rows by default** (`prune_orphans=True`, `--no-prune` to fall back to the old report-only behavior) — an orphaned row already makes a declaration invalid (`CapabilityDeclarationError` at registration), so `sync` needed to be able to hand back a valid table, not just report the problem; this is how both engines' `capabilities.yml` were mechanically narrowed from 294 rows to 206 (Spark) / 204 (DuckDB) in this change, a plain reviewable git diff on each data file with every surviving row's verdict/`requires`/`hint` byte-identical to before (except the one corrected `danger.allow_full_probe_actions` verdict on DuckDB, above). `capability_tooling.governed_leaves()`/`scaffold()`/`check()` all take the same `engine=` parameter so a brand-new engine's scaffold, and CI's `check`, agree with registration on exactly what that engine's checklist is. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/errors.py`, `aqueduct/executor/config_leaves.py`, `aqueduct/executor/capability_tooling.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/cli/dev.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.39, `docs/extending.md`, `docs/compatibility.md`, `AGENTS.md`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_config_scope_invariant.py` [new], `tests/test_capabilities/test_error_split.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_closure.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_walker_determinism.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_verdict_test_links.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_dev_capabilities.py`)

- **Corrected capability claim: `config.lineage.*` was declared as if engine-scoped; it is CORE, and both engines' rows for it are gone.** DuckDB's `capabilities.yml` declared `config.lineage.openlineage_url`/`openlineage_namespace` as `ignored_with_warning` with a hint reading "the DuckDB executor does not emit OpenLineage events" — that hint was false. `Surveyor.__init__` (`aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`) builds the OpenLineage emitter gated only on `openlineage_url` being set, with no engine condition anywhere; emission (START/COMPLETE/FAIL) and the column-level `columnLineage` facet are both derived from compile-time lineage (sqlglot) and delivered via `infra/http.py` — no engine module reads either key. A DuckDB run emits OpenLineage events exactly like a Spark run always has. Both leaves are now tagged `engine_scoped: False` in `aqueduct/config.py` and their rows are removed from BOTH `capabilities.yml` files (Spark's two `supported` rows and DuckDB's two `ignored_with_warning` rows) — core means absent from every table, not "supported everywhere." Nothing changes about what a user's `aqueduct.yml` does; this corrects only which engines are asked (none) and what the published matrix claims. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9, `docs/extending.md`, `docs/compatibility.md`, `AGENTS.md`)
- **BREAKING — engine config gets its own namespace: `spark_config` and `deployment.master_url` move under `engine.<name>:` (Q4 step 1).** `spark_config` was a top-level `aqueduct.yml` dict named after one engine, with no room for a second engine's own knobs short of a new top-level `<engine>_config` dict per engine forever — the exact scaling problem DuckDB (a second registered engine) exposes. `aqueduct_config` bumps `"1.0"` → `"2.0"`. Both `AqueductConfig` (`aqueduct/config.py`) and `BlueprintSchema` (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`) gain a matching `engine:` block, namespaced by engine name, mirroring each other's inner shape exactly — a key under `engine.<name>.` belongs to that engine and every other engine ignores it without ceremony (warn, never error — the existing `engine_key_ignored` config-leaf gate, unchanged mechanism, new leaf names).

  ```yaml
  # aqueduct.yml — before (1.0)              # aqueduct.yml — after (2.0)
  deployment:                                deployment:
    engine: spark                              engine: spark
    target: local                              target: local
    master_url: "local[*]"                   engine:
  spark_config:                                spark:
    spark.sql.shuffle.partitions: 200            master_url: "local[*]"
                                                  conf:
                                                    spark.sql.shuffle.partitions: 200
  ```

  ```yaml
  # Blueprint YAML — before                  # Blueprint YAML — after (same inner shape as aqueduct.yml)
  spark_config:                              engine:
    spark.sql.shuffle.partitions: 200          spark:
                                                  conf:
                                                    spark.sql.shuffle.partitions: 200
  ```

  **Migration is the loud rejection, not a compatibility shim.** Both old locations are hard-rejected by the existing `extra="forbid"` on `AqueductConfig` / `DeploymentConfig` / `BlueprintSchema` — pydantic's own "Extra inputs are not permitted" already names the exact offending key (`deployment.master_url`, `spark_config`), so a pre-2.0 file or Blueprint fails at load with a `ConfigError`/`ParseError` (both `AqueductError` subclasses, exiting through the existing `CONFIG_ERROR` code) naming precisely what to move and where — no bespoke legacy-key validator, no accept-both-shapes reader, no auto-migration. AGENTS.md's Bug-Family Prevention Rules gains a new standing rule for this: a breaking change ships as documentation (this entry, the specs.md update) and a hard schema rejection, not as code that keeps the old shape alive.

  `deployment.target` stays put — only `master_url` moved, since it is Spark's own `SparkSession.builder.master()`, not a cross-engine deployment concern. The target/master_url cross-check moved from `DeploymentConfig` to `AqueductConfig` (it now needs both blocks); `DeploymentConfig` still owns `engine`-registration validation and the emr/dataproc/databricks-block checks (independently of `master_url`). New models: `EngineConfig`/`SparkEngineConfig`/`DuckDBEngineConfig` (`aqueduct/config.py`), `EngineBlockSchema`/`SparkEngineBlockSchema`/`DuckDBEngineBlockSchema` (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`). The internal `Blueprint`/`Manifest` dataclass field `spark_config` (and its JSON `to_dict()` key) keeps its pre-2.0 spelling on purpose — it is engine-agnostic AST/compiled-artifact plumbing embedded in the Executor/FailureContext contract, not the Blueprint-authoring surface this rename covers, and renaming it would ripple into dozens of test fixtures that construct `Manifest(spark_config=...)` directly for no user-facing benefit; it is now sourced from `engine.spark.conf` at parse time. The `set_spark_config` PatchSpec op is UNCHANGED by name (still `engine_shaped` in the heal-provenance classification) — only its Blueprint-dict write target moved, from `spark_config.<key>` to `engine.spark.conf.<key>`.

  **DuckDB's knobs did not get wired.** `engine.duckdb:` exists as an accepted, empty sub-block (`extra="forbid"`, zero fields) on both sides. `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py::_make_session` always opens a bare `:memory:` connection today and reads nothing from `SessionSpec.engine_config` — wiring `memory_limit`/`threads` for real would mean generalizing every session-construction call site (the main run path, the patch-preview sandbox gate, doctor, `aqueduct test`) to resolve a per-engine config bag instead of always building Spark's merged conf, a session-plumbing change that reaches well past this task and risks a half-wired, inconsistent result across those call sites. Per AGENTS.md's "no silent no-ops" rule, a field nothing reads is worse than no field — left undeclared until a real consumer exists.

  Both shipped engines' `capabilities.yml` re-declare the two new leaves (`config.engine.spark.master_url`, `config.engine.spark.conf`) with the same verdicts the retired leaves carried (`supported` on Spark; `ignored_with_warning` on DuckDB, same hints reworded). `aqueduct dev capabilities sync` appended the new leaves as `undeclared` and flagged the two retired ones as orphaned rows for manual removal (by design — it never guesses a verdict or silently deletes a row). All 44 gallery snippets' `aqueduct.yml` (plus 3 Blueprint-level `spark_config:` blocks and the self-healing showcase) migrated to the new shape, along with every doc/template that showed the old one (`docs/specs.md` §4.2/§10.1 → Version 2.33, `docs/production_guide.md`, `docs/spark_guide.md`, `docs/cli_reference.md`, `SKILL.md`, both `aqueduct/templates/default/*.template` files). (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/patch/operations.py`, `aqueduct/patch/grammar.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/drift.py`, `aqueduct/cli/project.py`, `aqueduct/cli/diagnostics.py`, `aqueduct/cli/hooks.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/dashboard/app.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/session.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/ingress.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/warnings/jar_availability.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `AGENTS.md`; tests: `tests/test_parser/test_config.py`, `tests/test_config.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_config_gate.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_closure.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_patch_operations.py`, `tests/test_parser/test_resolver.py`, `tests/test_compiler/test_warning_rules_audit.py`, `tests/test_overrides.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_doctor_new.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_validate_format_json.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_env_resolution.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_cwd.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_hooks.py`, `tests/test_drift/test_drift_command.py`)
- **The handoff-spill release rule's one open trade-off is now RULED ON and ACCEPTED: a kept spill is released by a later success, even when someone is mid-investigation, and no protection is built.** `keep_on_failure: true` keeps a failed run's cross-engine spill so a manual `--resume` can read it; the orphan sweep releases it once a later run of the same blueprint succeeds. That release rule has an edge: a pipeline fails Monday, an operator is still debugging Tuesday, and an unrelated scheduled run succeeds Monday night — the spill is gone. Three candidate answers were named when the rule shipped (exempt the most recent failure per blueprint, add an opt-out, or accept it) and none had been chosen. The answer is **accept**, and the code, the spec, and the test suite now say so instead of describing an open question. **The reasoning, for the record:** the hazard is unmeasured, and every surface an operator actually debugs from survives the sweep untouched — the `run_records` row, the `failure_contexts` row, and the stack trace are store records, not spill directories. A handoff spill is an intermediate parquet materialisation of one island's output, so losing it costs a rerun, not a diagnosis. Both alternatives carry permanent cost against that unmeasured hazard: a most-recent-failure exemption is a second retention rule living beside the first and disagreeing with it at the edges, and an opt-out is a config field plus its `engine_scoped` tag plus a capability leaf every engine must declare a verdict for, forever, so a user can turn off a sweep whose cost nobody has quantified. **The OTHER gap stays open and is still documented as open:** a blueprint that fails and is never run again keeps its spill indefinitely, because nothing ever runs again for it to sweep — that one is meant to be answered by an explicit operator-invoked reclaim command, not by a clock and not by this rule. The `@pytest.mark.todo` stub that tracked the decision is gone, converted rather than deleted: a decided trade-off is not backlog, but an accepted behaviour that a future "helpful" exemption could silently undo is worth pinning, so `tests/test_executor/test_spill.py` now asserts that the MOST RECENT failure of a blueprint is reclaimed like any other once a later run succeeds, with a positive control (the same two-failure fixture minus the success row) proving the assertion can go red. No behaviour changed — the sweep does exactly what it did before. (`aqueduct/executor/spill.py`, `docs/specs.md`; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_spill.py`, `tests/test_backlog.py`)

### Removed
- **BREAKING: `aqueduct studio` and the `tui` extra are gone.** The interactive read-only TUI (`aqueduct/tui/`, Phase 67), the `studio` CLI command, and the `tui` optional extra have been removed outright — the feature was frozen since 2026-07-19 and the owner's call was to delete it rather than fix it. `pip install aqueduct-core[tui]` is no longer a valid extra; anyone with it in a requirements file or install script must drop it. The Streamlit-based `aqueduct dashboard` is unaffected and remains the one supported local, read-only observability viewer — it did not depend on `tui` and needs no migration. `aqueduct/stores/queries.py` (the shared read-time query layer `tui/data.py` re-exported from) stays; only the TUI's own package, its `studio` command guard in `aqueduct/cli/observability.py`, `tests/test_tui/`, `tests/test_cli/test_studio.py`, the `tui-tests` CI lane, and every doc/table row naming `studio`/`textual` are gone. (`aqueduct/tui/`, `aqueduct/cli/observability.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `scripts/lock.sh`, `requirements/ci-py311.txt`, `.github/workflows/test-suite.yml`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/cli_reference.md`, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `docs/specs.md`; tests: `tests/test_tui/`, `tests/test_cli/test_studio.py` removed)
- **Back-compat/defensive-scaffolding sweep — dead shims deleted, one live shim annotated with a removal condition it previously lacked.** Per-item verification (a mechanical "delete everything that says legacy/back-compat" pass was deliberately NOT taken — see per-item reasoning):
  - **`aqueduct/surveyor/blob_store.py` deleted.** Its own docstring already called it a "Back-compat shim (Phase 53)" over `stores/object_store.BlobStore`; `git grep` found zero production callers anywhere in `aqueduct/` — only test files still imported it as a convenience shortcut. Test call sites (`tests/test_redaction.py`, `tests/test_surveyor/test_provenance_integration.py`, `tests/test_surveyor/test_agent.py`) now construct `BlobStore(LocalBackend(...))` directly; the two tests in `tests/test_stores/test_object_store.py` that existed solely to prove the shim's own roundtrip (`test_legacy_shim_roundtrip`, `test_blob_marker_is_backcompat_with_legacy_shim`) collapsed into one test proving the CURRENT `local` backend's on-disk layout still matches the pre-Phase-53 marker format (the property that actually matters — no released DuckDB row gets orphaned). `docs/observability_guide.md`'s "Blob externalisation" section named the shim function directly (`blob_store.materialize()`); corrected to name the current `BlobStore.materialize()`.
  - **`load_executor()` removed from both `aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py` and `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py`.** Both were explicitly self-described ("kept for backward compatibility with Step 1 callers") and `git grep` across `aqueduct/`, `tests/`, and every `docs/*.md` found zero callers of either — Step 2's `get_protocol(engine).execute` registry lookup replaced them everywhere that mattered, and nothing was left depending on the direct accessor. `AGENTS.md` and `aqueduct/executor/capabilities.py`'s docstring (which cited `load_executor()` as an example of a lazy-import pattern) updated to point at what's actually still there (`_execute()`'s function-body-scoped `pyspark` import).
  - **Three `except ImportError` fallbacks for `sqlglot` removed from `aqueduct/compiler/lineage.py`** (`_extract_sql_lineage`, `referenced_function_names`, `referenced_function_names_in_expr`) — `sqlglot>=23.0.0` has been an unconditional base dependency (`pyproject.toml`) since this module's first commit (Phase 8); it has never been optional, so the guard could only ever fire on an already-broken install, at which point it silently degraded a compile-time feature (SQL column lineage, and the Phase 81 UDF-island-attribution gate that reuses the same parse) to an empty/`None` result with a DEBUG-level log line nobody sees by default — the "No silent no-ops" hazard, for a case that cannot legitimately occur. A genuine ImportError now propagates raw, same posture as every other base-dependency import in the module.
  - **`aqueduct/parser/parser.py`'s stale comment describing `path_keys.py`'s fallback as "a blanket union … shrink the fallback to `()` once every module type has an explicit entry" corrected** — that condition was already met (Pass C, 2026-08); `path_keys.py`'s own `_LEGACY_FALLBACK` has been `()` since, and its comment already says so. The parser-side comment just hadn't caught up.
  - **Verified and left alone, with the condition it needed added:** `aqueduct/surveyor/benchmark_store.py::_as_store`'s `Path | str | BenchmarkStore` acceptance IS a real shim (`BenchmarkStore` postdates Phase 52/47eefbc; every release through 1.2.1 only ever accepted a bare DuckDB path) — not dead scaffolding, so not deleted. It previously carried no removal condition (an open-ended "legacy DuckDB API" comment, exactly what the standing rule bans); now annotated with one: delete once `tests/test_benchmark_store.py` (the pre-Phase-52 suite exercising exactly this shape) is retired or migrated.
  - **Verified and deliberately left untouched, with reasons (the KNOWN SEEDS and others checked against this task):** the `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` chains in `aqueduct/surveyor/ddl.py` and `aqueduct/patch/index.py` are NOT defensive scaffolding for a schema no released version ever wrote — checked against every `2.0.x` git tag: each column these migrate in (`heal_attempts.tool_calls_json`/`chain_link`/`engine`, `failure_contexts.engine`, `healing_outcomes.engine`, `patch_index.engine`) was absent from the CREATE TABLE at the immediately-prior released tag and present at the tag that added it, i.e. every migration protects a real user upgrading between two real releases. `aqueduct/models.py` (the cross-layer re-export surface, pinned by `tests/test_models.py`) and the `cli/__init__.py` helper placement (dozens of live `mock.patch("aqueduct.cli._helper")` call sites across the test suite) were both re-confirmed live, not re-litigated. Also reviewed and found to be correctly-designed permanent default behavior, not compat scaffolding: `agent/toolbox.py`'s `_UNAVAILABLE_NO_SESSION` constant (live, used), `patch/preview.py`'s `spark_session` parameter name (a single live parameter, not a dual-shape accept — renaming it for cosmetic consistency touches 6+ files for zero functional gain), and the "backward-compatible default" field descriptions in `cli/hooks.py`/`parser/models.py`/`surveyor/webhook.py` (each describes the current, permanent semantics of an optional field, not a deprecated one). (`aqueduct/surveyor/blob_store.py` [deleted], `aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/capabilities.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/lineage.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/benchmark_store.py`, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `AGENTS.md`; tests: `tests/test_redaction.py`, `tests/test_surveyor/test_provenance_integration.py`, `tests/test_surveyor/test_agent.py`, `tests/test_stores/test_object_store.py`)
- **`version-matrix.yml`'s `unit-tests` job — a strict subset of `test-suite.yml`'s `coverage` job, gating nothing.** `unit-tests` ran `pytest -m "not spark and not integration and not agent"`; `coverage` runs `pytest --cov=aqueduct -m "not spark"` on the exact same trigger (`main` pushes + `workflow_dispatch`), under the identical committed lock (`requirements/ci-py311.txt`), on the same Python version (3.11) — and `"not spark"` positively selects everything `"not spark and not integration and not agent"` does, plus more. `version-matrix.yml`'s `needs:` appears exactly once (the `aggregate` job needs `compat`), so `unit-tests` gated nothing and could not fail fast for anyone. It also installed the `postgres`/`redis` extras only to immediately deselect the `integration`-marked tests that need them (`not integration`) — extras paid for with nothing exercising them. Verified no coverage loss before cutting: `coverage`'s extras (`dev,spark,llm,redis,stores,duckdb`) are a superset of `unit-tests`'s (`dev,spark,postgres,redis,duckdb` — `stores` already rolls up `postgres`+`redis`+`object-store`), and `tests/test_meta_ci.py`'s pre-merge-lane coverage guards (`_pre_merge_pytest_commands`) only ever scanned `test-suite.yml`, never `version-matrix.yml`, so this job was invisible to them either way. `tests/test_meta_ci.py::test_compat_promise_lanes_install_under_a_per_interpreter_lock` asserted `unit-tests`'s `PIP_CONSTRAINT` alongside `compat`'s — renamed to `test_compat_promise_lane_installs_under_a_per_interpreter_lock`, now asserting only `compat`'s (its actual remaining subject). (`.github/workflows/version-matrix.yml`, `tests/test_meta_ci.py`)
- **The 12 `agent_cascade_tier.field.*` capability rows on every engine — a per-engine question about something no engine can have an opinion on.** `agent.cascade` is the list of LLM tiers the healing loop escalates through on `stuck_signature` / `exhausted_attempts` / `deferred`. It is core and engine-invariant: which model answers the next healing turn has nothing to do with which engine runs the pipeline, and `aqueduct/config.py` already says so, tagging the field `engine_scoped: False` so `config_leaves.py` correctly emits no `config.agent.cascade` leaf. But `CascadeTierSchema` was also registered in `capability_leaves.py`'s `_SCHEMA_BLOCKS`, the walker that mechanically emits one `<block>.field.<name>` GRAMMAR leaf per field — so the same feature core had deliberately excluded from the framework came back in through the Blueprint door, as 12 rows per engine, all `supported`, on both shipped tables. The rows are gone from `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml` and `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml` (Spark 317 → 305 leaves, DuckDB 325 → 313; `docs/compatibility.md`'s generated matrix regenerated), and `_SCHEMA_BLOCKS` no longer lists the model. **No behaviour changes at all** — `CascadeTierSchema` itself stays exactly where it was, still validating the `agent.cascade:` block of `aqueduct.yml`; same config key, same tiers, same escalation. Only the capability-leaf registration is removed. They date from when the Blueprint `agent:` block accepted `cascade:` directly; making that block policy-only moved every connection field to `aqueduct.yml` only, and the rows outlived the move because the closure test compares each engine's table against THIS generator, not against Blueprint grammar — an unreachable block keeps matching perfectly, forever. That blind spot is what the new reachability guard closes: every `_SCHEMA_BLOCKS` entry must be reachable from `BlueprintSchema` by following pydantic field annotations, checked against the field graph rather than against the registry it validates, so the two can genuinely disagree. `CascadeTierSchema` was the only unreachable entry; the other eleven blocks all still hang off `BlueprintSchema`. (`aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `docs/compatibility.md`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_schema_block_reachability.py`)

### Fixed
- **`pip install aqueduct-core[all]` did not install DuckDB's numpy, so every DuckDB Python UDF raised `UDFError` on an `[all]` install.** The `all` aggregate listed `spark` but not `duckdb`, and the omission was invisible because `duckdb` and `sqlglot` are already BASE dependencies — DuckDB appeared to work, right up to the first `conn.create_function()` call. numpy is the extra's only real payload (DuckDB needs it internally for argument/result marshalling on every Python UDF, regardless of return type), so an `[all]` user got a working DuckDB engine that could not register a UDF. `all` now includes the `duckdb` leaf, which is where the two-axis extras policy always said an engine leaf belongs. Found by the release workflow, which is the only job in the repo that installs `[all]` — every Test Suite lane installs a narrow extra set, so no amount of green CI could have surfaced it. (`pyproject.toml`)
- **`tests/test_airflow.py::test_dagbag_import` broke on whatever Airflow CI resolved that day.** It passed `include_examples=False` to `DagBag(...)`, a kwarg newer Airflow no longer accepts, and `airflow = ["apache-airflow>=2.7"]` has no ceiling — so the test's fate depended on the release date rather than on the code. It now decides by SIGNATURE (`inspect.signature(DagBag.__init__)`) rather than by version string: a version include-list would need editing every time Airflow moves, which is the drift shape this repo bans, and the kwarg is incidental to the assertion (that a DAG importing `AqueductOperator` parses without import errors — a custom `dag_folder` pulls in no example DAGs either way). **The deeper finding is left as a queue item, not fixed here: the Test Suite lane that runs `tests/test_airflow.py` installs `.[dev,llm,redis]`, which contains no Airflow at all**, so all three `airflow`-marked tests have always self-skipped in CI and the release gate is the only place they have ever executed. (`tests/test_airflow.py`)
- **Four harness-side failures that all rendered as the model's fault — a clean exit reported as an error, an empty LLM response reported as malformed JSON, a truncated one reported as the same, and our own budget's abort reported as the model giving up.** Found by live smoke runs against a real endpoint (DeepSeek via `openai_compat`), and grouped because they are one bug family: at each of these points the harness knew exactly what had happened and threw the fact away, leaving the user to debug a prompt, a model, or a CLI that were all working correctly.

  **(1) Every subcommand `--help` and the bare `aqueduct` banner exited 2 with `✗ unexpected error: 0`.** `_AqueductCLIGroup.invoke` wraps the whole dispatch in a broad `except Exception` so nothing escapes as a bare traceback, re-raising the four things that are control flow rather than failure — `SystemExit`, `KeyboardInterrupt`, `click.exceptions.Abort`, `click.ClickException`. `click.exceptions.Exit` is in none of them, and it is the object click raises to request a SUCCESSFUL exit: its MRO is `Exit -> RuntimeError -> Exception`, so the net caught it, printed `str(Exit(0))` — the exit code the command ASKED for, rendered as error text, which is where the bare `0` came from — and returned `DATA_OR_RUNTIME`. `aqueduct --help` and `--version` looked clean only because click resolves the ROOT group's eager params before entering `Group.invoke` at all, which is exactly why the two smoke checks everyone runs missed it. `Exit` joins the re-raise tuple with a comment saying why it does not look like it belongs there. The fix is deliberately the class of bug and not `--help`: only two `ctx.exit(` sites exist in `aqueduct/cli/` (the net's own, and the bare-`aqueduct` banner) because every other command exits via `sys.exit`, so no real exit code was being clobbered yet — but the net rewrote ANY `Exit`, so the first `ctx.exit(HEAL_PENDING)` anyone wrote would have silently become 2.

  **(2) `content: ""` — an empty STRING — was not an empty response.** The non-streaming OpenAI-compatible path guarded with `if text is None`, so an empty string sailed through to the JSON parser, which reported `JSON parse error at line 1, column 1: Expecting value` above a blank "your output near the error" excerpt. That blames the model for malformed JSON when the model returned nothing at all; it burned a reprompt each time it happened and lost a scenario outright once. The streaming path thirty lines below had always guarded with `if not text` — the two paths disagreed about what an empty response is. Both now test falsy-or-whitespace, keep their messages distinguishable by path, and mean the same thing by "empty". The identical `is None` check in the tool-use loop's final turn is fixed in the same pass and routed through the same explanation rather than a second copy that would drift.

  **(3) The response says WHY it is empty, and nothing read it.** `finish_reason` had no reader anywhere in the codebase, and `message.reasoning_content` had one only on the streaming path. So a response TRUNCATED at the output-token limit (`finish_reason: "length"`) and a reasoning-only turn from a deepseek-r1/o-series model — two different causes with two different fixes, one of them not a prompt problem at all — both surfaced as the same generic "the model may have refused the request, hit a content filter, or been interrupted". The empty-content error now states which of the three was actually observed: truncation (naming the limit that was ACTUALLY SENT, read off the request payload rather than the `max_tokens` argument, because `provider_options` merges into the payload after `max_tokens` is set and therefore wins — reporting the argument would name a number the endpoint never saw — plus `-s agent.provider_options.max_tokens=N` as the override), a reasoning-only turn, or genuinely empty. **Reporting only.** No patch is ever parsed out of `reasoning_content`: the reasoning channel is not the answer channel, and mining it would be a behaviour change nobody asked for. No new default, no threshold, no knob — every number in the message is a value the request or the response already carried.

  **(4) A heal our own budget ended was reported as "the model failed to produce a valid patch after 1 attempt".** Two real runs of the same scenario: 84.1s over three attempts → PASS, then 140.6s over ONE attempt → FAIL, the second being a single slow call eating the whole `agent.budget.max_seconds` so no reprompt was possible. The tracker was not at fault and was not touched: `BudgetTracker` sets `budget_seconds_exceeded` on every seconds-axis path, `generate_agent_patch` returns it, `run_scenario` copies it onto `ScenarioResult`, `benchmark_store` persists it, and `aqueduct heal` / `aqueduct run` both print it. `aqueduct benchmark --format json` hand-builds its per-pair dict and simply never listed the key — so the one output a user actually reads showed `None` for every pair, PASS and FAIL alike, and a harness-side stop was indistinguishable from a model that could not do the job. The field is now emitted (as a bare string — `StopReason` is a `StrEnum`; `null` stays `null`, no invented axis). The run banner's LLM-call ceiling, which advertised `18 ceiling (max_reprompts=3)` and then permitted exactly one call per pair, now also names the seconds axis, so the number reads as a ceiling rather than a plan. No budget default changed and no knob was added.

  (`aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/agent/providers.py`, `aqueduct/cli/benchmark.py`, `docs/cli_reference.md`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_clean_exit.py`, `tests/test_agent/test_providers_empty_content.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_benchmark_stop_reason.py`)
- **`-s/--set` was the weakest input in the engine-config merge, not the strongest.** `--set` is documented as the top precedence layer (`--set > blueprint > aqueduct.yml > defaults`), and for ordinary config it is: the flag is overlaid onto the loaded `AqueductConfig` and wins. Engine/session config is not ordinary config. It has its own merge — `resolve_session_engine_config` layers the Blueprint's `engine.<name>:` block over the `aqueduct.yml` one, Blueprint winning — and `--set` was applied to the `aqueduct.yml` layer, i.e. UNDERNEATH the Blueprint. So `-s engine.spark.conf.spark.sql.shuffle.partitions=800` lost to a value a self-heal had written into the Blueprint months earlier, silently, with the flag reported nowhere: "explicit beats default" inverted for the single most explicit statement a user can make about a run. `--set` is now a genuine THIRD layer above the Blueprint (`aqueduct.yml` < Blueprint < `--set`), not a mutation of a lower one, and the order is stated once inside `resolve_session_engine_config` rather than re-implemented at any of the twelve sites that resolve a session config. It is safe for a flag to outrank a heal precisely because it is per-invocation and never written back to any file — it overrides a healed value for one run, it cannot undo one. **Where the layer is carried is the load-bearing design decision.** It rides on `AqueductConfig` as a `PrivateAttr` (never a schema field, so never an `aqueduct.yml` key, never a `config.*` capability leaf, never in `model_dump()`), written in exactly one place — `overrides.apply_to_model`, the one function every config `--set` already flows through. Every site that resolves a session config already threads `cfg`; an extra parameter would have had to be remembered at each of them, and a site that forgot it would have silently dropped the user's flag, which is the same silent-no-op class this change exists to fix. `session_config_fingerprint` needed no change at all and deliberately got none: the new layer lives inside the function whose output it hashes, so a session built with `--set` already fingerprints differently from one built without it — the invariant closed in `6fd353f` holds by construction rather than by a second, drift-prone term. A useful consequence falls out: a heal writing a Blueprint value the flag shadows produces the SAME fingerprint and correctly triggers no session rebuild, because the session's actual config did not change. Also generalizes the layer-1 shape from a hardcoded `if engine == "spark"` to the same structural "does this engine's block declare a free-form `conf` bag" question the Blueprint side already asks. (`aqueduct/executor/session_config.py`, `aqueduct/overrides.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`, `docs/specs.md` → Version 2.64, `docs/cli_reference.md`; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_session_config.py`)
- **`--set engine.spark.conf.spark.sql.shuffle.partitions=800` set a key named `spark` whose value was a dict.** `--set` splits its path on every dot, and `engine.spark.conf` is a `dict[str, Any]` whose own key names are themselves dotted — so the four trailing segments became a nested tree (`conf["spark"]["sql"]["shuffle"]["partitions"]`) instead of the one flat key the user meant. Because the field is free-form, pydantic accepted it without a word and the Spark session was configured with a garbage key. A silent wrong answer, found while fixing the precedence bug above and fixed in the same change rather than written up. The router now rejoins every segment past a free-form-dict boundary into a single dotted key. A dict of STRUCTURED entries (`stores.depots: dict[str, DepotMountConfig]`) is deliberately not such a boundary — there the segments after the dict really are a key followed by that model's own fields, and collapsing them would have broken paths that work today. (`aqueduct/overrides.py`, `docs/cli_reference.md`; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_session_config.py`)
- **The engine-config delta gate could not tell a redundant patch from one the user's own flag was blocking, and told the user the wrong thing.** Gate 1 refuses a `set_engine_config` write whose effective delta is empty, on the reasoning that the fix is a different patch — a different key or a different value. With `--set` now above the Blueprint, that branch is reachable for the first time from a source no Blueprint value can outrank, and there the advice is false: nothing the model writes can win against the flag, so the resolution belongs to the user. The gate still REFUSES (applying it would spend a heal attempt on a change this invocation cannot exhibit, and the in-loop retry runs under the same pin and fails the same way), but with a distinct message naming the exact `--set` path, the value it pins, and the two real ways forward. A patch that moves some keys and writes others the invocation pins still passes on the strength of the ones that move, with the pinned ones reported in the gate's `detail` and a new `cli_pinned` field rather than folded into a clean verdict — a partial no-op reported as a clean pass is the same lie the gate was built to stop. `aqueduct patch preview` accepts no `--set`, so its verdict is always measured with no pins; `cli_pinned` is emitted as `{}` there rather than omitted, so a consumer can tell "measured without pins" apart from an older report that lacks the field. (`aqueduct/patch/config_delta.py`, `aqueduct/executor/session_config.py`, `aqueduct/cli/patch.py`, `docs/specs.md`, `docs/cli_reference.md`; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_config_delta.py`)
- **`handoff.keep_on_failure` acquired disk and nothing ever gave it back.** Keeping a failed run's cross-engine spill was an acquisition event with no release event anywhere in the system: the orphan sweep exempts a spill on `run_records.status == 'error'`, that row never changes, and so the exemption never expires. Self-healing made the leak permanent rather than merely slow — a heal patches the Manifest, which changes the whole-Manifest hash that is baked into the spill's own directory path, so the kept spill becomes unreachable by every future run while still being exempted from every future sweep. Two deterministic release events now bound it. First, a successful `--resume` deletes the spill it consumed: `run_polyglot()` previously deleted only its OWN `run_id` directory, so the resumed-FROM directory — the one case where consumption is provable, because at least one island was skipped by reading it — was the single spill nothing in the system could ever reclaim. A FAILED resume still keeps it, since it remains resumable, which is the whole point of keeping it. Second, the sweep reclaims a kept-failure spill once a LATER run of the same blueprint has SUCCEEDED: a success means the failure is resolved and no rerun will resume from that spill again. **The shape of that second rule is the load-bearing part.** Time-based retention was rejected outright: the sweep only runs at the start of a run, so a wall-clock window would be an action-trigger wearing a fuzzy predicate, and no measurement exists that would justify a number of days — `finished_at` is read here to order two rows against each other and never as a duration, so no threshold, window, or `retention_days` knob ships. "Superseded by N later terminal runs" was rejected too: a multi-patch heal mints a terminal `run_records` row per iteration, so a count would delete the spill minutes after keeping it, while the heal that needs it is still running. And the accepted statuses are `success` AND `patched`, not `success` alone — `Surveyor.record()` stamps `patched` on the iteration that succeeded after a patch was applied, which is the single most common way an Aqueduct failure is resolved, so a `success`-only rule would have left every healed failure's spill unreleased and closed nothing. The status tuple is hoisted to `executor/models.py` beside `ExecutionStatus` and aliased by perf attribution, which had its own copy. `run_records` carries no `manifest_hash` column, so "unreachable because the hash moved" is not computable from the store; this rule does not need it. Two consequences are stated rather than papered over, in the code, in `docs/specs.md` and in the config field's own description: a blueprint that fails and is never run again keeps its spill indefinitely (that gap is meant to be closed by an explicit operator-invoked command, not by a clock), and a failure under active investigation loses its spill if an unrelated scheduled run of the same blueprint succeeds in between (that second case has since been RULED ON and accepted — see `### Changed` above). (`aqueduct/executor/spill.py`, `aqueduct/executor/orchestrator.py`, `aqueduct/executor/models.py`, `aqueduct/patch/perf_attribution.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`, `docs/specs.md` → Version 2.64; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_spill.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_orchestrator.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_orchestrator_e2e.py`, `tests/test_backlog.py`)
- **The engine-config delta gate decided things nothing recorded — it was the one gate absent from `patch_simulation`.** The lineage, sandbox and explain gates each write a row through `surveyor.record_patch_simulation`; the effective-engine-config gate shipped without one, so the fleet metric that reads that table (`stores/queries.py::gate_rejection_rates`, itself only recently fixed to read it at all) saw a gate that had never rejected anything and could not tell that state apart from a gate that does not exist. `_run_patch_gates_inline` now records it as a fourth row under `gate = 'engine_config'`, alongside the three it already wrote, with the same best-effort posture (a store failure logs at WARNING and never touches the loop). **Where it is evaluated is the load-bearing detail.** The gate is ENFORCED elsewhere — inside `_check_guardrails`, on every apply path — and enforcement did not move: `gates_passed` is unchanged, the returned tuple keeps its three-gate shape, and nothing here can refuse a patch. But the gate's failing outcome is a raised `PatchError`, not a returned result, and `_check_guardrails` raises the same class for `forbidden_ops`, `allowed_paths` and allowlist violations, which belong in `heal_attempts` instead. Passing a result down from the enforcing site would have left the refusal unrecordable without matching on message text — the exact thing the error taxonomy exists to prevent. Calling the delta gate directly at the recording site makes the attribution structural: only one check runs there, so any `PatchError` it raises is the effective-config refusal by construction. `duration_ms` is measured at that call site rather than added to `EngineConfigDeltaResult`, because the refusal path has no result object to carry it and the two outcomes must be timed the same way. Both existing gate-recording tests asserted `call_count == 3` and now assert the full ordered gate vocabulary. (`aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `docs/observability_guide.md`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_engine_config_gate_simulation.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_patch_gates.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_heal_apply_callback.py`)
- **Gate 1 was skipped entirely in the self-heal reprompt loop whenever a Blueprint declared no `agent.guardrails`.** Both `aqueduct run`'s and `aqueduct heal`'s in-loop apply callbacks short-circuited on an empty guardrails block before calling `_check_guardrails`, and the benchmark scenario replay did the same. But that function also enforces two checks that are not guardrail-gated at all — `set_engine_config`'s core allowlist and (now) the effective-config delta — so on the majority of Blueprints, which declare no guardrails, neither reached the model as reprompt feedback. Nothing unsafe applied (both checks still fired at the later real-apply site), but the model never saw the rejection and could not correct it within the same heal, and the benchmark scored config patches as PASS that production refuses — the exact over-reporting `_try_apply_patch`'s guardrail call was added to end. All three now call Gate 1 unconditionally; the benchmark's `violated_guardrails` field still reports *guardrail* violations specifically, so a non-guardrail refusal no longer gets mislabelled as one.
- **A `set_engine_config` patch is never replayed from the heal cache.** The cache replays a stored patch body when an identical error signature recurs, but engine config is environment-specific: the right `shuffle.partitions` on a 10-node cluster is wrong on a 3-node one, and the signature captures the failure, not the cluster it ran on. A cached candidate containing that op now always falls through to the LLM, announced the same way an unparseable or retired-op candidate already is — a silently discarded candidate would be indistinguishable from a cache miss. The stored record is untouched and remains audit history. (`aqueduct/agent/memory.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_heal_cache.py`)
- **Gate 3's `pass` detail for a config-only patch read as though the sandbox had validated the fix.** It had not: a small local sample proves the session built and rows flowed under the patched config, and can never reproduce the cluster-scale OOM or shuffle failure such a patch usually targets. The detail now says so explicitly and names the full re-run as what actually validates efficacy. Labelling only — `status` and `gates_passed` are unchanged, and the same string flows into `patch_simulation`, so the dashboard inherits it. (`aqueduct/patch/preview.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/patch.py`, `docs/observability_guide.md`; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview_gate3.py`)
- **Docs claimed a heal-triggered rerun could resume from a handoff spill. It never can.** Two independent mechanisms prevent it: a heal patches the Manifest, which changes the whole-Manifest hash that is baked into the spill path (measured — a patch touching only a downstream island still changes it), and the CLI passes no resume id once a patch has been applied. What is actually true is narrower: spill resume applies to a manual `aqueduct run --resume <run_id>` after a plain failure with no Blueprint edit in between.
