Corrected in `aqueduct/executor/spill.py`, `AGENTS.md` and `docs/specs.md`, which now also state the asymmetry between the two resume mechanisms plainly — module checkpoints reuse across a changed Manifest with a warning, handoff spills silently do not reuse at all — since a reader previously could not learn that without reading both code paths side by side. Also fixed `orchestrator.py`'s docstring pointer to `_island_is_resumable`, a function that does not exist. (`aqueduct/executor/spill.py`, `aqueduct/executor/orchestrator.py`, `AGENTS.md`, `docs/specs.md` → Version 2.56)
- **DuckDB's `--resume` never checked whether the Manifest had changed, and neither engine's warning about it was suppressible.** `duckdb_/executor.py` wrote `_manifest_hash` into every checkpoint directory but nothing ever read it back, so a resume silently reused checkpoints written by a DIFFERENT Manifest with no signal at all — Spark at least warned. DuckDB now compares the stored hash and warns-then-proceeds on a mismatch, matching Spark's existing permissive policy rather than inventing a second one. Separately, that warning fired on both engines through a bare `logger.warning`, so `warnings.suppress` / `--suppress-warning` could never reach it despite the message being hand-formatted to look like a suppressible rule, and it never reached the persisted run record. Both engines now route `runtime_resume_hash_changed` through `aqueduct.warnings.emit()` with the run's suppress list, the same path session-startup warnings already use. (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/executor.py`; tests: `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_executor.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_executor_orchestration.py`)
- **The heal loop re-executed the original Manifest on a session left behind by a failed config patch, and persisted the resulting phantom failure.** Rebuilding the session before a patch retry (previous entry) fixed only the forward direction. The outer heal loop re-executes the ORIGINAL, unpatched Manifest at the top of each iteration to observe the current failure — so a rejected `set_engine_config` patch (say a bad `memory_limit` guess) left that baseline running under the failed patch's config, failing with an error the user does not have. That phantom failure became the run's error SIGNATURE, and signatures are persisted to the blob store and `patch_index` and replayed on later runs, so the loop manufactured durable bad data and then asked the LLM to diagnose a bug that did not exist. Config healing makes failed config patches routine, since the model is guessing values. The execution funnel now enforces one invariant — never execute a Manifest on a session built from a different Manifest — by comparing a fingerprint of the resolved session config (`session_config_fingerprint`) before every single-engine execution and rebuilding only on mismatch. That is a deterministic equality check on derived config rather than a list of ops that count, so a future session-affecting op cannot slip past it, and it costs nothing when a patch touched no session config. The narrower post-patch rebuild helper is removed: two mechanisms that could disagree became one. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/executor/session_config.py`, `AGENTS.md`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_run_heal_session_rebuild.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_session_config.py`)
- **`gate_rejection_rates` never read a single `patch_simulation` row.** Two defects stacked. Its predicate was `WHERE status != 'passed'`, but no gate has ever written `'passed'` — the vocabulary is `pass`/`warn`/`fail`/`skip` (+ `not_applicable`) — so every row matched. Then the shared row-accumulation loop, which expects `(label, count)` pairs from the `heal_attempts` fallback query, received `(gate, status)` and added the status STRING to an integer total, raising `TypeError` on the first row of any non-empty result. An `except Exception: continue` swallowed it, so the function silently fell through to `heal_attempts.gate_that_rejected` (a narrower, different signal) or returned `{}`. The query is now `SELECT gate, COUNT(*) ... WHERE status = 'fail' GROUP BY gate`, and the docstring states the definition it implements: only `fail` is a rejection — `warn` does not block, `skip` is treated as acceptance by the gate caller, and `not_applicable` means the gate had nothing to check. (`aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `docs/observability_guide.md`; tests: `tests/test_stores/test_queries.py`)
- **A heal retry ran the patched Manifest on the pre-patch engine session, then blamed the patch for failing.** The single-engine `aqueduct run` path built its session once, before the first execution, and the heal loop's re-execute helper closed over that same object — while Spark applies conf only at session-build time (nothing calls `spark.conf.set` at execute time). So no engine-config change a patch made reached the retry that judged it. The sandbox gate meanwhile builds its own fresh session from the patched blueprint, so Gate 3 passed, the retry failed on unchanged config, and `_stage_failed_patch` filed a correct fix as a failure. Every heal retry now closes the current session and rebuilds it from the patched Manifest, re-resolving through `resolve_session_engine_config`. Unconditional by design: rebuilding only for config-carrying ops would be an include-list of op types, so any future op affecting session state would silently miss it. The rebuild genuinely stops the old session first, because `SparkSession.builder.getOrCreate()` returns the active session and silently ignores most new conf. The polyglot path is unaffected — `run_polyglot()` already opens a session per island on every call. `ExecutorProtocol` is untouched. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_run_heal_session_rebuild.py`)
- **The lineage gate (Gate 2) reported `pass` for patches it never checked.** `run_lineage_gate` returned `LineageGateResult`'s default `status="pass"` unchanged for any patch whose operations touch zero modules — which is every engine-config patch, since `set_spark_config` carries only `key`/`value` and `touched_module_ids()` therefore always returns `[]` for it. In `patch_simulation` and on every surface rendering it (`aqueduct patch preview` text and JSON, the dashboard's gate table), that was indistinguishable from "checked the downstream column consumers and found nothing broken". Gate 2 now reports a distinct `not_applicable` status carrying a `detail` reason ("no module-lineage surface for this patch's ops"), set explicitly on that path rather than falling through a dataclass default. Informational only: patch acceptance was already decided solely by the sandbox and explain gates (`gates_passed = sandbox_res.status in ("pass", "skip")`), so no patch outcome changes. (`aqueduct/patch/preview.py`, `aqueduct/cli/patch.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `docs/specs.md` §8.7 → Version 2.52; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview.py`)
- **Gallery snippet `23_table_first` failed on the DuckDB engine — a snippet setup gap, not a DuckDB defect.** Both `version-matrix.yml` snippet lanes (`snippets`, `snippets-lts`) run every gallery snippet against both registered engines; this one failed `aqueduct-run` on DuckDB with `[src] table 'demo_table' not found or unreadable: Catalog Error: Table with name demo_table does not exist!`, reproduced locally. Root cause: `populate_tables.py` only ever seeded `demo_table` into Spark's local Derby-backed Hive metastore — nothing created it anywhere DuckDB's engine could see. This is not a DuckDB engine bug (`con.table()` correctly raised `CatalogException` for a table that genuinely doesn't exist in its catalog) and not a case for declaring the snippet's feature `unsupported` on DuckDB either (`feature.table_addressing` is genuinely `supported` there — DuckDB has a real catalog and Pass G2 built the `table:` read/write mapping onto it).

  Fixed the snippet: `populate_tables.py` now also seeds a persistent DuckDB catalog file (`demo.duckdb`) with the same `demo_table` contents, unconditionally (the harness runs the populate step before knowing/caring which engine `aqueduct run` will target); `aqueduct.yml` adds `engine.duckdb.database_path: demo.duckdb` so DuckDB's session resolves it there instead of a per-process `:memory:` connection that can't survive into the separate `aqueduct run` process; `inspect_results.py` now reads the DuckDB catalog file for the `demo_output` table when the Spark-shaped `spark-warehouse/demo_output` directory isn't present. Verified end-to-end on both engines locally (populate → run → inspect, 10 rows each). (`gallery/snippets/23_table_first/populate_tables.py`, `aqueduct.yml`, `inspect_results.py`, `.gitignore`, `README.md`)
- **`tests/test_executor/test_executor_session.py`'s conf-restore fixture used `spark.conf.getAll`, a PySpark **4.0+**-only `RuntimeConfig` property — broke every test in the file on the Legacy compat combo (python 3.12 / pyspark 3.5.8 / delta 3.3.0) with `AttributeError: 'RuntimeConfig' object has no attribute 'getAll'`.** The autouse fixture (added by the timezone-leak fix, see the "Cross-engine remediation audit triage" entry below) snapshots every Spark conf key before/after each test so no test can leak a mutated default (e.g. `spark.sql.session.timeZone`) into `tests/test_executor/test_incremental.py`'s order-dependent watermark assertions; `getAll` was added to the Python API surface only in pyspark 4.0 (confirmed by reading `python/pyspark/sql/conf.py` at tag `v3.5.8` — the class exposes only `get`/`set`/`unset`/`isModifiable`), so the Legacy lane's 3.5.8 install never had it.

  Replaced with a version-portable snapshot: the bare SQL `SET` command (`spark.sql("SET")`) is a core SQL command that predates Spark 3.5 and reads from the identical JVM-side key set PySpark 4.x's `getAll` reads (verified by execution on pyspark 4.1.1 — zero-diff key sets between the two). `SET`'s rows pass through `SQLConf.redactOptions`, though, masking any key matching the redaction regex (e.g. a key containing `password`/`secret`/`token`) to a literal `"*********(redacted)"` string — verified by execution — which would have silently corrupted restore for a future test mutating a sensitively-named key. `SET` is therefore used only to enumerate the portable key universe; each value is re-read with `spark.conf.get(key)` (present, unredacted, on both 3.5 and 4.x). No hardcoded key list — the fixture still snapshots every key, keeping the guarantee the timezone-leak fix introduced it for. (`tests/test_executor/test_executor_session.py`)
- **`Module.spillway:` — documented, parsed, validated, remapped through Arcade expansion, and serialized into every compiled Manifest for months, but read by NO executor on either engine — now actually does something (owner-approved).** Spillway ROUTING itself already worked end to end on both engines (an Assert rule with `on_fail: quarantine`, or a Channel `spillway_condition`, plus an explicit `{from: X, to: Y, port: spillway}` edge); the orphaned piece was specifically the module-level `spillway: <target>` FIELD — a wrong value was rejected at parse time (`parser/graph.py::validate_spillway_targets`) while a correct one silently did nothing at run time, the exact shape the CHANGELOG's own "Deferred" note above this entry flagged and left unfixed pending a product decision on how to wire it without duplicating the edge mechanism.

  Fixed as authoring SUGAR, not a second runtime path: `aqueduct/compiler/wirer.py::desugar_module_spillway` expands `spillway: <target>` into a real `Edge(from_id=module.id, to_id=target, port="spillway")` — the ONE mechanism every engine's executor already reads — wired into `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`'s pipeline right after Arcade expansion (step 4) and before spillway/Assert-quarantine validation (step 5). Compile time, not parse time, matching the codebase's own existing precedent for this exact class of expansion — linear-edge sugar (`compiler.py` step 3.8) already desugars an omitted `edges:` into real edges at compile time, and Arcade expansion is also compile-time — and for a concrete reason beyond precedent: `compiler/expander.py` already remaps `m.spillway` per Arcade instance (`id_map.get(m.spillway)`, the same treatment `attach_to` gets) specifically so a spillway field survives expansion correctly namespaced; desugaring AFTER expansion lets one simple function handle a top-level module and an Arcade-nested one identically, with no Arcade-awareness of its own — desugaring before expansion would have missed every Arcade-nested spillway field outright (those modules don't exist in the flat list yet) and needed a second, duplicated pass inside `expander.py`.

  Conflict handling: a module carrying BOTH the `spillway:` field and an explicit spillway edge to the SAME target is idempotent (no duplicate edge — proven against a pre-existing test, `test_islands.py::test_compile_spillway_crossing_island_raises`, that already exercised exactly this combination and continues to pass unchanged); to a DIFFERENT target is a `CompileError` naming both (never silently pick one). `Module.spillway` is cleared to `None` on every module this touches once desugared, whether idempotent or newly-added, so the compiled Manifest carries exactly one encoding of the fact — the edge — never a second, possibly-stale one. The existing `spillway_port_mismatch` compiler warning needed no code change to keep working correctly on the sugar path: it already reads `manifest.edges`, which now includes the sugar-produced edge indistinguishably from a hand-authored one (verified both directions — condition + sugar-supplied edge stays silent; sugar-supplied edge with no condition still warns, proving sugar creates no blind spot for a genuine mismatch). `parser/graph.py::validate_spillway_targets` (target-exists check) and `lint.py`'s reachability rule (which already counts `m.spillway` directly, pre-compile) are both unchanged and stay correct.

  Guarded against silently rotting again the same way: every test asserts the desugared EDGE actually exists in the compiled Manifest, never merely that the field parsed — the gap that let this rot for months in the first place. (`aqueduct/compiler/wirer.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `docs/specs.md` §4.3 → Version 2.51, `SKILL.md`; tests: `tests/test_compiler/test_spillway_sugar.py` [new], `tests/test_compiler/test_compiler_warnings.py`)
- **`aqueduct run` on a duckdb-engine Blueprint crashed with a silent `exit 1` on a base install with no `pyspark` — regardless of `deployment.engine`.** `aqueduct/cli/run.py`'s `run()` command unconditionally did `from aqueduct.executor import ExecuteError` before `deployment.engine` was even resolved; that name was resolved lazily via `aqueduct.executor.__init__.__getattr__`, which imported it from `aqueduct.executor.spark.executor` — dragging `pyspark` into EVERY run, including a pure-DuckDB one, even though `aqueduct.executor.spark.executor`'s own docstring says the lazy resolution exists precisely so the package works "without a Spark installation." `duckdb` is a BASE dependency (`pyproject.toml`); `pyspark` is the optional `[spark]` extra, so a plain `pip install aqueduct-core` with `deployment.engine: duckdb` set could never run at all. Compounding this: Spark's and DuckDB's executors each separately defined their OWN `ExecuteError` class (same name, both `AqueductError` subclasses, but two distinct types that never compared equal) — so even once the import stopped crashing, the CLI's `except ExecuteError` handler would only ever have caught SPARK's failures; a DuckDB setup failure of the identical shape (a cycle, a bad `--from`/`--to`) would have passed through uncaught. Triage against `docs/failure_taxonomy.md`: class #4, "Partial-coverage wiring" — the pyspark-optional lazy-resolution mechanism `aqueduct/executor/__init__.py` documents was wired for `path_keys.py`/`models.py` but not for its own `ExecuteError` name; also shares class #6's shape ("guard added, sibling paths forgotten" — DuckDB's parallel `ExecuteError` was never unified with Spark's).

  Fixed by giving `ExecuteError` an engine-agnostic home: `aqueduct/errors.py` (the exception-taxonomy root already holding `ParseError`/`CompileError`, one per layer — `AqueductError`'s own docstring names "parser, compiler, executor" as the expected layer classes, and `ExecuteError` was the missing one) rather than `aqueduct/executor/models.py` (data models only — `ExecutionResult`/`ModuleResult` — no exception class lives there anywhere in the codebase, by precedent). Both `aqueduct/executor/spark/executor.py` and `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py` now import this ONE class instead of defining their own, so both engines raise the same type; `aqueduct/executor/__init__.py::__getattr__` resolves `ExecuteError` directly from `aqueduct.errors` (never via `aqueduct.executor.spark`), so `from aqueduct.executor import ExecuteError` never touches pyspark on any engine. `aqueduct/executor/orchestrator.py`'s polyglot failure handler, which previously had to catch the broader `AqueductError` because "there is no shared `ExecuteError` base to import here without naming an engine" (its own prior comment), now catches `ExecuteError` specifically.

  Also added a CLI-wide safety net (`aqueduct/cli/__init__.py::_AqueductCLIGroup`, a `click.Group` subclass) so this bug's *symptom* — an unexpected exception escaping a command produces a genuinely silent `exit 1` with ZERO output under Click's own `CliRunner` (it records the exception but never prints it; a real terminal gets a raw, unstyled traceback instead) — cannot recur for a DIFFERENT root cause. Any exception that isn't `ClickException`/`Abort`/`SystemExit`/`KeyboardInterrupt` now gets a clean `click.echo` message and `exit_codes.DATA_OR_RUNTIME`; an `ImportError` naming a known optional-engine-extra module (`pyspark`/`delta`/`py4j` → `spark`) gets a specific "install the matching extra" hint, the same shape `aqueduct/config.py`'s existing "not a registered engine" message already uses. This surfaced one pre-existing real gap of the exact same class while testing the net: `aqueduct test` on a malformed test YAML file raised a raw `yaml.YAMLError` with no handler at all (`aqueduct/cli/project.py::test_cmd`); now caught explicitly and mapped to `CONFIG_ERROR` with a real message, rather than silently relying on the net's generic `DATA_OR_RUNTIME` fallback for a case that has an obviously more correct, specific answer. (`aqueduct/errors.py`, `aqueduct/executor/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/executor.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py`, `aqueduct/executor/orchestrator.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/project.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_run_duckdb_no_pyspark.py` [new — fresh-subprocess `sys.meta_path` pyspark blocker, real DuckDB run through `CliRunner`], `tests/test_cli/test_cli_test.py`)
- **Cross-engine remediation audit triage (2026-08) — cheap-model audit reports verified and the confirmed real defects fixed.** `.dev/AUDITS/pending/` held 29 per-module reports from a high-recall/low-precision audit skill; each PROVEN finding below cleared a verification gate (read the cited code, apply AGENTS.md's documented neutralizers, write a concrete failure scenario, confirm still true at HEAD) before being fixed. Most findings across the 8 reports triaged were hallucinated or already-fixed; the following survived verification:
  - DuckDB's Channel `spillway_condition` branch produced a relation with no `_aq_error_*` columns at all, unlike Spark's (which stamps 4 via `withColumn`); a typed spillway edge (`edge.error_types`) filtering on `_aq_error_type` against a Channel-sourced spillway hit a DuckDB Binder error on a blueprint that runs fine on Spark. Fixed by stamping the same 4 columns; two hardcoded `"_aq_error_type"` literal filters now use the shared constant. (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py`)
  - `duckdb_/egress.py`'s docstring claimed `append` is unsupported; it is implemented (non-atomic union-all-by-name rewrite) and declared `supported`. Corrected.
  - `@aq.*` Tier 1 dispatch let a bare Python `TypeError` escape whenever a registered function was called with the wrong arg count (e.g. `@aq.date.offset()` with no args) — now converts to `CompileError`. `_java_to_strftime` mapped only `yyyy/MM/dd/yy`; `HH/mm/ss` were added (a common `'yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss'` pattern previously left the literal text `"HH:mm:ss"` embedded in the output). (`aqueduct/compiler/runtime.py`)
  - Macro `_parse_call_args` used `args_str.split(",")`, so a quoted value containing a comma (`cols="a,b"`) silently kept only `"a"`. Macro placeholder substitution was gated behind `if args_str.strip()`, so calling a parameterized macro with no args (`{{ macros.name }}`) left literal `"{{ key }}"` text in the output instead of raising the documented error. (`aqueduct/compiler/macros.py`)
  - The compile-time quarantine gate's two hand-listed rule-type sets covered only 8 of `AssertRuleType`'s 10 members — `schema_match`/`spillway_rate` silently passed `on_fail=quarantine` with no spillway-edge requirement. Restructured as an exclude-list (a small closed row-quarantine-capable set; everything else defaults to the safe "not quarantine-capable" bucket). (`aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`)
  - `expander.py` mutated another module's frozen `config` dict in place when rewiring a Funnel's `inputs` list after Arcade expansion; now uses `dataclasses.replace()`. Disabled (`enabled: false`) Probe/Assert modules were still validated by compile steps 5/5.5, so you could not disable your way out of a missing-`attach_to` or missing-spillway-edge error on a module that would never run. (`aqueduct/compiler/wirer.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`)
  - The documented java/scala UDF `class:` contract was broken end-to-end: the schema field's alias dumps to `"class"`, but the Spark executor read `entry.get("class_name")` — a key the dump never produces — so every `class:`-declared UDF hit `"'entry' is required"` at runtime despite compiling cleanly. Relative `jar:` paths were never anchored to the Blueprint directory despite specs.md's documented claim (`udf_registry` is a top-level list, not a module `config:` block, so the existing anchoring pass never reached it). (`aqueduct/executor/spark/udf.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/executor/path_keys.py`)
  - `parser.parse()` caught only `FileNotFoundError`/`yaml.YAMLError`; a directory path, an unreadable file, or a non-UTF8 file escaped as a raw builtin instead of `ParseError`. `AgentConfig.to_dict()` dropped `heal_on_errors`/`never_heal_errors` from its `guardrails` sub-dict (invisible in `report --json` / manifest storage, though the live LLM prompt reads the raw dataclass and was unaffected). (`aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`)
  - `set_spark_config`/`replace_context_value` patch ops wrote raw (unquoted) strings; ruamel (the dump side, YAML 1.2) does not treat `"on"/"yes"/"off"/"no"` as reserved words, but `apply.py`'s step-5 verification re-parses via PyYAML (YAML 1.1), which does — a heal setting `spark.eventLog.enabled: "on"` applied cleanly and every subsequent parse silently read a Python bool. Both ops now force-quote string values, matching the existing guard on `set_module_config_key`. (`aqueduct/patch/operations.py`)
  - `aqueduct.yml.template` claimed `target: databricks` is rejected at config-load, grouping it with the genuinely-unimplemented `emr`/`dataproc`; the config accepts it and `deploy/databricks.py` consumes it. Corrected, and added the previously-undocumented `deployment.databricks:` example block. `_validate_store_backends`'s fail-fast SDK check covered only observability and the default depot mount — an extra named depot mount or the benchmark store's own backend loaded cleanly and only died with a bare `ImportError` at first real use, mid-run. Two config-load warnings used raw `warnings.warn()` with a hand-embedded rule-id prefix instead of `aqueduct.warnings.emit()`, so `warnings.suppress:`/`--suppress-warning` silently did nothing for either. `agent.api_key`'s docstring and its insecure-literal warning both claimed the value "is redacted from logs and LLM payloads" — true only for `@aq.secret()`-resolved values; a `${ENV_VAR}` value or a plaintext literal was never registered for redaction at all. Now registered unconditionally after config load. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`)
  - The healing-transcript renderer hardcoded `"temperature=0.9"` on stuck-detection escalation while `loop.py` actually applies `providers._ESCALATION_TEMPERATURE` (0.8) — now references the real constant. Cascade's cascade-wide-token-cap-exhausted path mutated a PRIOR tier's `AgentPatchResult.stop_reason` in place and returned it, leaving `.patch` populated with that tier's supposedly-discarded diagnosis — a caller could see `stop_reason=budget_tokens_exceeded` while still holding a patch from an unrelated escalation. Now builds a fresh result via `dataclasses.replace()`. `_STOP_PHRASE` was missing `progress_stalled` (one of `StopReason`'s 8 members), rendering the raw enum string instead of a phrase. `agent/regression_artifact.py` imported a private symbol (`_TESTABLE_TYPES`) directly out of `executor/spark/test_runner.py` — a private cross-module import that also violated the "no engine import in `aqueduct/agent/`" rule; hoisted to the existing engine-agnostic `executor/models.py`. (`aqueduct/agent/transcript.py`, `aqueduct/agent/cascade.py`, `aqueduct/agent/regression_artifact.py`, `aqueduct/executor/models.py`)
  - `FailureContext.root_exception`/`object_name` were assigned straight from the structured error extractor with no redaction scrub, unlike every other comparable field (`error_message`, `stack_trace`, `manifest_json`, `provenance_json`, `blueprint_source_yaml`) — a secret embedded in an exception message (a DSN, a token) persisted unredacted to `failure_contexts`. `scenario.py`'s `config_contains` numeric grader's type-check condition algebraically reduced to "only fires for bools," so a genuine int/float `expected_val` (the common case) always fell through to substring comparison — `config_contains: {retries: 1}` passed against an ACTUAL of 11, 21, or 100001 — and a bool `expected_val` used loose `!=`, letting `1 == True` satisfy a boolean expectation. Both fixed; both are silent-wrong-answer defects in a benchmark grader. (`aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/scenario.py`)
  - `doctor._host_port` called `urlparse(url).port` unguarded, which raises `ValueError` for a non-numeric or out-of-range port — every doctor check's contract is "return a `CheckResult`, never raise," and the CLI has no try/except around them. `check_blueprint_sources` recursed into Arcade `ref` includes with no visited-set or depth guard (unlike `compiler/expander.py`'s own Arcade expansion, capped at 10 for the identical reason) — a cyclic include hit `RecursionError`, aborting the whole doctor run and losing every other check's results. (`aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`)
  - `duckdb_/udf.py`: a plain `pip install aqueduct-core` user writing a Python UDF on DuckDB got DuckDB's raw `"'numpy' is required for this operation, but it wasn't installed"` text with no indication it's a packaging gap (numpy ships with the `[duckdb]` extra, not the base install) rather than a bug in their UDF. Detects the missing-numpy case and names the fix.
  - Independent of the audit: `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.py`'s and AGENTS.md's leaf/supported-row counts were stale (206/189/261 vs. the actual 320/303/319) — corrected and flagged as a snapshot, not a contract; `docs/specs.md` §8.7's gate numbering (five gates) contradicted AGENTS.md and the code itself (four numbered gates, compile-check unnumbered) — corrected to match the code, which is load-bearing in test/file names (`test_patch_preview_gate3.py` == sandbox == Gate 3).

  Deferred items previously listed here have since ALL been fixed, none by product decision after all: the `--store-dir` write/read split-brain was not a decision at all — docs/specs.md §10.4.1 already defines `--store-dir` as a routing base and says `report`/`runs` aggregate across `<base>/*/observability.db`, so the read side was simply out of spec; the phantom Channel-level `udfs:` key has been removed from SKILL.md/specs.md, since UDFs register session-wide from `udf_registry` and were never scoped per Channel; and `Module.spillway:` — this passage's last remaining entry, at the time genuinely deferred as "wiring it up would duplicate the edge mechanism" — is fixed below (owner-approved) as sugar over that SAME edge mechanism rather than a second one, closing the concern that deferred it.
- **`schema_hint`/`schema_match` type comparisons now resolve through the type hub, with numeric-family widening (Pass G2).** Both were already routed through `aqueduct/typehub.py` for the DECLARED side (Phase 80 work package 3), but compared the result to the actual engine-inferred type LITERALLY — which broke whenever two engines' own CSV type inference genuinely disagreed on width for the same data: Spark's `inferSchema` picks the narrowest candidate that fits (`IntegerType` for small values), DuckDB's CSV sniffer only ever proposes `BIGINT` for any whole-number column regardless of value range (verified against a real `DuckDBPyConnection`). An author who wrote `quantity: integer` — Spark's own inferred type for that column — got a `schema_hint type mismatch` on the DuckDB lane even though the column is genuinely integer-valued on both engines; `30_ingress_schema_hints` hit this on every DuckDB-lane run. New `aqueduct.typehub.widens_to(hint, actual)`: true when `actual` is the SAME fixed-width numeric family (`tinyint`/`smallint`/`int`/`bigint`, `float`/`double`) as `hint` and at least as wide — one-directional (a hint WIDER than the actual type still fails), and scoped to `schema_hint`/`schema_match` VALIDATION only (a Channel `cast` still casts to the exact requested type; this never touches a value, only a type-name comparison). `duckdb_/type_render.py` gains `parse_duckdb_scalar_type` (a narrow, DRY reverse mapping off the existing `_SCALAR` table — deliberately excludes `TIMESTAMP`/composites, which stay on the pre-existing literal-comparison path for the same ambiguity reasons `_normalize_actual_type` already documented) and `schema_type_matches` (exact-then-widened comparison), now the single implementation both `duckdb_/ingress.py`'s `_validate_schema_hint` and `duckdb_/assert_.py`'s `_check_schema_match` call, replacing their previously near-identical duplicated normalize/compare logic. `24_assert_types_full`'s `order_id: bigint` + explicit `CAST(order_id AS BIGINT)` (added in an earlier pass specifically to paper over this same defect) is no longer needed — reverted to the original `order_id: int` with no CAST, which now validates on both engines through the widening rule instead of forcing DuckDB's wider inference to match Spark's via a manual cast. Verified end to end on both engines for both snippets (real `execute()` runs, not just compile). (`aqueduct/typehub.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/type_render.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/ingress.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/assert_.py`, `gallery/snippets/24_assert_types_full/blueprint.yml`, `docs/specs.md` §4.4/§9 → Version 2.48; tests: `tests/test_typehub/test_typehub.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_executor.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_assert.py`)
- **UDF `deterministic` was unread by Spark — the same Blueprint field behaved differently per engine, worse than uniformly dead (Pass G1).** `UdfSchema.deterministic` (default `true`) was a documented field with zero consumers before a recent pass; DuckDB now honours it (mapped onto `side_effects`, inverted), but Spark's `udf.py` still ignored it entirely, so `deterministic: false` silently did nothing there while genuinely preventing constant-folding/caching on DuckDB — a Blueprint authored once against this field got different runtime guarantees depending on `deployment.engine`. Wired into `aqueduct/executor/spark/udf.py::_register_python_udf`: `deterministic: false` builds the UDF via `pyspark.sql.functions.udf(fn, return_type).asNondeterministic()` before registration — Spark's own documented mechanism for telling its optimiser not to constant-fold, cache, or re-order calls to the UDF — applied whether `fn` is a plain callable or already a pre-built Spark UDF object (a factory-produced UDF that was built WITHOUT `asNondeterministic()` is forced nondeterministic too). The default (`true`/unset) path is unchanged — still a plain callable + `return_type` string passed to `spark.udf.register()`, verified byte-identical via a regression test. Both engines' `udf.field.deterministic` capability leaf stays `supported` — now honestly, on both. (`aqueduct/executor/spark/udf.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/udf.py` [docstring correction — it previously described Spark's non-consumption as a permanent fact], `docs/specs.md` UDF Registry field table → Version 2.47; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_udf.py`)
- **BREAKING — Egress `repartition`/`coalesce` were dead knobs on Spark, the reference engine (Pass G1).** Both fields were documented (`parser/schema.py`) and declared `supported` on both engines' `capabilities.yml`, but grepping every reader in the repo found ZERO — `aqueduct/executor/spark/egress.py` never read either key. The only consumers were the `file_format_no_repartition`/`perf_delta_append_no_partition` compiler warnings, which SUGGEST setting them ("Add `coalesce: 1` or `repartition: N` to control output file count") — so a user who followed that advice made the warning disappear (both keys count toward "has partitioning configured") while the actual output was completely unchanged: wrong advice and a silent no-op, together. Implemented for real in `aqueduct/executor/spark/egress.py::_apply_output_partitioning`, applied before every write path (including `mode: merge`/`overwrite_partitions`/`depot`/`custom` — a single call site at the top of `write_egress`): `repartition: N` → `df.repartition(n)` (full shuffle, can raise or lower file count); `coalesce: N` → `df.coalesce(n)` (merge, no shuffle); both accept `true` as shorthand for `1`, matching the common `coalesce: 1` authoring pattern the warnings themselves suggest — the schema types both fields `int | bool`, never a column name (that's the unrelated Channel `op: repartition`/`op: coalesce` contract). **DuckDB got a real, examined verdict for both leaves instead of an unread `supported` claim of its own:** `coalesce` is genuinely honoured (`duckdb_/egress.py::_copy_options`) — a non-partitioned `COPY ... TO '<path>'` already writes exactly one file by default (measured against a real connection), so `coalesce` truthy now pins that explicitly via `PER_THREAD_OUTPUT false` rather than leaving it to an undocumented default; stays a no-op-but-already-satisfied case when `partition_by` is also set (DuckDB already writes one file per partition value, and `PER_THREAD_OUTPUT` cannot even combine with `PARTITION_BY` — verified, raises `NotImplementedException`). `repartition` flips from `supported` to honestly `unsupported`: DuckDB has no shuffle/partition-count concept for a `COPY` target at all (`PARTITION_BY` groups by column VALUE, not by a target file count) — no code change could make it real without inventing a bucketing feature out of scope for this pass. No gallery snippet sets either field today, so no snippet's output file count changes as a result of this fix. **Visible side effect:** any Blueprint that already set `repartition`/`coalesce` on a Spark Egress expecting them to control output file count — they previously did nothing — will now see the output file count actually change. (`aqueduct/executor/spark/egress.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/egress.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `docs/specs.md` §4.4 Egress section → Version 2.46, `docs/compatibility.md` regenerated; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_executor_egress.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_executor.py`)
- **BREAKING — a custom Assert rule (`type: custom`) whose own code was broken used to fail OPEN, on both engines (Pass G1).** Two situations — no `fn:` configured, or `fn(df)`/`fn(rel)` itself raising (a bug, a bad import, a wrong-engine API call) — unconditionally logged a warning and let the pipeline continue as if the rule had PASSED, regardless of the rule's own `on_fail:`. A quality gate whose entire job is stopping bad data must not silently permit it through just because the gate's own code broke. Both paths now route through the SAME `on_fail` action the rule's author already declared, exactly like a rule that evaluated and genuinely failed: `abort` aborts, `warn` warns and continues (same as before, but now via the rule-failure path rather than an unconditional log line), `webhook` fires, `trigger_agent` defers to the healing loop, and `quarantine` falls back to the existing "aggregate rule, no row filter available" warn behavior (there is nothing to quarantine when the rule never ran) — no new `on_fail` vocabulary. The two failure reasons keep distinct identifiers (`custom_missing_fn`/`custom_error`, both surfaced in `AssertError.rule_id` and in the warning message text) so the cause stays diagnosable from a generic `custom` rule failure. **Visible side effect:** an existing Blueprint with `on_fail: abort` (or `trigger_agent`) on a `type: custom` rule whose `fn:` is broken will now abort (or trigger healing) instead of silently warning and passing data through — this is the point of the fix, but it changes behavior for anyone relying on the old silent-pass. (`aqueduct/executor/spark/assert_.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/assert_.py`, `docs/specs.md` §4.4 Assert section → Version 2.45; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_executor_assert.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_assert.py`)
- **`15_regulator_timeout`'s Probe signal used `type: sql`, which is not a recognized Probe signal type on either engine — found while acceptance-testing Pass F's DuckDB Probe implementation.** Neither engine's dispatcher has ever had an `sql` signal type (the built-ins are `schema_snapshot`/`row_count_estimate`/`null_rates`/`sample_rows`/`value_distribution`/`distinct_count`/`data_freshness`/`partition_stats`/`threshold`/`custom`); an unrecognized `type:` hits the generic "unknown signal type" warning and is skipped — no `passed` key is ever written, so `evaluate_regulator()` defaults the gate OPEN, directly contradicting the snippet's own comment ("This signal is always False, which closes the gate") and README. Changed `type: sql` to `type: threshold` — the built-in designed exactly for a boolean gate expression — with no other change to the `expr:`/`id:` fields. Verified on the DuckDB lane: the Regulator now genuinely polls for the full `timeout_seconds` and then skips the downstream Egress (`on_block: skip`), matching the documented behavior; it had previously silently passed through regardless of the signal, on both engines, since the defect predates any engine-specific behavior. (`gallery/snippets/15_regulator_timeout/blueprint.yml`)
- **`ingress.format.depot` was a phantom capability leaf no engine could ever legitimately mark `supported` (Pass E item 1).** `format: depot` is a real, dedicated code path on the EGRESS side only (`depot.put(key, value)`), but the only depot READ mechanism anywhere in the product is the compile-time `@aq.depot.get()` function (`aqueduct/compiler/runtime.py`) — engine-independent, resolved before any engine session exists. Neither Spark's nor DuckDB's `ingress.py` has ever had a `format=depot` dispatch branch, and `IngressConfigSchema` has no `key` field to name which KV entry such a read would even fetch. `ingress.format.depot`'s curation violated the module docstring's own stated rule for `INGRESS_FORMATS`/`EGRESS_FORMATS` ("only formats with a DEDICATED engine code path are curated leaves") — it was added on an incorrect assumption of read/write symmetry with the Egress side. Spark's declared `supported` verdict for it was therefore false: the only test backing it (`test_ingress_pathless_formats_no_error`) asserts nothing depot-specific and swallows any exception. Removed `"depot"` from `INGRESS_FORMATS` (`aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`) and the now-orphaned `ingress.format.depot` row from both engines' `capabilities.yml`, rather than flipping it to a verdict that would have shipped a capability that structurally cannot exist without inventing new Blueprint grammar. (`aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `docs/compatibility.md`)
- **`null_rate` — a schema-legal Assert rule type — was reachable in Spark's dispatcher only through a raw string literal, never a member of its own `AssertRuleType` enum (Pass E item 3, "Constants, not literals").** `parser.schema.AssertRuleSchema.type`'s Literal always included `"null_rate"` (load-bearing for gallery snippet `12_assert_null_sampling`), and DuckDB's own `AssertRuleType` (added in the prior pass) already had a `NULL_RATE` member — only Spark's enum, the one the raw comparisons were dispatching against in the SAME file, had lagged. Added `NULL_RATE = "null_rate"` to `aqueduct/executor/spark/assert_.py::AssertRuleType` and routed both comparison sites (the `null_rules` filter and the `_handle_fail` call) through it. Pure refactor — no behaviour change, verified by `12_assert_null_sampling` running green before and after. Audited every other `AssertRuleType` member for the same raw-string-comparison pattern (none found — `null_rate` was the only gap) and confirmed no OTHER schema-legal rule type is missing from either engine's enum: both now list exactly the same 10 members as the schema Literal. `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py` also compares against `"null_rate"` as a raw string (its `_AGG_NO_QUARANTINE` quarantine-validation set) — left as-is: the Compiler layer must not import an Executor-layer enum (4-layer boundary), and `parser.schema.AssertRuleSchema.type`'s Literal — not either engine's `AssertRuleType` — is the correct single source of truth at that layer; hoisting a shared engine-agnostic constant (the `channel_ops.py`/`path_keys.py` precedent) would resolve this cleanly but is a separate, larger decision not made unilaterally here. (`aqueduct/executor/spark/assert_.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/assert_.py` [docstring correction only])
- **The patch-preview sandbox gate (Gate 4) had no access to `AqueductConfig` at all — a DuckDB sandbox replay never saw `engine.duckdb.*` (memory_limit, threads, database_path, extension_repository, the S3 secret names) or any httpfs/secrets wiring (Phase 82 remediation).** `patch/preview.py::run_sandbox_gate` built its owned sandbox session's `SessionSpec.engine_config` from `sandboxed_manifest.spark_config` unconditionally — a Spark-only field that is always `{}` for a non-Spark target — so a sandbox replay against `engine="duckdb"` ran under a completely different session shape than a real `aqueduct run` on the same `aqueduct.yml` would use: it could pass a patch that would fail in the real run (or vice versa), and against a remote `s3://` root it could fail for a reason unrelated to the patch (no credentials, since the S3 secret names never reached the session). Fix: `run_sandbox_gate` now takes a required `cfg: AqueductConfig` parameter and resolves its owned session's `engine_config`/`engine_options` through the SAME `aqueduct.executor.session_config.resolve_session_engine_config`/`session_secrets_options` helpers `aqueduct run`'s single-engine and polyglot paths already use (extracted from `aqueduct/cli/run.py` into a new engine-agnostic module — `aqueduct/executor/protocol.py` is a pure `ExecutorProtocol` *contract* declaration and these are session-config helpers, not part of that contract). Every caller — `aqueduct/cli/patch.py`'s `patch preview --sandbox` and `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`'s `_run_patch_gates_inline` (itself now also requiring `cfg`, threaded from its three call sites in `aqueduct/cli/run.py`) — already had a resolved `cfg` in scope. Verified with a test that intercepts the REGISTERED `DUCKDB.make_session` (bypassing `ExecutorProtocol`'s frozen-dataclass `__setattr__` via `object.__setattr__`) and queries the real DuckDB connection's own `current_setting('memory_limit')`/`current_setting('threads')` after a sandbox replay — proving the session was actually BUILT with a non-default `engine.duckdb.*` value, not merely that an argument was forwarded. (`aqueduct/executor/session_config.py` [new], `aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, `aqueduct/patch/preview.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/patch.py`, `docs/specs.md` §8.7; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview_sandbox_engine.py` [new proof test], `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview_gate3.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_preview_sandbox_explain.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_patch_gates.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_sandbox_mode.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_sandbox_default.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_heal_apply_callback.py`)
- **DuckDB's incremental-Channel watermark was a silent no-op — `${ctx._watermark}` reached the SQL layer unsubstituted (Pass A item 3, cross-engine remediation).** Root cause: `duckdb_/executor.py` had ZERO `_watermark` handling at all — `materialize`/`watermark_column` being freeform `config:` keys (fixed above) meant nothing on the DuckDB side ever read them, so the literal token `${ctx._watermark}` reached `duckdb_/channel.py`'s sqlglot transpile unsubstituted and raised a confusing `ParseError: Expected AS after CAST`, with the real cause documented as a known Stage-A gap in this module's own docstring instead of fixed (banned twice over per AGENTS.md: "No silent no-ops" + "Fix the defect you found; do not write it up"). Implemented DuckDB's executor-level substitution to mirror Spark's `read -> substitute -> run -> persist-new-max` cycle exactly, in DuckDB's own idiom: the Depot's persisted `MAX(watermark_column)` (or the `'1900-01-01 00:00:00'` sentinel on the first run) is substituted, quoted, for `${ctx._watermark}` in the Channel's `config.query` before `execute_channel` runs; after a successful downstream Egress write, the new `MAX(watermark_column)` is computed with a SQL aggregate over the WRITTEN output file (not the upstream DAG, no double-scan) and persisted back to the Depot — same Depot-only contract as Spark (the Depot is engine-independent), same runtime `[runtime_incremental_overwrite]`/`[runtime_watermark_compute_failed]`/`[runtime_watermark_no_depot]` warning vocabulary. Verified end-to-end on `19_depot_incremental`: two consecutive DuckDB runs against the same 5-row source file process 5 rows (full scan, no prior watermark) then 0 new rows (watermark already past every source row) — real incremental behaviour, not a lucky compile-clean no-op. The module docstring's stale "not implemented" caveat is removed (it documented a defect that no longer exists). (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py`; tests: `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_incremental.py` [new, 7 cases including a real two-run incremental-behaviour proof])
- **`44_dag_cycle_detection` had no `populate_data.py` — the one gallery snippet without a data generator (Pass A item 1, cross-engine remediation).** `gallery/**/data/` is git-ignored by design and every snippet regenerates its own input at run time; `44_dag_cycle_detection`'s `data/nodes.csv` only ever existed as an untracked local file, so it silently vanished once `run_snippets.sh` started actually running `dag/blueprint_fixed.yml` for real (the entry below). Added `gallery/snippets/44_dag_cycle_detection/populate_data.py` (same convention as every other snippet) generating the 3-row `id,value` fixture the snippet's own README documents, and fixed `run_snippets.sh`'s `run_dag_cycle_snippet` special case, which — unlike the generic per-snippet flow — never invoked any `populate*.py` step at all. Replaced the `xfail(strict=True)` regression stub in `tests/test_backlog.py` (which asserted a committed fixture file that was never supposed to exist) with a real test exercising the generator. (`gallery/snippets/44_dag_cycle_detection/populate_data.py` [new], `scripts/run_snippets.sh`; tests: `tests/test_gallery.py` [new test], `tests/test_backlog.py` [stub removed])
- **`44_dag_cycle_detection` was fully skipped by `run_snippets.sh` under a comment that misdescribed why, and its fixed blueprint had a real path bug.** The skip comment claimed the snippet needed a live external service or was incompatible with a one-blueprint-per-snippet assumption — neither was true: `dag/blueprint.yml` is SUPPOSED to fail (it demonstrates compile-time cycle detection), so skipping it tested nothing. It's now run as an expected-failure assertion (`run_dag_cycle_snippet` in `scripts/run_snippets.sh`): asserts a non-zero exit AND that the output names the cycle (`"Cycle detected in module graph"`), then runs `dag/blueprint_fixed.yml` and expects it to pass. Found while wiring this up: `dag/blueprint_fixed.yml`'s Egress `path: data/output/result.parquet` (line 41) resolves against the blueprint's own directory (`dag/`, per `Manifest.base_dir`) while its Ingress `path: ../data/nodes.csv` (line 19) reads from the snippet root's `data/` — so the egress wrote to `dag/data/output/` while the README documents one root-level `data/`. Fixed to `../data/output/result.parquet`; verified via `parse()` that both paths now resolve under the snippet root on both engines. (`gallery/snippets/44_dag_cycle_detection/dag/blueprint_fixed.yml`, `scripts/run_snippets.sh`)
- **`12_assert_null_sampling`'s null-rate assertion could legitimately pass or fail run to run — an unseeded data generator, not a real bug in the assertion itself.** `populate_data.py` used Python's `random` module with no seed, so the ~5% true null rate it generated varied enough between runs that a 50%-sample check against a 6% max threshold sometimes tripped. Seeded the generator (fixed seed + 20,000 rows, up from 1,000 unseeded) so the sample's observed rate stays in a tight, well-clear-of-threshold band; verified empirically (300 repeated Spark `.sample()` draws against the new fixture stayed within 4.3%-5.2%, versus the old 1,000-row fixture failing the 6% threshold in ~40% of repeated draws) and with 4 consecutive real `aqueduct run` executions, all passing. No retry, tolerance, or "expected either way" state added — the deterministic data is what makes the assert deterministic. Also fixed `inspect_results.py`'s "(threshold was 50%)" label, which named the sample fraction, not the actual 6% max threshold. (`gallery/snippets/12_assert_null_sampling/populate_data.py`, `gallery/snippets/12_assert_null_sampling/inspect_results.py`)
- **Seven `inspect_results.py` gallery scripts assumed Spark's directory-of-part-files output shape and silently under-reported (or missed entirely) DuckDB's single-file output.** `11_spillway_channel`, `13_assert_freshness`, `14_assert_row_quarantine`, and `40_error_types_quality` globbed `part-*.csv` for a CSV spillway/quarantine output — a DuckDB-written single CSV file matches no `part-*.csv` glob, so the old code silently rendered an empty table instead of erroring (verified live on DuckDB: `11_spillway_channel`'s rejected-rows table showed 0 rows with the old code, 2 with the fix). `23_table_first`, `28_delta_merge_retry`, and `38_partition_io` (DuckDB-SQL-based scripts) gained the same either-shape handling for completeness, though `23`/`28` currently always classify `UNSUPPORTED` on DuckDB (`feature.table_addressing`/`egress.format.delta`) so the fix is not yet exercised there. Verified the general claim that plain `pd.read_parquet(path)` already handles both shapes transparently via pyarrow's dataset layer for the other 36 snippets — true, no changes needed. (`gallery/snippets/11_spillway_channel/inspect_results.py`, `gallery/snippets/13_assert_freshness/inspect_results.py`, `gallery/snippets/14_assert_row_quarantine/inspect_results.py`, `gallery/snippets/23_table_first/inspect_results.py`, `gallery/snippets/28_delta_merge_retry/inspect_results.py`, `gallery/snippets/38_partition_io/inspect_results.py`, `gallery/snippets/40_error_types_quality/inspect_results.py`)
- **`38_partition_io`'s Channel SQL failed on DuckDB with a real cross-engine SQL portability bug, found while running the new DuckDB harness lane.** `SUBSTRING(date, 1, 7)` implicitly relies on Spark's lenient DATE->string coercion; DuckDB's `SUBSTRING` has no such implicit cast and raises a Binder error. Fixed by casting explicitly — `CAST(date AS STRING)`, not `VARCHAR` (Spark's parser rejects a bare, unlengthed `VARCHAR` with `DATATYPE_MISSING_SIZE`; DuckDB accepts `STRING` as a `VARCHAR` alias, and both engines accept it). Verified passing on both engines after the fix. (`gallery/snippets/38_partition_io/blueprint.yml`)
- **A hub `timestamp_tz` column crossing the Spark write side of a cross-engine handoff silently lost its timezone-awareness — no error, a naive value on the DuckDB read side.** Cause: Spark's own default for `spark.sql.parquet.outputTimestampType` is `INT96`, a legacy Hive-interop encoding whose Parquet files carry no logical-type annotation distinguishing an instant-aware timestamp from a naive one, so a hub `timestamp_tz` column (Spark's plain `timestamp`) written under that default read back on DuckDB as a naive `TIMESTAMP` rather than `TIMESTAMPTZ`. Fix: `make_spark_session` (`aqueduct/executor/spark/session.py`) now sets `spark.sql.parquet.outputTimestampType=TIMESTAMP_MICROS` as Aqueduct's own default, once, at session creation — never toggled around an individual write, since every connected component in `--parallel` mode shares one SparkSession across threads and a toggle-and-restore would make a user's own Egress output encoding depend on thread timing. An explicit `engine.spark.conf.spark.sql.parquet.outputTimestampType` value still wins over this default. **Visible side effect:** this changes the on-disk Parquet encoding of any `timestamp` column a Spark Egress module writes, from `INT96` to `TIMESTAMP_MICROS` — values are unchanged, and `INT96` is deprecated in the Parquet spec (Spark's own docs recommend `TIMESTAMP_MICROS`), but existing pipelines will see the new encoding in their output files. `timestamp_ntz` was never affected (Spark always writes it with a modern, correctly-annotated logical type), and the reverse direction (DuckDB → Spark, either timestamp variant) was never affected. (`aqueduct/executor/spark/session.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.3/§10.9; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_executor_session.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_orchestrator_e2e.py`)
- **The ISSUE-026 class ('NoneType' object has no attribute 'sc') fixed structurally, not by another opt-in patch.** pyspark's `SparkContext` is a process-wide singleton and the test suite shares one session-scoped `spark` fixture across the whole run; teardown was previously prevented only by an OPT-IN convention — `stop_spark_session`'s `AQ_TESTING` guard (`aqueduct/executor/spark/session.py`) — that protects exactly one call path. `ExecutorProtocol.close_session` (`aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py`) performs a RAW `session.stop()` by design (it owns teardown for a real `aqueduct run`), and the inline sandbox gate (`_run_patch_gates_inline` → `run_sandbox_gate`) reaches it when `spark_session` isn't passed — under pytest that session IS the shared fixture, so it silently died for every test running afterward, order-dependently. `tests/conftest.py` gains an autouse, session-scoped fixture that no-ops `SparkContext.stop()` for the whole pytest session (restored at teardown; guarded so pyspark-absent lanes still collect) — this blocks every route structurally, including any future one, instead of requiring each new call site to remember the `AQ_TESTING` convention. No production code changed. Verified with a throwaway 3-test reproduction (primed the shared `spark` fixture, ran the raw-stop path via `run_sandbox_gate` with no `spark_session`, then reused the fixture): reproduced the exact `AttributeError: 'NoneType' object has no attribute 'sc'` before the fix, clean pass after. (`tests/conftest.py`)
- **`tests/test_cli/test_lineage.py` moved to `tests/test_compiler/test_lineage.py`.** It tests `aqueduct/compiler/lineage.py`, but the `compiler` CI path filter runs only `pytest tests/test_compiler/` — a compiler-lineage change never ran its own test pre-merge. `git mv`, no content changes needed (all fixtures already used `tmp_path`); 18/18 pass from the new location. (`tests/test_compiler/test_lineage.py`)
- **Two `except Exception` sites gain the justification comment AGENTS.md's "over-broad except" rule requires.** `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py::check_java`'s pyspark-version nudge is best-effort and additive on top of an already-valid Java-version result, so pyspark being absent or its version string not parsing is safe to swallow silently. `aqueduct/stores/queries.py::failure_context`'s inner tier (a pre-migration-schema retry without `manifest_json`/`provenance_json`) can't narrow to the expected missing-column/-table error without importing both optional backend drivers (duckdb and psycopg2) just to name their error classes, since this read layer is deliberately backend-agnostic; the comment says so and explains why also swallowing a genuine connection error here is acceptable (the function already documents "None if absent" as a normal outcome). (`aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`)
- **AGENTS.md's TODOs Memory Rule pointed at `TODOs.md` as if it were a repo file — it isn't; it lives in the user's Claude memory directory.** Fixed the pointer to the real path. A new `seed_ts` fixture (`tests/conftest.py`) gives store-fixture tests a one-line way to seed a relative timestamp (`seed_ts(days=-2)`) instead of a hardcoded calendar date, which can silently age out of a `now()`-relative query window (`report --trend`'s 30-day default, `count_recent_heal_attempts`) the way `test_report_trend[postgres]` did once in CI (see the incident note in `tests/test_stores/test_backend_conformance.py`); `tests/test_cli/test_report_trend.py` now seeds through it. (`tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_report_trend.py`, `AGENTS.md`)
- **Five false `hint:` reasons in DuckDB's `capabilities.yml` corrected (httpfs-work hint audit) — `hint:` strings render VERBATIM into `docs/compatibility.md`, so a wrong one is a published false statement, and nothing had ever checked their accuracy.** Two were given as known examples going in and three more were found by auditing every remaining hint against the actual code or a real DuckDB connection, not by reasoning from the verdict:
  - `feature.python_udf` claimed "DuckDB Python UDFs are vectorized (operate on Arrow batches)". **False** — `conn.create_function(name, fn, params, ret)` defaults to NATIVE, row-at-a-time execution matching Spark's semantics (measured: a per-row call log shows one call per input row; vectorized is opt-in via `type='arrow'`). Verdict unchanged (`unsupported` — nothing implemented or tested); reason rewritten to state what actually blocks it (no registry, deterministic flag, or error-handling convention built yet).
  - `feature.table_addressing` claimed "there is no catalog to resolve a bare `table:` name against". **False** — DuckDB reports a real catalog (`memory.main`, `system.main`/`information_schema`/`pg_catalog`, plus whatever `ATTACH` adds), a genuine three-part `catalog.schema.table` namespace. Verdict unchanged; reason rewritten to name the actual gap (no mapping IMPLEMENTED, not the absence of a catalog) — `tests/test_capabilities/test_table_addressing.py` updated (it asserted the old, false hint text verbatim).
  - `egress.format.depot`/`ingress.format.depot` claimed "Requires the httpfs extension ... Not implemented" — **false**, found while investigating which leaves the httpfs task should flip: `format: depot` writes/reads a single KV entry via the `DepotStore` PYTHON interface (`depot.put`/`depot.get`), never routed through this engine's SQL/relation layer at all — httpfs is unrelated even when the Depot's own backend happens to be S3. The real gap is simply no `format=depot` dispatch branch implemented (same category as `jdbc`/`kafka`/`custom` — no handler, not a missing extension). Direct consequence: the httpfs task's Part 1d instruction ("flip the verdicts for leaves whose hints say httpfs is required") turned out to apply to ZERO leaves once verified — these two were mischaracterized, not actually httpfs-gated, so no verdict changed as a result of shipping httpfs.
  - `feature.metrics_boundary` and `config.metrics.use_observe` both claimed "module metrics are collected... [unconditionally/directly from relation execution]". **False** — `aqueduct/executor/models.py`'s own module docstring states this engine's Stage A executor writes NO per-module metrics at all outside the Handoff module; `ModuleResult` carries no row/byte fields either. Verdicts unchanged (both correctly `unsupported`); reasons rewritten to state there is no existing per-module metrics path for either flag to configure, rather than claiming an always-on alternative that doesn't exist.
  - `feature.parallel_mode` claimed "a DuckDB connection is thread-safe for concurrent read queries". **False as stated** — measured: two threads calling `.sql()` on the SAME `DuckDBPyConnection` object concurrently raise `Invalid Input Error: Attempting to execute an unsuccessful or closed pending query result`; a per-thread `con.cursor()` IS safe for concurrent reads, but that is real, un-built wiring, not a free property. Verdict unchanged (`unsupported`); reason corrected to state the actual (safe-with-cursors) requirement.

  Judgement on mechanical guarding (asked explicitly): hint TEXT accuracy is a claim about real engine behavior (does a UDF registry batch, does a connection support concurrency, does a format have a dispatch handler) and cannot be cheaply checked by a grep/lint — a checker would need to encode the same domain ground-truth the audit itself required, and per AGENTS.md's "measure the hazard before building the guard" a truth-table needing constant updates as the implementation changes rots into a rubber stamp faster than it catches drift. No guard built; this remains a judgement pass, repeatable on demand. (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `docs/compatibility.md`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_table_addressing.py`)
- **`test-suite.yml` had zero pre-merge coverage for the DuckDB engine, the Arrow type hub, and the deploy package.** `tests/test_executor_duckdb/` (a shipped engine's test suite), `tests/test_typehub/`, and `tests/test_deploy/` were reachable only through the main-only `coverage` job (`github.ref == 'refs/heads/main' || workflow_dispatch`), so a break in any of them was caught AFTER merge, never before — the same bug class as the compat-matrix engine gap fixed in 2.0.5, just in the other CI file. Added `duckdb-tests`, `typehub-tests`, and `deploy-tests` jobs (same shape as `parser-tests`: shared pip cache key, `scripts/ci-summary.py` step summary, `--junitxml`, the `|| github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'` guard) plus matching `changes` path filters, and added `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.py` to the existing `capabilities` path filter (previously only listed the Spark engine's file). `tests/test_meta_ci.py` gained two structural guards so this class of gap can't recur silently: `test_every_registered_engine_has_a_pre_merge_lane_in_test_suite_yml` (every engine from `load_engines()`/`CAPABILITY_REGISTRY` must have a lane whose `if:` isn't gated solely on `main`/`workflow_dispatch`) and `test_no_test_directory_is_absent_from_every_pre_merge_lane` (every `tests/test_*/` directory must appear in some pre-merge lane, or be named in an explicit `_MAIN_ONLY_TEST_DIR_ALLOWLIST` with a reason — `tests/test_dashboard/` is the one intentional entry, gated on the `dashboard` extra).
- **All 7 `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_udf.py` cases failed in CI with a raw DuckDB error, passing only locally where numpy happened to already be installed.** `con.create_function()` — the only way `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/udf.py` registers a Python UDF — requires numpy internally, unconditionally: reproduced with numpy import-blocked against a real `DuckDBPyConnection`, the failure fires for every `return_type` and both `type="native"`/`type="arrow"` execution modes, so no alternative type mapping avoids it. numpy is not implied by the base `duckdb` dependency (its wheel declares no runtime requirements) and is not reliably pulled in by `pyspark` either (numpy sits behind pyspark's own `ml`/`mllib`/`sql` extras, not its base install). Declared `numpy>=1.21` on the existing `duckdb` extra (`pyproject.toml`) rather than inventing a new feature-named extra — the extra previously only re-stated two already-base dependencies for documentation purposes, so this is its first real addition. Every CI lane that exercises a DuckDB Python UDF but wasn't installing `[duckdb]` gained it: `duckdb-tests`/`coverage` (`test-suite.yml`), `unit-tests`/`compat`/`snippets`/`snippets-lts` (`version-matrix.yml`). `scripts/lock.sh` gained `--extra duckdb` for both `requirements/ci-py311.txt` and the `requirements/compat-py3.NN.txt` loop; regenerated and committed. Verified in a from-scratch venv with numpy absent beforehand: `pip install -e ".[dev]"` (no `duckdb` extra) reproduces the exact 7 failures; `pip install -e ".[dev,duckdb]"` pulls numpy transitively and all 7 pass. (`pyproject.toml`, `scripts/lock.sh`, `requirements/ci-py311.txt`, `requirements/compat-py3.11.txt`, `requirements/compat-py3.12.txt`, `requirements/compat-py3.13.txt`, `.github/workflows/test-suite.yml`, `.github/workflows/version-matrix.yml`, `docs/compatibility.md`)
- **`test_hudi_roundtrip_with_maintenance`/`test_iceberg_roundtrip_with_maintenance` failed to parse — Egress `config.output_columns` is a phantom key nothing reads.** `output_columns` is absent from `EgressConfigSchema`, and grepping `aqueduct/executor/` for it finds nothing — the only other hits in the tree are an unrelated `_output_columns_by_module` helper in `patch/preview.py` and an unrelated CLI test name. It was a leftover from the old free-form `config:` dict, which `extra="forbid"` on the per-module-type schema split now correctly rejects (`ParseError: Extra inputs are not permitted`); these two fixtures were its last users. No `gallery/**` blueprint references it either. Removed the key from both fixtures rather than adding it to the schema, which would have recreated the exact silent-no-op the schema split exists to prevent. (`tests/test_executor/test_executor_lakehouse.py`)
- **`tests/test_executor/test_incremental.py`'s 3 watermark-string assertions were order-dependent, failing only when the full `tests/test_executor/` suite ran — root cause found and fixed, not papered over.** Bisected to `tests/test_executor/test_executor_session.py`: its tests call `make_spark_session()` directly (to unit-test the factory's own config-application behavior against fresh app names), but Spark's `builder.getOrCreate()` creates a SparkContext once per JVM — once the session-scoped `spark` fixture has created the shared session, every later `make_spark_session(other_name, spark_config)` call reuses that SAME session, and Spark applies the `spark.sql.*` runtime confs from it directly onto the live session (`"Using an existing Spark session; only runtime SQL configurations will take effect"` — a genuine Spark warning, confirmed, not assumed). `TestSessionTimezone::test_universal_timezone_applied_when_no_native_override` sets `spark.sql.session.timeZone` to `"America/New_York"` this way and nothing restored it afterward, so it stayed changed for the REST of the pytest session — the exact mechanism `test_incremental.py`'s `MAX(ts)`-vs-literal-string watermark assertions depend on, since a TIMESTAMP's display string is computed against whatever `spark.sql.session.timeZone` happens to be at read time. Reproduced deterministically: running just `test_executor_session.py` then `test_incremental.py` fails the same 3 cases; running `test_incremental.py` alone passes. This is a genuine, fixable test-hygiene leak — not a shared-session design limitation — so the fix is a `_restore_shared_session_conf` autouse fixture in `test_executor_session.py` that snapshots `spark.conf.getAll` before each test and restores every changed/added key afterward (unsetting a newly-introduced key, restoring a changed one to its prior value; a handful of static configs like `spark.driver.memory` are on Spark's own runtime-immutable denylist and raise `CANNOT_MODIFY_CONFIG` on `.set()`/`.unset()` — caught and skipped, since a key Spark itself refuses to change post-startup cannot have leaked a *different* value to a later test either). No test reordering, no `@pytest.mark.order`, no flaky marker. Verified: the reproduction pair now passes, and the full `tests/test_executor/` suite (578 spark-marked tests, `-m spark -p no:randomly`) passes clean in one run — the same 3 cases that failed before the fix. (`tests/test_executor/test_executor_session.py`)
- **Error-taxonomy enforcement sweep — the "user-reachable errors raise an `AqueductError` subclass" rule (AGENTS.md, landed 2026-07-31) had never been applied to the code.** Audited every raw `raise ValueError|RuntimeError|TypeError|KeyError|OSError|NotImplementedError` in `aqueduct/` (~50 sites) plus the store/deploy driver boundaries the rule calls out by name. New `StoreConnectionError` (`aqueduct/stores/base.py`, sibling to the existing `BackendUnsupportedError`) covers a store backend that cannot be reached or used:
  - `stores/postgres.py::_get_pool`: `psycopg2.OperationalError` propagated raw when Postgres is unreachable (the rule's own cited seed instance); its missing-driver `ImportError` was equally unwrapped. Both now `StoreConnectionError`.
  - `stores/redis_.py`: same missing-driver `ImportError` fix. `RedisDepotStore.kv_put` — unlike `kv_get`/`kv_delete`, which already degrade-and-log by design — had NO exception handling at all, so a dropped/refused connection let a raw `redis.exceptions.*` escape on a write; a write failure must be surfaced, never silently degraded, so it now raises `StoreConnectionError` instead.
  - `stores/duckdb_.py::_connect_with_retry`: a non-lock `duckdb.connect()` failure (corrupted file, permissions) hit a bare re-raise of whatever DuckDB exception type; the lock-retry-exhausted path raised a bare `RuntimeError`. Both now `StoreConnectionError`.
  - `stores/object_store.py::FsspecBackend`: missing-`fsspec` `ImportError`; `__init__`'s `url_to_fs` and `put`/`get` each dispatch to a different cloud SDK (boto3/botocore for s3, google-cloud for gcs, azure for adls) with no shared narrow exception type across them, so the broad catch is commented and justified per AGENTS.md's over-broad-except rule. `get` still lets `FileNotFoundError` through unwrapped — a missing key is not a connectivity failure, matching `LocalBackend`'s existing semantics.
  - `deploy/databricks.py`: every DBFS/Jobs-API call site (`_db_put`'s create/add-block/close, `submit`, `poll`) caught `httpx.HTTPStatusError` (a bad response) but not its sibling `httpx.RequestError` (connection refused, DNS failure, TLS error, timeout) — an unreachable/misconfigured `workspace_url` propagated raw. Added matching `except httpx.RequestError` clauses raising `DeployError` (or, in `poll()`, returning the same error `ExecutionResult` shape its `HTTPStatusError` branch already returns); factored the add-block failure path's duplicated best-effort handle-close cleanup into `_best_effort_close()` so both the existing and new except clauses share it.
  - `surveyor/benchmark_store.py::BenchmarkStore.from_config`'s postgres-without-`path` `ValueError` — already caught at all 3 CLI call sites in `aqueduct/cli/benchmark.py`, but a bare builtin nonetheless — is now `ConfigError` (an exact-fit existing class; one call site already anticipated it, catching `(ConfigError, ValueError)` together before this change).

  Left unconverted, with reasons — the judgement half of this pass, since most of the ~50 sites are already correct:
  - `parser/schema.py` (14 sites) and `config.py` (5 sites): `ValueError` inside a pydantic `@field_validator`/`@model_validator` is the CORRECT idiom — pydantic converts it to `ValidationError` — untouched per AGENTS.md's explicit carve-out. `patch/grammar.py`'s `PatchSpec` validator is the same idiom (`PatchSpec` is itself a pydantic model).
  - `agent/loop.py`'s 3 `TypeError`s, `agent/progressive.py::merge_patch_specs`'s `ValueError`, `depot/depot.py`'s constructor `TypeError`: precondition guards on private/internal callables always invoked with a correct argument combination by every existing caller — a developer contract, not a user-triggerable path.
  - `dev/scaffolds.py::render`'s "unknown scaffold kind" `ValueError`: `kind` is already constrained by `click.Choice` at the one CLI call site; only reachable via direct programmatic misuse of the public API. Its sibling `KeyError` ("scaffold contract drift") is a dev-time anti-rot check firing only when a schema field is renamed.
  - `executor/capabilities.py`'s 4 `ValueError`s (`version_satisfies`, `Capability.__post_init__`): an internal signal already caught and converted to `CapabilityDeclarationError` at the sole production call site (`load_declaration`); the other caller (`doctor/checks_io.py`) only ever passes an already-validated `requires` spec.
  - `executor/capability_tooling.py::write_matrix`'s "missing markers" `ValueError`: already caught and mapped to `CONFIG_ERROR` at its one CLI call site (`aqueduct dev capabilities docs`).
  - `executor/duckdb_/type_render.py` + `typehub.py`'s "unrecognized hub type" `TypeError`s: exhaustiveness assertions over the closed `HubType` union, firing only when a developer adds a new hub type without updating the renderer — a Blueprint can only ever produce a known `HubType` via the parser.
  - `mcp/server.py`'s redacted `RuntimeError` envelope: deliberate — the MCP SDK immediately re-wraps it into a structured `isError` result via `str(exc)` regardless of type, so the exception class carries no user-facing meaning here.
  - `patch/provenance.py`'s module-level `RuntimeError`: an import-time closure/sanity check over `VALID_PATCH_OPS`, firing only on a dev change to the op set.
  - `surveyor/benchmark_store.py`'s "unknown benchmark backend" `ValueError` (`# pragma: no cover`, a config `Literal` already blocks other values) and `surveyor/scenario.py`'s 4 `ValueError`s (both real `load_scenario` callers — `doctor/checks_io.py`, `surveyor/scenario.py`'s own batch loader — already catch broadly and degrade to a failing `CheckResult` or a skipped file): already unreachable-or-caught everywhere they're actually invoked.
  - `surveyor/surveyor.py`'s "start() before record()" `RuntimeError`: a caller-ordering invariant, not user-triggerable.
  - `tools/registry.py`'s `ValueError` (`read_only=False` unsupported) guards a plugin seam not wired to any external caller yet; its `KeyError` (unknown tool name) is already caught broadly at both real call sites (`mcp/server.py`, `agent/toolbox.py`), each with its own justification comment.
  - `cli/run.py::_do_compile`'s "compile dependencies missing" `RuntimeError`: flagged, not fixed — reachable only if a core (non-optional) module fails to import, i.e. a fundamentally broken install; no test exercises it, arguably below even `EnginePluginError`'s bar (that covers third-party plugins, this is the core package itself). (`aqueduct/stores/base.py`, `aqueduct/stores/postgres.py`, `aqueduct/stores/redis_.py`, `aqueduct/stores/duckdb_.py`, `aqueduct/stores/object_store.py`, `aqueduct/deploy/databricks.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/benchmark_store.py`, `aqueduct/cli/benchmark.py`; tests: `tests/test_stores/test_postgres.py`, `tests/test_stores/test_redis.py`, `tests/test_stores/test_object_store.py`, `tests/test_deploy/test_databricks.py`, `tests/test_surveyor/test_benchmark_v2_backend.py`)


## [2.0.5] — 2026-07-23

### Added
- **DuckDB's healing prompt gains an out-of-memory / capacity-exhaustion defer rule (Phase 79 item 6).** DuckDB is single-node — it fails by running OUT OF MEMORY, a failure class Spark's prompt pack has no idiom for. Without a defer rule the healer read an OOM as a code defect and patched in circles. `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/prompt_rules.py`'s `DUCKDB_PROMPT_RULES` now classifies an "Out of Memory" / `memory_limit` exceeded / buffer-allocator allocation failure as a non-patchable capacity situation: a new always-shown `rules` bullet (rendered regardless of `allow_defer`) tells the model not to chase the error with repeated config-value edits, and the `DeferRules` pack (`infra_examples` extended, a new `extra_bullets` entry) makes the same call in the "when to defer to a human" section shown when `allow_defer` is on. DuckDB-specific only — Spark's composed prompt is unchanged, and the engine-independent scaffold (`aqueduct/agent/prompts.py`) names no engine.
- **Type-hub docs catch up to the shipped code (Phase 80, work package 4 of 4).** Work packages 1–3 shipped the vocabulary, the capability gate, and the runtime mapping; the docs still described the pre-hub "Aqueduct owns no types, types are Spark DDL strings" monoculture. `docs/specs.md` §9 is rewritten to the settled formulation — Aqueduct invents no types, it adopts Arrow's model as a deliberately small subset, each engine declares its own mapping — with the full constructor table (the `timestamp_tz`/`timestamp_ntz` pair called out as load-bearing), the semantic-vs-representational ambiguity rule stated as two explicit halves (bare `timestamp` rejects-and-names-alternatives; `long`/`bigint` canonicalize silently), the bare-`timestamp` deprecation window, the native-namespace escape hatch, and an honest close: the hub is a superset that turns engine divergence into a visible compile-time refusal, not a portability guarantee. §10.9's forward-references to the old §9.3 narrative are reconciled, and its "Phase 80 work package N" labels are dropped (per `AGENTS.md`'s user-facing-docs sweep rule — `docs/extending.md`'s matching label too). `docs/roadmap.md` gains a new "Type hub: long-tail constructors" section for what's still deferred (uint*/unions/dictionary/string_view, and `interval` verified-out with its specific reason: two Arrow families on Spark, a third on DuckDB, µs→ms Parquet truncation). `SKILL.md` gains a dedicated type-spellings section (portable table, deprecated-bare-`timestamp` note, native-hatch capability consequence, one worked cast/schema_hint example). The Blueprint template's `schema_hint`/`return_type` comments now point at the hub instead of "Spark DDL type string"; `docs/specs.md` §5.4's UDF `return_type` field description had the same stale claim, fixed. `docs/compatibility.md` gains one prose line pointing at specs §9 for the vocabulary the `type.*`/`type.native.*` matrix rows enforce. No code changes. (`docs/specs.md` → Version 2.31, `docs/roadmap.md`, `docs/extending.md`, `docs/compatibility.md`, `SKILL.md`, `aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`)
