- **Engines actually CONSUME the hub types now (Phase 80, work package 3 of 4).** Work packages 1–2 built the hub vocabulary and gated it at compile time; runtime still passed raw user spellings through three hand-rolled per-engine alias dicts — `duckdb_/channel.py::_CAST_TYPE_ALIASES` (9 entries), `duckdb_/ingress.py::_TYPE_ALIASES` (9 entries), `spark/ingress.py::_TYPE_ALIASES` (5 entries) — all now DELETED. `ExecutorProtocol` (`aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`) gains `render_type: (HubType | NativeType) -> str`, OPTIONAL like the schema-reader members (not required like `execute`/`extract_error`/`prompt_rules` — forcing a complete type mapper on every third-party engine mid-MVP would be premature): an engine with no mapper still runs a Blueprint using only its OWN native escape-hatch spellings, but a HUB spelling against it is refused, not silently mishandled. `render_native_type(engine, spelling)` is the one seam every engine's runtime type consumption routes through: a `NativeType` naming THIS engine returns `.spelling` verbatim; a `NativeType` naming a DIFFERENT engine is a defensive `EnginePluginError` (the compile-time `type.native.*` gate should already have refused it — this only fires on an ungated path); a hub type renders through the target engine's `render_type`. Both shipped engines register a real mapper: `aqueduct/executor/spark/type_render.py` (pure string logic, mostly identity — Spark's DDL already matches the hub's own canonical spellings character-for-character; the one load-bearing exception is the timestamp pair, since `timestamp_tz`/`timestamp_ntz` are not themselves valid Spark DDL, `timestamp_tz` renders to Spark's plain `timestamp`) and `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/type_render.py` (verified empirically against a real `duckdb.connect()`, including nested composites — `array<map<string,int>>` → `MAP(VARCHAR, INTEGER)[]`). Each engine's `normalize_type_spelling()` helper preserves the deleted dicts' raw-passthrough fallback for a `TypeSpellingError` (a spelling the hub doesn't recognize at all — DuckDB's own `BLOB`/`STRUCT(x INTEGER)` DDL, Spark's `interval day to second`/`variant`) so every previously-accepted native spelling still works unchanged. Channel `op: cast` (both engines), Ingress `schema_hint` (both engines), and Spark UDF `return_type` (both the python and java/scala registration paths) now route through this seam; DuckDB has no UDF path (`feature.python_udf: unsupported`) so no work there. The concrete fix this closes: `array<int>`/`map<string,int>`/`struct<a:int>`/`decimal(10,2)`/`timestamp_ntz`/`binary` as a DuckDB cast or schema_hint type used to reach DuckDB's SQL parser completely unmodified and fail (DuckDB wants `INTEGER[]`, not `array<int>`) despite `type.array` already being declared `supported` — a declaration/behavior mismatch work package 2's own capabilities.yml comments documented explicitly. Ingress `schema_hint` validation on DuckDB normalizes the HINT's expected type through the hub but leaves the live column's own `str(dtype)` untouched (only lower-cased) — parsing an already-concrete native spelling like `"TIMESTAMP"` back through the hub would route it through the hub's bare-`timestamp`-is-ambiguous resolution (work package 1) and silently misreport a genuinely naive DuckDB column as `timestamp_tz`. (`aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/type_render.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/type_render.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/channel.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/ingress.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/udf.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/channel.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/ingress.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.30, `docs/extending.md`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_executor_protocol.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_executor.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_executor_channel.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_executor_ingress.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_udf.py`)
- **Type constructors are capability leaves, and the compile gate enforces them (Phase 80, work package 2 of 4).** Work package 1 shipped the hub vocabulary (`aqueduct/typehub.py`) as validation-only — no engine declared a verdict for a type, and §9.3 documented that gap explicitly. This package closes it: `aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py::type_leaves()` derives one `type.<constructor>` leaf per hub type constructor (`type.boolean`, `type.array`, `type.decimal`, `type.timestamp_tz`, …) from `typehub.constructor_names()` — a new enumeration hook, never a hand-duplicated list — plus one `type.native.<engine>` leaf per REGISTERED engine (resolved via `capability_tooling.discover_declarations()`, the same path the closure test already uses, never a hand-listed engine name), folded into both `all_leaves()` and `execution_leaves()` (a `supported` type leaf now carries the same verdict→test link obligation as any other execution leaf). The native namespace is governed, not exempt: `type.native.spark` is `supported` on Spark / `unsupported` on DuckDB and vice versa — an engine-native spelling aimed at the WRONG engine is a compile error, not a runtime parser crash. `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py::leaves_for_module()` (per-module: Channel cast columns, Ingress schema_hint) and a new `type_leaves_for_manifest()` (manifest-scoped, UDF `return_type` — not owned by one module, same reasoning as the existing `feature_leaves_for_manifest`) walk each inventoried type spelling RECURSIVELY, so `array<map<string,int>>` checks `type.array` + `type.map` + `type.string` + `type.int` individually; `CapabilityProblem` gained a `spelling` field so the resulting `CompileError` names the offending literal spelling (`"duckdb:HUGEINT"`), not just the constructor leaf id. Surface EXTRACTION (where the type strings live in a module's config / udf_registry — the columns dict-vs-list shape, the schema_hint dict-vs-list shape) is now shared between compiler step 8h and the gate via new `aqueduct/compiler/type_surfaces.py`, so the two passes cannot independently drift on "where do type strings live"; the actual `parse_type()` call stays separate per call site (8h validates, the gate derives leaves) — re-parsing a handful of short strings at compile time is negligible, and keeping the gate's only input `module.config`/`udf_registry` (data the Manifest already carries verbatim) avoided growing the Manifest schema with a cached, potentially-stale type-usage side channel. Both engines earned real verdicts leaf by leaf: Spark declares all sixteen constructors `supported` (backed by real `channel.op.cast` tests casting to each type's genuine Spark DDL spelling, including `timestamp_ntz` and Spark 4's `variant` for the native-namespace proof) and `type.native.duckdb` `unsupported`; DuckDB — verified empirically against a real `duckdb.connect()`, not copied from Spark's table — also declares all sixteen `supported` (DuckDB natively represents every hub constructor: `TINYINT`, `BLOB`, `TIMESTAMPTZ`/`TIMESTAMP`, native `ARRAY`/`MAP`/`STRUCT`, `DECIMAL(p,s)`) plus `type.native.spark` `unsupported`, with a documented weak spot: `_normalize_cast_type`/`_normalize_type`'s small alias dicts don't yet know the hub's own canonical spellings, so a Blueprint author writing `array<int>` verbatim still reaches DuckDB's parser unmodified and fails there today — closed by work package 3's hub↔native mapping tables, not this one. The doctor version-gate path (`aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, reusing `leaves_for_module`) is unaffected — no `type.*` leaf carries a `requires` constraint. (`aqueduct/typehub.py`, `aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/type_surfaces.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.29; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_executor_channel.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_executor.py`)
- **The Arrow-borrowed hub type vocabulary (Phase 80, work package 1 of 4).** Today a Blueprint type string (Ingress `schema_hint`, Channel `op: cast` columns, UDF `return_type`) is a raw Spark DDL string passed through unvalidated to whichever engine compiles it — on Spark that costs nothing (Spark's own parser reads it), but every other engine reads it through a hand-rolled alias dict, and the worst gap is silent: Spark's `timestamp` is an instant, DuckDB's `TIMESTAMP` is naive wall-clock, so the same Blueprint means a different value on each engine. New `aqueduct/typehub.py` (engine-agnostic, no `pyspark`/`duckdb` import) defines one internal type vocabulary borrowing Apache Arrow's semantics for ~15 constructors (`boolean`, `tinyint`/`smallint`/`int`/`bigint`, `float`/`double`, `string`, `binary`, `date`, `decimal(p,s)`, `timestamp_tz`/`timestamp_ntz`, `array<T>`, `map<K,V>`, `struct<name:type,...>`) plus a `NativeType` `<engine>:<spelling>` escape hatch — a deliberately small SUBSET of Arrow's full taxonomy, not a pyarrow dependency. `parse_type()` canonicalizes familiar unambiguous aliases silently (`long`→`bigint`, `integer`→`int`, `varchar`/`char`→`string`, …) and raises a new `TypeSpellingError` (`aqueduct/errors.py`, a `ParseError` subclass) naming the nearest valid spellings for anything else. Bare `timestamp` is the one semantically ambiguous spelling: for this release it still parses (canonicalizing to `timestamp_tz`, today's Spark behavior, so no Blueprint's runtime behavior changes) but emits a new suppressible warning, rule id `ambiguous_type_spelling` (`aqueduct/warnings.py` machinery, documented in `docs/spark_guide.md`) — it becomes a hard parse error next release. `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py` routes every inventoried surface's type string through `parse_type` at compile time for validation only; the original string still reaches the executor unchanged (no engine mapping, no runtime behavior change — later work packages). This package intentionally ships no engine ↔ hub mapping and no capability-leaf gating for types (work packages 2–4); `docs/specs.md` §9 is unchanged until the hub is fully wired to an engine. (`aqueduct/typehub.py`, `aqueduct/errors.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `docs/spark_guide.md`; tests: `tests/test_typehub/`)
- **Verdict-to-test linking for the capability matrix (Phase 79).** `supported` required a test on that engine as policy, but nothing checked it. Capability rows now carry an optional `tests:` key (pytest node ids or bare file paths); `Capability` gained a shape-validated `tests` field (`aqueduct/executor/capabilities.py`), `capability_leaves.py::execution_leaves()` derives — from the same per-category walkers, not a hand-listed set — the in-scope EXECUTION-leaf subset (`module.type.*`, `channel.op.*`, `ingress/egress format/mode/on_new_columns`, `junction/funnel.mode.*`, `feature.*`; `config.*` and the schema-authoring leaves stay out, warn-only/engine-invariant with nothing per-engine to exercise), and a new closure test (`tests/test_capabilities/test_verdict_test_links.py`) fails the build if a `supported` execution row has no test id or a declared id does not resolve against the real test tree. `aqueduct dev capabilities check` reports the same gaps (informational, does not flip its exit code); `sync`/`scaffold` already only append/generate rows and never rewrite an existing one, so a `tests:` block survives untouched. Both shipped engines' `capabilities.yml` are backfilled. The six Spark EXECUTION leaves initially left honestly unbacked (`egress.format.custom`, `egress.mode.error`/`errorifexists`/`ignore`, `egress.on_new_columns.alert`, `ingress.format.custom`) now carry real tests too: `egress.mode.error`/`errorifexists`/`ignore` get genuine write-then-write-again Spark assertions (raise-on-existing-target vs. fresh-target vs. silent no-op); `egress.on_new_columns.alert` exercises the alert branch end to end (warning logged naming the drifted column, write still absorbs it via mergeSchema); `egress.format.custom`/`ingress.format.custom` get dispatch-proof tests against a real Spark 4.0+ Python DataSource — the ingress side does a genuine metadata-level read through the registered class (schema/columns), while the egress side stubs only the final `DataFrameWriter.save()` (executing a Python DataSource write needs `pyarrow`, not installed in CI/dev), named honestly as dispatch-level rather than a full round trip. `tests/test_capabilities/test_verdict_test_links.py` is fully green on both engines.
- **Heal-patch provenance + a cross-engine self-heal gate (Phase 79).** Self-heal's system prompt is engine-flavored by design (each engine registers its own `PromptRules` pack), so a patch generated while healing a DuckDB run can carry DuckDB-dialect SQL/casts/format options; nothing previously tagged where a patch's content came from, so that patch could land in a Blueprint later deployed to Spark — the healing feature manufacturing production defects. `aqueduct patch apply` (and the `agent.approval: auto` direct-write path) now appends a machine-written `healed_by:` record to the Blueprint YAML per applied patch (`patch_id`, `engine`, `engine_version` [best-effort, nullable], `run_id`, `classification`, `applied_at`, `validated_on`) — a new top-level Blueprint field (`parser/schema.py::HealedByRecordSchema`, `extra="forbid"`), machine-written only via the existing ruamel round-trip machinery, never hand-authored. Every PatchSpec op is classified once in `aqueduct/patch/provenance.py` as `dialect_neutral` (retry/timeout, structural rewiring, resource numerics — safe across engines) or `engine_shaped` (can carry SQL/cast/format/session-config content); `set_module_config_key` is field-sensitive (classified by which config key it touches). The compiler's capability-gate area gains a new check (`aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py::check_cross_engine_heal`): a `healed_by` record classified `engine_shaped`, healed on a different engine than the compile target, and not yet in that record's `validated_on`, emits a suppressible warning (rule_id `cross_engine_heal`) naming the patch, origin, and target — never an unconditional error, since healing's value is a shippable Blueprint. `warnings.strict` (new `aqueduct.yml` field, a list of rule_ids, default empty — symmetric with `warnings.suppress` in the opposite direction) escalates listed rules to a hard `CompileError`. A GREEN run on engine X (the CLI `run` command's success path) appends X to every `healed_by` record's `validated_on`, best-effort, never touching a Blueprint with no provenance block and never failing an otherwise-successful run (`aqueduct/patch/apply.py::stamp_validated_engine`). The provenance block is excluded from `Manifest` assembly entirely, so it never perturbs a compiled Manifest's content or checkpoint hash (proven by a same-blueprint ± provenance-block hash-equality test). (`aqueduct/patch/provenance.py`, `aqueduct/patch/apply.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/warnings.py`, `docs/specs.md` §8.14 → Version 2.27; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_patch_provenance.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_healed_by_provenance.py`, `tests/test_compiler/test_cross_engine_heal.py`)
- **`docs/extending.md` — the engine-author's guide (Phase 78).** Written after DuckDB shipped through the real scaffold-and-fill workflow, not speculatively: the capability-scaffolding steps, the `ExecutorProtocol` contract member by member, the entry-point declaration, the two error types (`EnginePluginError` vs `CapabilityDeclarationError`), layer rules, and testing expectations, plus an explicit up-front warning that `ExecutorProtocol` is not yet a stable public API. Linked from `AGENTS.md`'s Documentation map and `README.md`'s References list.
- **`SessionSpec` gains a reserved `engine_options` escape hatch (Phase 78).** `SessionSpec`'s fields are the union of what registered engines need to build a session (`blueprint_id`, `engine_config`, `master_url`, `quiet`, `quiet_startup`), and it is a frozen dataclass third-party engines consume — adding a field to it after this release ships would be a breaking change. Reserved now, ahead of any concrete need: `engine_options: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)`, an opaque per-engine bag an engine reads only the keys it understands from and ignores the rest, same contract as the named fields but open-ended. Nothing in core populates it yet; both registered engines (`spark`, `duckdb`) tolerate a spec carrying unknown `engine_options` keys without error. (`aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.25; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_executor_protocol.py`)
- **The compile-time capability gate now enforces `module.type.*` and `feature.*` verdicts — they were decorative (Phase 78).** `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py::leaves_for_module()` only ever emitted the per-config dispatch leaves (`channel.op` / `egress.*` / `ingress.format` / `junction.mode` / `funnel.mode`). It never emitted `module.type.<Type>` or any `feature.*` leaf, so a verdict like `module.type.Assert: unsupported` or `feature.python_udf: unsupported` did nothing: the blueprint compiled and the failure surfaced later as an opaque runtime error (a DuckDB `Catalog Error: Scalar Function … does not exist` for a missing UDF; an `ExecuteError` for an unhandled module type) instead of a clean `CompileError`. This was the root of BOTH DuckDB Stage A "genuinely broken" census snippets — engine-honest capability declarations that the gate simply never read. Now `leaves_for_module()` emits `module.type.<Type>` for every module (always a real leaf — pydantic guarantees a valid type at parse time), and a new `feature_leaves_for_manifest()` derives the `feature.*` leaves a compiled Manifest actually exercises from real manifest fields — today `feature.python_udf` / `feature.java_udf` from each `udf_registry` entry's `lang` (`python`→python_udf, `java`/`scala`→java_udf), attributed to the UDF id so the error names what pulled the feature in — never a hardcoded list, so a Blueprint that declares no UDF exercises no UDF feature and a runtime-only concept with no manifest signal (e.g. `feature.parallel_mode`, the `--parallel` flag) is deliberately not fabricated. On Spark (every leaf `supported`) all of this is a no-op — `test_gate_noop.py` stays **43/43** across all 44 shipped snippets. On DuckDB the two previously-broken snippets now compile-error cleanly and become census PASSes: `05_channel_sql_udf` → `Module 'mask_sensitive' uses 'feature.python_udf', which engine 'duckdb' does not support. …` and `14_assert_row_quarantine` → `Module 'validate_orders' uses 'module.type.Assert', which engine 'duckdb' does not support. …` (both verified through the CLI: `aqueduct run --set deployment.engine=duckdb` now prints `✗ compile error: …` instead of crashing). The doctor version-gate path (`aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, which reuses `leaves_for_module`) is unaffected — it only acts on leaves carrying a `requires` constraint, and `module.type.*`/`feature.*` carry none. Spark invariant held: `tests/test_executor/` and `tests/test_cli/` (no `-m`) green, `tests/test_compiler/` 178/178. (`aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.23; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_gate_module_and_feature.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_gate_noop.py`)
- **`ExecutorProtocol` can now RUN an engine, not just register one — session lifecycle behind the contract (Phase 78).** The protocol described how an engine executes and heals, but not how its session is BUILT: `aqueduct/cli/run.py` hardcoded `if engine == "spark": make_spark_session(...) else: raise NotImplementedError`, then called `execute(manifest, session, parallel=…, use_observe=…, sampling=…, observability_store=…, explain_capture=…)` with Spark-only kwargs — so the CLI could not reach any other engine no matter what handlers existed (the exact reason the Stage A DuckDB census had to bypass the CLI via a direct `execute()`). Two additions close it: (1) `ExecutorProtocol` gains a `make_session: (SessionSpec) -> session` / `close_session: (session) -> None` pair, and a `SessionSpec` frozen dataclass (`blueprint_id`, `engine_config`, `master_url`, `quiet`, `quiet_startup` — the *union* of what registered engines need, an engine reads what it understands so a single-node engine ignores `master_url`). Both are optional at registration (a compile-only engine or a test double has no session) and resolved on the run path through `session_factory()` / `session_closer()`, which raise a clean `EnginePluginError` naming the engine — the `AqueductError` replacement for the bare `NotImplementedError`. (2) The Spark-only run options became *clearly-optional capabilities an engine may ignore*: the shared CLI passes the same option set to every engine, and each engine's protocol adapter forwards only what its real `execute()` accepts. Spark's adapter forwards all of them into the UNCHANGED `spark.executor.execute`; DuckDB's adapter drops `parallel`/`use_observe`/`sampling`/`observability_store`/`explain_capture` (all UNSUPPORTED/unimplemented on DuckDB — see its capability table) so its `execute()` is never handed an option it cannot honour. `aqueduct/cli/run.py`'s session creation + teardown now dispatch entirely through `get_protocol(engine)` with no `if engine == "spark"` on that path; an unknown engine surfaces as the existing `deployment.engine` config error, not `NotImplementedError`. **Verified end to end:** `aqueduct run --set deployment.engine=duckdb` on `gallery/snippets/01_ingress_csv_options` (UNMODIFIED) now completes THROUGH THE CLI — both modules ✓, `output.parquet` written with the correct 3 rows/inferred types — and the same snippet on Spark is behaviourally unchanged (identical status lines, `spark local[*]` banner). Spark invariant held: `test_gate_noop.py` 43/43, `tests/test_executor/` and `tests/test_cli/` (no `-m` filter) green, `test_executor_protocol.py` 23/23. Spark's session construction moved behind the protocol with byte-for-byte identical behaviour (raw `session.stop` teardown preserved, not swapped for `stop_spark_session`'s `AQ_TESTING` guard, which belongs to short-lived commands). (`aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.23; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_executor_protocol.py`)
- **The DuckDB execution engine — Phase 78 Stage A, the capability framework's first real second engine.** `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/` registers as `duckdb` through the `aqueduct.engines` entry point (`aqueduct-core[duckdb]` extra; `duckdb`/`sqlglot` are already base dependencies), earning a 261-row `capabilities.yml` leaf by leaf against real handlers and tests rather than copying Spark's table — every leaf marked `unsupported` carries a hint explaining why and, where relevant, the workaround. Implements: Ingress `parquet`/`csv`/`json` (lazy `DuckDBPyRelation` reads, same pass-through principle as Spark's lazy DataFrames); Channel `sql`/`join` (Spark-SQL transpiled to DuckDB SQL via `sqlglot`, already a dependency) plus native `filter`/`select`/`deduplicate`; Junction (all three modes — pure filters, cheap on any engine); Funnel `union_all`/`union`; Egress `parquet`/`csv` via `COPY ... TO` (`overwrite`/`error`/`errorifexists`/`ignore` — DuckDB's `COPY` has no native append, so `mode: append`/`merge`/`overwrite_partitions` are honestly `unsupported`); Regulator (pass-through gate logic, no engine-specific code). `channel.op.{rename,cast,sort,repartition,coalesce,cache,union}`, `funnel.mode.{coalesce,zip}`, `egress.on_new_columns.*`, `egress.format.{delta,iceberg,hudi,depot,custom}`, `ingress.format.{delta,jdbc,kafka,depot,custom}`, `module.type.{Assert,Probe}`, and `feature.{python_udf,java_udf,parallel_mode,metrics_boundary,broadcast_junction,custom_datasource,delta_write,delta_time_travel}` are all declared `unsupported` — not implemented/tested this stage, honestly declared rather than silently faked. `deployment.target` stays local-only (`config.deployment.databricks.*` unsupported); `master_url`/`spark_config` are `ignored_with_warning` so one `aqueduct.yml` stays valid on both engines. `extract_duckdb_error` (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/error_extraction.py`) parses DuckDB's own exception vocabulary (`BinderException`/`CatalogException`/etc. class names, `object_name`/`suggested_columns` regex-parsed out of DuckDB's "Candidate bindings"/"Did you mean" message text — DuckDB has no structured `getMessageParameters()` API the way Spark 4 does) into the same `FailureContext` field shape Spark's extractor produces. `DUCKDB_PROMPT_RULES` (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/prompt_rules.py`) is a from-scratch healing-prompt pack (persona, DuckDB-specific rule bullets, `DeferRules`) — zero Spark text, verified by the existing anti-bleed guard test with no modification. `duckdb` may be imported only inside `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/`, mirroring the `pyspark`-only-inside-`aqueduct/executor/spark/` rule (the obs/depot/benchmark stores' own `duckdb` dependency, `aqueduct/stores/duckdb_/`, is unrelated). New `duckdb` pytest marker (`tests/test_executor_duckdb/`, no `pyspark` dependency so no `collect_ignore_glob` guard is needed the way Spark's suite needs one). Spark is unaffected: `test_gate_noop.py` 43/43, `tests/test_executor/` 550/550, `tests/test_cli/` 531/531 (no `-m` filter) all green — the only pre-existing test that needed updating was one hardcoded `engines == ["spark"]` assertion in `test_engine_registration.py`, now legitimately `["duckdb", "spark"]`. (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/`, `pyproject.toml`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.22, `docs/compatibility.md`; tests: `tests/test_executor_duckdb/`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_engine_registration.py`)

  **Known Stage A gaps, found and left honestly open, not papered over:**
  - `aqueduct run --set deployment.engine=duckdb` does not complete end to end through the CLI yet. `aqueduct/cli/run.py::_setup_surveyor`'s engine-session bootstrap hardcodes `SparkSession` construction with an `if engine == "spark": ... else: raise NotImplementedError(...)`, and its main `execute(manifest, session, ...)` call site passes Spark-only kwargs (`parallel`, `use_observe`, `sampling`) unconditionally — `aqueduct.executor.duckdb_.executor.execute()`'s signature doesn't accept them. This is the same "Spark-by-name coupling" this phase's brief pre-declared out of Stage A scope for `agent/toolbox.py`; it turns out `cli/run.py` has the identical disease. Verified the actual Stage A deliverable is unaffected by calling `aqueduct.executor.duckdb_.executor.execute()` directly (parse → compile → execute, bypassing the CLI) for the snippet census below — the engine itself works; the CLI orchestration around it does not yet know a second engine exists.
  - The compile-time capability gate (`aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py::leaves_for_module`) never emits `module.type.*` or `feature.*` leaves — it only checks `channel.op`/`egress.mode`/`egress.format`/`egress.on_new_columns`/`ingress.format`/`junction.mode`/`funnel.mode`. A Blueprint using `Assert` or `Probe` on `engine: duckdb` (both `unsupported`) compiles cleanly and only fails at runtime with a plain `ExecuteError`, not the intended clean `CompileError` — verified directly (`compile(..., engine="duckdb")` on `gallery/snippets/14_assert_row_quarantine` succeeds). Same for a Channel SQL query calling a Python UDF: `feature.python_udf` is `unsupported`, but nothing checks `manifest.udf_registry` against it, so the failure surfaces as a raw DuckDB `Catalog Error: Scalar Function ... does not exist` at execute time instead of a compile-time refusal. This gap predates Stage A — Spark's all-`supported` table made it invisible — and is a real finding about the capability-check machinery, not a DuckDB bug; per this phase's brief, the gate itself was deliberately left unmodified and the finding is reported here instead.
  - DuckDB's `register()` is a mutable catalog binding (a replacement scan pointing at a live Python object), not a value capture the way a Spark temp view effectively is. Stage A's `sql`/`join`/`deduplicate`-with-key Channel ops therefore materialize into a uniquely-named temp table immediately rather than staying unevaluated — necessary because the single-input convenience alias (`__input__`) is reused across every such Channel module in a run, and an unmaterialized relation built against it could otherwise silently read whatever a later module registers under that name before anything forces evaluation. `filter`/`select`/plain `deduplicate`/Ingress/Junction/Funnel stay fully lazy (no registration involved). Documented in `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/channel.py::_run_sql` and `docs/specs.md` §10.9.
  - No `duckdb_config` engine-config knob was added (`config.spark_config`'s counterpart) — Stage A's DuckDB session is always `:memory:`. A per-engine connection-config leaf is a reasonable near-term follow-up but touches `aqueduct/config.py`, `parser/schema.py`, and the Manifest, which is out of this stage's declared file scope.
  - **Snippet census** (16 gallery snippets, unmodified blueprints, `--set deployment.engine=duckdb`, run via direct parse/compile/execute since the CLI gap above blocks `aqueduct run`): **7 PASS** (`01_ingress_csv_options`, `02_blueprint_variables`, `06_channel_deduplicate`, `07_channel_join_variants`, `10_sql_macros`, `16_junction_conditional_else`, `41_enabled_conditional`), **7 clean `CompileError`** (`08_channel_union_fanout` — `channel.op.union`; `09_channel_repartition_cache` — `channel.op.{repartition,cache}`; `17_funnel_coalesce_fallback` — `funnel.mode.coalesce`; `25_channel_native_ops` — `channel.op.{rename,cast,sort}`; `30_ingress_schema_hints` — `egress.on_new_columns.allow`; `36_funnel_modes` — `funnel.mode.zip`; `37_egress_modes` — `egress.mode.{append,overwrite_partitions}`) — all honestly declared, all a PASS by this phase's design, **2 genuine failures**, both real bugs surfaced by the gate-coverage gap above rather than by the engine's own handlers: `05_channel_sql_udf` (Python UDF called from Channel SQL — `feature.python_udf` is `unsupported` but nothing gates it pre-execution) and `38_partition_io` (`SUBSTRING(date_col, int, int)` — Spark's 1-indexed `substring` on a `DATE` column auto-casts; DuckDB's `substring(VARCHAR, BIGINT, BIGINT)` does not accept a `DATE` argument at all, a real Spark-vs-DuckDB SQL-dialect gap `sqlglot`'s transpile does not paper over).

- **The DuckDB capability table, re-earned leaf by leaf — checkpoint 3b's honesty rework (Phase 78).** The prior checkpoint's 261-row `capabilities.yml` had drifted from its own rule: several `supported` rows had no covering test (a handler existed but nothing proved it ran on DuckDB), a few `unsupported` rows were false negatives (a handler existed and just needed a test), and four `config.*` rows claimed `supported` for knobs the DuckDB executor genuinely never reads. Re-audited every leaf against the rule stated at the top of the YAML: EXECUTION leaves need a real handler AND a DuckDB test; ENGINE-INVARIANT leaves need an end-to-end test proving core orchestration treats the engine identically, not an assumption. Net result — **218 supported / 37 unsupported / 6 ignored_with_warning** (was 215/44/2), 261 leaves total, unchanged (no leaf added or removed, only verdicts and hints). Changes: (1) **7 leaves flipped `unsupported` → `supported`** — `channel.op.{cast,rename,sort,union}`, `egress.mode.append`, `funnel.mode.{coalesce,zip}` — all had working handlers already; each now has a dedicated DuckDB test. (2) **4 `config.*` leaves demoted `supported` → `ignored_with_warning`** — `config.lineage.openlineage_url`, `config.lineage.openlineage_namespace` (the DuckDB executor emits no lineage events), `config.probes.default_sample_fraction`, `config.probes.max_sample_rows` (Probe is `unsupported`, so the knobs it would configure are inert) — matching the existing `master_url`/`spark_config` treatment. (3) **12 leaves reworded and grouped as explicitly DEFERRED** — `ingress.format.{delta,jdbc,depot}`, `egress.format.{delta,depot}`, `egress.mode.overwrite_partitions`, `egress.on_new_columns.*`, `feature.{delta_write,delta_time_travel,parallel_mode}` — each hint now names the concrete DuckDB mechanism (a delta-rs bridge, the postgres scanner extension, httpfs) and says "deferred to a later stage, not implemented in Stage A" instead of a vague "out of scope"; grouped under one YAML comment block so the eventual flip is a one-line edit per leaf. (4) New end-to-end tests prove `module.type.{Junction,Funnel,Regulator}` actually run through `execute()` (not just their handler functions in isolation), `feature.spillway`/`feature.checkpoint` work through a real run (including checkpoint-resume), `module.type.Arcade` compiles and runs (Arcades expand away at compile time, so the executor never dispatches one directly), and — the engine-invariant category's actual proof, not a rubber stamp — an `on_failure` command hook fires on a real `aqueduct run --set deployment.engine=duckdb` invocation and a module `retry:` policy drives the DuckDB executor's own `_with_retry` loop to both a successful-after-retries outcome and an exhausted-retries failure.

  **Three real defects found and fixed while writing those end-to-end tests** (none caught by the prior checkpoint's handler-level unit tests, because each only manifests across a real multi-module run): (1) `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/funnel.py` — `union_all`/`union`/`coalesce`/`zip` all returned an unevaluated `con.sql(...)` relation immediately after the `finally` block unregistered the upstream views that query referenced by name, so the FIRST time anything downstream (a real `execute()` run, not a same-scope unit test) tried to read the result it hit `CatalogException: Table with name … does not exist` — the identical mutable-catalog-binding bug class `channel.py::_run_sql`'s docstring already explains at length, but Funnel never got the matching fix. Now materializes into a temp table immediately, same pattern. (2) `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/egress.py::_copy_options` unconditionally emitted `HEADER <bool>` for csv even when the caller's `options:` block also set `header` (legal — `options:` is a freeform passthrough), producing DuckDB's "duplicate option" Parser Error the instant a Blueprint did both — hit by gallery snippet `11_spillway_channel` verbatim. User-supplied `options` now wins; the default `HEADER` line is skipped when `options` already sets one. (3) `aqueduct/cli/hooks.py::_run_in_process_blueprint_hook` hardcoded `get_executor("spark")` for every `in_process: true` chained-blueprint hook, so an in-process hook fired during a DuckDB run crashed the instant it touched the (DuckDB) session as if it were a SparkSession — hit by gallery snippet `42_hooks_lifecycle`. `run_hooks`/`_run_in_process_blueprint_hook` now take an `engine` parameter (default `"spark"` for backward compatibility), threaded from `aqueduct/cli/run.py`'s already-resolved `engine` variable at both call sites; the chained target compiles and executes through the PARENT's engine, its own `deployment.engine` (if set) is not consulted — matching the parent session is the entire point of in-process reuse.

  **Snippet census, unmodified gallery blueprints via `--set deployment.engine=duckdb`, run through the real `aqueduct run` CLI** (the CLI-level engine gap the prior checkpoint's census had to route around is closed as of 2.23): of 43 candidate snippets (44 minus `44_dag_cycle_detection`, excluded by design — no single root blueprint), every snippet either runs to a clean SUCCESS or fails compilation with a clean, capability-gate `CompileError` naming the exact unsupported leaf — both count as PASS. Three snippets are **genuinely broken** at runtime, one already known and two newly surfaced by this census: `38_partition_io` (Spark's `SUBSTRING` auto-casts a `DATE` argument, DuckDB's does not — a real SQL-dialect gap, left broken as designed), `19_depot_incremental` (the `${ctx._watermark}` token in an incremental Channel's `op: sql` query is substituted by Spark's watermark machinery, which the DuckDB executor does not implement — a pre-existing, already-documented Stage A gap in `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/executor.py`'s "what this stage does NOT implement" notes, not a new defect), and `23_table_first` (Spark catalog `table:` addressing has no DuckDB equivalent and, unlike every other Stage A gap, is not gated by any capability leaf at all — DuckDB's Ingress/Egress simply require `format:`+`path:` and fail at runtime with a plain "'format' is required" instead of a clean `CompileError`). The last two are real findings, honestly reported rather than squared away to make "38_partition_io is the only genuine failure" true: both are out of Stage A's declared scope, but neither is currently gated, which is itself a gap in the capability-leaf walker's coverage (materialize-incremental watermark substitution and catalog-table addressing are not schema-derivable leaves the way Channel ops/Egress modes are) — tracked as a follow-up, not fixed here. Spark invariant held throughout: `test_gate_noop.py` 43/43, `tests/test_capabilities/` and `tests/test_executor_duckdb/` fully green on both Python 3.12 and 3.14. Also folded in: a Pure-DRY fix collapsing three independently-hardcoded `.aqueduct/observability` literals (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`'s `_obs_routing_base`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`'s `_DEFAULT_OBS_ROOT`, `aqueduct/stores/read.py`'s `_OBS_ROUTING_ROOT`) onto one canonical `aqueduct.config.DEFAULT_OBS_ROUTING_ROOT` constant — zero behavior change. (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/capabilities.yml`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/funnel.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/egress.py`, `aqueduct/cli/hooks.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `aqueduct/stores/read.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.24, `docs/compatibility.md`; tests: `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_executor.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_duckdb_engine_invariant.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_hooks.py`)

- **`aqueduct dev scaffold <kind>` — a starting point for every extension seam (Phase 78).** Aqueduct has five "bring your own code" seams (custom Probe signal, custom Assert rule, python UDF, custom Python DataSource, custom secrets resolver) and each has a code side and a config side that must agree; an author previously had to reverse-engineer both from the docs. `aqueduct dev scaffold probe|assert|udf|datasource|secrets` writes the stub and prints the YAML that points at it. The stubs are generated FROM the live contracts, not from template strings: config keys come from the pydantic models (`UdfSchema`, `SecretsConfig`) via `model_fields`, the Assert vocabulary from the real `AssertRuleType`/`AssertOnFailAction` enums, the emitted Probe signal is classified by the real `probe_plugins.custom_signal_source()` before it is returned, the secrets resolver's signature is read off `load_resolver_fn`'s annotated type, and the DataSource stub implements exactly the extension points of the INSTALLED `pyspark.sql.datasource.DataSource` (introspected — it declares no abstractmethods, so its methods-that-raise-NotImplementedError are read instead; streaming methods are excluded because Aqueduct is a batch engine). A renamed schema field makes generation fail loudly instead of emitting YAML the parser rejects. The two contracts with no introspectable object (the Probe signal's `fn(df, sig_cfg)` and the Assert rule's `fn(df)` → `passed`/`message`/`quarantine_df`, which exist only as call sites in the Spark executor) are stated in `aqueduct/dev/scaffolds.py` and PINNED by tests that parse those call sites, so a change to either breaks the build. Every generated stub is loaded back through its seam's real loader in tests, and a scaffolded UDF + custom Probe signal were run end to end on a real Spark job. (`aqueduct/dev/scaffolds.py`, `aqueduct/cli/dev.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_dev_scaffold.py`)
- **`@aq.deployment.engine()`** — a Tier 1 runtime function returning the engine the Manifest is compiled for (`deployment.engine`), so a Blueprint can stamp the engine into an output path or a tag when the same pipeline runs on more than one engine. Threaded from `compile(engine=...)`, the same value the capability gate uses, so it cannot disagree with what actually ran. (`aqueduct/compiler/runtime.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `docs/specs.md` §5.3, `SKILL.md`; tests: `tests/test_compiler/test_runtime.py`)
- **The CI/dev environment is locked, two-tier (Phase 78).** No lockfile existed anywhere: every CI job resolved fresh from the ranges in `pyproject.toml`, so what got installed depended on what PyPI published that morning (the diagnosed cause of the intermittent `28_delta_merge_retry` failure, where a fresh resolve pulled pandas 3.0.3 / numpy 2.5.1 / pyarrow 25). Library dependencies **stay as ranges** — `aqueduct-core` is a published library and exact pins would make it uninstallable alongside a user's own stack — while the CI/dev **environment** is pinned by committed pip constraints files (`requirements/ci-py311.txt` for the python-3.11 lanes, `requirements/compat-py3.11/3.12/3.13.txt` for the compat matrix's three interpreters). Constraints, not requirements: they add nothing to an install, they only fix the version of whatever the ranges resolve to, so the existing `pip install -e ".[…]"` lines are unchanged and the workflows just set `PIP_CONSTRAINT`. The compat files carry no pyspark/delta-spark pin: those axes are pinned per combo by the matrix itself (the Legacy combo installs pyspark 3.5.8 explicitly). **The canary lane stays deliberately unpinned** — `version-matrix.yml`'s `snippets` job sets no constraint, because resolving fresh is exactly what makes it go red first when upstream drifts; that early warning is the feature, and locking it would delete it. Regenerate with `bash scripts/lock.sh` (documented in CONTRIBUTING.md). (`requirements/`, `scripts/lock.sh`, `.github/workflows/test-suite.yml`, `.github/workflows/version-matrix.yml`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `docs/compatibility.md`; tests: `tests/test_meta_ci.py`)
- **The capability tooling ships — `aqueduct dev capabilities scaffold|sync|check|docs` (Phase 78).** The tool lived in `scripts/capabilities.py`, and `scripts/` is not in the wheel (`[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages = ["aqueduct"]`), so a third-party engine author who `pip install`ed `aqueduct-core` could not generate the capability table their engine cannot register without. That table is 261 rows: hand-writing it does not scale, and copying Spark's hands the new engine 261 `supported` rows — a silent claim to implement the entire grammar, the exact blindness the capability framework exists to prevent. The logic now lives in the installed package (`aqueduct/executor/capability_tooling.py`) behind a new `dev` CLI group (`aqueduct/cli/dev.py`), following the same group conventions as `patch` / `stores` / `mcp`. `scaffold --engine <name>` works for an engine with no table at all: it emits every grammar + config leaf with verdict `undeclared`, plus a header stating that every row must be replaced, that the build fails while any `undeclared` remains, and that Spark's table is a reference to read rather than a file to copy. `check` (exit 1 on drift) is what CI runs; `sync` appends newly-derived leaves as `undeclared` and never invents a verdict or deletes a row; `docs` regenerates the engine matrix in `docs/compatibility.md`. Declarations are discovered through the `aqueduct.engines` entry points via `find_spec` — without importing the engine module, since an engine with an incomplete table is precisely the one that cannot be imported. `scripts/capabilities.py` is now a thin wrapper that forwards to the CLI, so there is one implementation, not two. Bumps specs.md to Version 2.21 (§10.9). (`aqueduct/executor/capability_tooling.py`, `aqueduct/cli/dev.py`, `scripts/capabilities.py`, `.github/workflows/test-suite.yml`, `docs/cli_reference.md`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_dev_capabilities.py`)
- **Config-leaf governance (Phase 78 Step 3).** The capability framework covered the Blueprint grammar only — `aqueduct.yml` engine-config keys (`aqueduct/config.py`) carried no verdict at all, so a key meaningless on the target engine (e.g. `deployment.master_url` on a future single-node engine) was a silent no-op. New `aqueduct/executor/config_leaves.py`: `all_config_leaves()` derives the canonical `config.*` leaf set by walking `AqueductConfig`'s pydantic models (never hand-listed — a `list`/`dict`-of-submodel field like `stores.depots`/`agent.cascade` is one atomic leaf, not enumerated per key); `explicitly_set_config_leaves(cfg)` walks a validated config INSTANCE via `model_fields_set` at every nesting level, so only keys the user actually wrote are checked (untouched defaults never warn). `aqueduct/config.py::load_config()` calls this once `deployment.engine` is known and validated, and emits a suppressible `engine_key_ignored` warning — the SAME rule id and `aqueduct.warnings.emit` machinery the compile-time blueprint gate uses — for any explicitly-set leaf whose verdict on that engine isn't `supported`. Deliberately WARN, never ERROR: unlike the blueprint gate (`unsupported` is a hard `CompileError`), a config key that means nothing on the current engine must not break loading `aqueduct.yml` — the same file has to stay valid across engines, deployment profiles, and test overrides. The 101 derived config leaves fold into the SAME closure test as the grammar leaves (`tests/test_capabilities/test_closure.py`), and are unioned into Spark's default-`supported` leaf set (`aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.py`) so the reference engine declares them all `supported` — a genuine no-op for every existing `aqueduct.yml` and all 44 gallery snippet configs, proven for a future engine via a fake single-node engine that declares `config.deployment.master_url` as `ignored_with_warning`. (`aqueduct/executor/config_leaves.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_config_gate.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_closure.py`)
- **`scripts/capabilities.py` — the capability declaration dev tool.** `sync` walks the grammar + config models and appends any leaf missing from an engine's `capabilities.yml` as `undeclared` (never inventing a verdict, never deleting a row — a human decides, and an orphaned row is reported for review rather than silently dropped). `scaffold --engine <name>` writes a **complete** `capabilities.yml` for a brand-new engine with every leaf present and every verdict `undeclared` — the answer to "how do I implement a new engine". It is generated from the walkers (so it cannot go stale like a checked-in template) and deliberately is not a copy of Spark's table (which would hand a new engine ~261 `supported` rows — a silent claim to implement the whole grammar, the exact blindness the framework prevents); the generated header says so. The engine will not register until every row is a real decision. `check` reports drift without writing (CI-friendly, wired into the `capabilities-tests` job). `docs` regenerates the engine capability matrix in `docs/compatibility.md` between `<!-- ENGINE_MATRIX_START/END -->` markers, so the published matrix (declared totals per engine + every version-gated / ignored / unsupported leaf with its hint) is generated from the declarations rather than hand-maintained. Bumps specs.md to Version 2.20 (§10.9, corrected — see Fixed below). (`scripts/capabilities.py`, `docs/compatibility.md`)
- Engine capability framework (Phase 78): every engine declares, per grammar leaf (module config fields, Channel ops, Egress write modes, Junction/Funnel fan modes, feature flags), whether it is `supported` (optionally version-gated), `unsupported`, or `ignored_with_warning`. The full leaf set is derived from the actual grammar (`aqueduct/executor/capability_leaves.py`), not hand-listed, and a closure test fails the build if a new leaf lacks an explicit verdict in a registered engine's table. Spark declares default-`supported` for (almost) the whole grammar today (`aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.py`); a future engine starts default-`unsupported` instead. Wired in as the last step of `compile()` (`aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py` — `CompileError` on an unsupported leaf, suppressible warning `engine_key_ignored` otherwise) and as a new `aqueduct doctor` check (`check_capabilities`) that validates version-gated capabilities against what's actually installed. See `docs/specs.md` §10.9.
- Engine registration seam (Phase 78 Step 1): an execution engine registers itself through the `aqueduct.engines` setuptools entry-point group (`pyproject.toml`'s `[project.entry-points."aqueduct.engines"]` — `spark = "aqueduct.executor.spark.engine"`). `aqueduct/executor/capabilities.py::load_engines()` resolves and imports every registered entry point once per process; core never imports an engine's package by name, so a future engine (DuckDB) needs no edit to `aqueduct/compiler/`, `aqueduct/config.py`, or anywhere else in core. `deployment.engine` in `aqueduct.yml` is now validated against the set of registered engines instead of a fixed `Literal`, and `flink` — a placeholder that was never implemented — is no longer special-cased; it fails with a `ConfigError` listing the registered engines like any other unregistered name. `aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py` is the reference entry-point module (`load_executor()` — a lazy accessor deferring the `pyspark` import to call time; the full cross-engine `ExecutorProtocol` is a later step).
- Two new `AqueductError` subclasses in the error taxonomy (`aqueduct/errors.py`), both from the engine-registration seam: `UnknownEngineError` (subclasses `CompileError`, so `compile()`'s existing error contract is unchanged) is raised by `get_capabilities()` for an engine with no registered capability declaration, and carries the registered-engine list on the exception so callers distinguish "unknown engine name" from "nothing registered at all" by type rather than by matching the message. `EnginePluginError` is raised when an `aqueduct.engines` entry point fails to import, naming the entry point, its target, and the underlying cause — a broken or half-installed third-party engine plugin surfaces as a clean Aqueduct error instead of a raw `ImportError` escaping out of `aqueduct.yml` loading.
- `ExecutorProtocol` — the engine-agnostic execution contract (Phase 78 Step 2, `aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`): a frozen dataclass (`engine`, `execute`, `extract_error`, `prompt_rules`) every registered engine provides alongside its capability declaration, as an import side effect of the same `aqueduct.engines` entry-point module. `execute` is the engine's `(manifest, ...) -> ExecutionResult` function; `extract_error` maps an engine exception to `FailureContext` fields (`error_class`, `root_exception`, `sql_state`, `suggested_columns`, `object_name`); `prompt_rules` is a `PromptRules` pack — the engine's half of the healing system prompt (`persona`, `root_cause_note`, `rules`). Both `extract_error` and the `PromptRules` pack are required: `ExecutorProtocol.__post_init__` raises `EnginePluginError` at construction time if either is missing or incomplete, so an engine cannot register without a way to populate structured failure context or give the healing LLM engine context. This is the structural fix for "heal quality silently degrades on a new engine's errors". `get_executor()` (`aqueduct/executor/__init__.py`) now resolves through this seam instead of a hardcoded Spark-only branch, so an unknown engine raises `UnknownEngineError` rather than a bare `ValueError`. Spark's `ExecutorProtocol` (`aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py`) wires today's existing behavior through the seam rather than reimplementing it, and constructing the protocol object never imports `pyspark` (only calling `execute(...)` does, deferred to call time). Bumps specs.md to Version 2.18 (§10.9). (`aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_executor_protocol.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_executor_orchestration.py`)
- The healing system prompt is now composed per engine instead of being one Spark-flavored template (Phase 78 Step 2). `aqueduct/agent/prompts.py`'s `_SYSTEM_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` is now the engine-independent scaffold — PatchSpec schema, op-selection table, provenance rules, output contract, defer rules — and names no engine. Everything Spark-specific moved to `aqueduct/executor/spark/prompt_rules.py` and reaches the agent through `ExecutorProtocol.prompt_rules`: the persona line, the note describing what the structured root-cause block holds, the four Spark-flavored rule bullets (temp-view naming, `AnalysisException` / format-misread advice, `PREDICTED_SCHEMA_DRIFT`, `schema_hint` type mismatch), and the engine's slice of the defer-to-human section (a `DeferRules`: its infrastructure failure modes, the languages its UDFs are written in, and any defer category that exists only for it). The defer section is assembled at request time rather than living in the template constant, and it carried three Spark strings — Hive metastore locks, Python/Scala UDF code, "the failure is in Spark internals" — that a non-Spark heal would otherwise have been told to defer over. Half-generic bullets were split rather than moved wholesale, so the generic defer categories (upstream schema changes, checkpoint corruption, object-store consistency, cluster config) stay in the scaffold for every engine. The agent pulls the target engine's pack from the registry at prompt-build time (`_build_system_prompt(..., engine=...)`, threaded from `deployment.engine` through `AgentRunConfig` / `generate_cascade_patch` / `aqueduct heal`), so the agent layer imports no engine specifics and the executor layer imports nothing from the agent layer. A future engine ships its own healing persona and rules with its executor: it cannot silently inherit Spark's advice, and it cannot register with none at all. **The composed Spark prompt is byte-identical to the previous single template** across every `allow_defer` × `tools_enabled` combination (guarded by a golden test), so no `PROMPT_VERSION` bump: moving text between the scaffold and an engine's pack without changing the composed output is a refactor, not a prompt change. The anti-bleed guard greps the COMPOSED prompt for a fake non-Spark engine rather than the source constants, so a fragment assembled at request time (as the defer section is) cannot slip past it. (`aqueduct/agent/prompts.py`, `aqueduct/agent/loop.py`, `aqueduct/agent/providers.py`, `aqueduct/agent/cascade.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/prompt_rules.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/heal.py`; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_prompt_composition.py`)
- `.github/workflows/version-matrix.yml`: split the compat-matrix job contract into promised lanes (the 3 pinned combos — now actually blocking on failure) and one unpinned canary lane (`snippets`, `continue-on-error: true` — early-warning only, does not fail the workflow).

### Changed
- **BREAKING: bare `timestamp` is now a hard compile-time error, effective immediately — no deprecation window.** `aqueduct/typehub.py::parse_type` used to canonicalize a bare `timestamp` type spelling (Ingress `schema_hint`, Channel `op: cast` columns, UDF `return_type`) to `timestamp_tz` while emitting a suppressible `ambiguous_type_spelling` warning, with the warning's own text promising a hard error "next release." That plan is superseded by a direct decision: since the same bare spelling silently means an INSTANT on Spark (`timestamp_tz`) and a NAIVE wall-clock value on DuckDB (`timestamp_ntz`), there is no safe default to keep shipping even for one more release, so the warning branch is now `raise TypeSpellingError(...)` unconditionally — not suppressible via `warnings.suppress`/`--suppress-warning`/`warnings.strict`, because it was never a warning to begin with once the safe-default rationale is gone. **Migration:** any Blueprint using bare `timestamp` in a `schema_hint` field, a `cast` column, or a UDF `return_type` must be updated to `timestamp_tz` (instant semantics — the legacy behavior this spelling used to resolve to) or `timestamp_ntz` (naive wall-clock semantics), or to an engine-native spelling (`spark:timestamp`) if the Blueprint intentionally targets one engine only. The `ambiguous_type_spelling` rule id constant is retained (unemitted) only because `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py` and existing tests still reference it; nothing emits it anymore. (`aqueduct/typehub.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py`, `docs/specs.md` §9.2 → Version 2.32, `docs/spark_guide.md`, `SKILL.md`, `aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`; tests: `tests/test_typehub/test_typehub.py`, `tests/test_compiler/test_typehub_integration.py`, `tests/test_executor_duckdb/test_executor.py`)
- **`PROMPT_VERSION` bumped `1.8` → `1.9` (Phase 79 item 6).** The DuckDB engine's `PromptRules` pack gained the out-of-memory / capacity-exhaustion defer rule described above this changed DuckDB's composed healing system prompt, so the bump policy in AGENTS.md requires a version bump. Spark's composed prompt is byte-identical to `1.8` — the version constant is global, so Spark is relabeled too.
- **`PROMPT_VERSION` bumped `1.7` → `1.8` (Phase 78).** The DuckDB engine's `PromptRules` pack (`aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/prompt_rules.py`) gained a DuckDB-worded `PREDICTED_SCHEMA_DRIFT` rule bullet this phase, so DuckDB's composed healing system prompt changed — the bump policy in AGENTS.md requires a version bump for any composed-prompt body change. Spark's composed prompt is byte-identical to before; `PROMPT_VERSION` is a single global constant, so Spark's prompt is relabeled `1.8` too, a trade-off accepted rather than splitting the constant per engine. (`aqueduct/agent/loop.py`; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_prompts.py`)
- **`docs/spark_guide.md` reframed as the Spark engine's guide, not Aqueduct's general engine guide.** Now that DuckDB is a second registered engine (Phase 78), the doc's title and intro make explicit that it documents Spark-engine behavior (compiler warnings, performance, tuning, gotchas) specifically, and point to `docs/compatibility.md` for the cross-engine capability matrix. No `duckdb_guide.md` was created — deliberate, only warranted once there is DuckDB-specific content to hold. (`docs/spark_guide.md`)

### Fixed
- **`03_ingress_delta_incremental` and `34_time_travel` gallery snippets silently broke on pyspark 4.1.x.** Both snippets' `aqueduct.yml` hardcoded `spark.jars.packages` to the Delta 4.0.x artifact (`delta-spark_4.0_2.13`) by default, while `populate_delta.py` already derived the CORRECT artifact (`delta-spark_4.1_2.13` on pyspark 4.1) for its own populate-only SparkSession — so the actual `aqueduct run` step loaded a Delta jar built against a different Spark minor line and failed with `NoSuchMethodError` inside Delta's `DataSource` init, surfacing as a misleading `source not found or unreadable` ingress error. `populate_delta.py` now writes the coordinate it derived to a `.env` file beside `aqueduct.yml` (git-ignored), which `aqueduct run`'s existing project-`.env` auto-discovery picks up, so both scripts agree on the same jar without the user setting anything manually.
- **The patch sandbox gate (Gate 3) hardcoded Spark, crashing or silently disabling itself on a DuckDB deployment (Phase 79).** `aqueduct/patch/preview.py::run_sandbox_gate` built its own session via `make_spark_session` whenever no session was passed — and no call site ever passed one — then resolved the target engine's executor and called it with Spark-only `observability_store=`/`explain_capture=` kwargs regardless of what engine the Blueprint actually targeted. On a DuckDB deployment this either sandboxed a DuckDB blueprint ON SPARK or, once the executor resolved correctly, crashed with an uncaught `TypeError` (duckdb's `execute()` doesn't accept those kwargs, and the handler only caught `ParseError`/`CompileError`/`ExecuteError`); without `pyspark` installed it instead returned `status="skip", detail="sandbox could not start Spark"` — the patch safety gate silently disabled itself and blamed the wrong engine. `run_sandbox_gate` now takes a REQUIRED `engine: str` parameter (no default — mirrors the `Surveyor` decision that every construction site must resolve and pass the real target engine) and resolves the target's own `ExecutorProtocol` (`aqueduct.executor.protocol.get_protocol`) for both its session (`session_factory()`/`session_closer()`, building and tearing down a sandbox-owned session when none is passed) and its `execute()`. A DuckDB blueprint now gets a real DuckDB sandbox run; a genuinely unavailable engine's `skip` detail names the ACTUAL target engine, never Spark. `engine` reaches every call site through the same plumbing `Surveyor`/`FailureContext` already use — `cfg.deployment.engine` — threaded through `aqueduct/cli/patch.py`'s `preview --sandbox` command and `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py::_run_patch_gates_inline` (now itself a required `engine` parameter, threaded from `aqueduct/cli/run.py`'s already-resolved `engine` variable at its three call sites: the heal-cache replay-candidate check, the deep-loop validate callback, and the auto-mode multi-patch gate). (`aqueduct/patch/preview.py`, `aqueduct/cli/patch.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.26; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview_sandbox_engine.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview_gate3.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_sandbox_mode.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_sandbox_default.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_patch_gates.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_heal_apply_callback.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_heal_cache.py`)
- **`execute()` kwargs an engine adapter can't honor dropped SILENTLY — the opposite of how an ignored config key is handled (Phase 79).** `engine_key_ignored` (`aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py`) already warns when a Blueprint/`aqueduct.yml` KEY means nothing on the target engine; the analogous `execute()` KWARGS (`observability_store`, `explain_capture`, `parallel`, `use_observe`, `sampling` — meaningful to Spark, meaningless to DuckDB Stage A) had no such signal: DuckDB's `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py::_execute` filtered them out of every call with a bare allowlist and no warning, while Spark's adapter forwarded them straight into a signature that doesn't have them (a `TypeError`, on an engine narrower than Spark, uncaught by anything downstream). New engine-agnostic mechanism in `aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`: `ExecutorProtocol` gains an optional `execute_kwargs: frozenset[str] | None` field (an engine's allowlist of `OPTIONAL_EXECUTE_KWARGS` it actually consumes; `None` — Spark's declaration — means "consumes everything, no filtering"), and `filter_execute_kwargs(engine, kwargs, suppress=...)` / `call_execute(engine, *args, **kwargs)` drop anything outside that allowlist and emit one suppressible warning per dropped kwarg through the SAME `aqueduct.warnings.emit` machinery `engine_key_ignored` uses, under its own rule id `engine_kwarg_ignored` — naming both the kwarg and the engine. DuckDB's `ExecutorProtocol` now declares `execute_kwargs` from its existing (reused, not duplicated) allowlist constant; its `_execute` filter becomes a silent defensive backstop for any caller that bypasses `call_execute`, since the warning-bearing filtering now happens upstream of it. A third-party engine gets this behavior for free by declaring its own `execute_kwargs` — no protocol-author code required. Every caller that passes these kwargs routes through the mechanism: `aqueduct/cli/run.py`'s run loop (via `filter_execute_kwargs` ahead of its already-resolved `execute` callable, preserving existing `get_executor()`-mock test conventions) and the patch sandbox gate (`aqueduct/patch/preview.py::run_sandbox_gate`, via `call_execute`, alongside the Defect-A fix above). Never a crash, never silence. (`aqueduct/executor/protocol.py`, `aqueduct/executor/duckdb_/engine.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/patch/preview.py`, `docs/specs.md` §10.9 → Version 2.26; tests: `tests/test_patch/test_patch_preview_sandbox_engine.py`)
- **The capability leaf walker under-derived on the entire supported Python range, so the shipped Spark engine failed to register on 3.11–3.13 (Phase 78).** `aqueduct/executor/config_leaves.py::_direct_basemodel` unwrapped an optional sub-config field only when `typing.get_origin(annotation) is typing.Union`. But `aqueduct/config.py` writes those fields in the PEP 604 spelling (`budget: AgentBudgetConfig | None`, `databricks: DatabricksDeployConfig | None`, …), and `get_origin` of a PEP 604 union returns **`types.UnionType`** on Python 3.11–3.13 — it only *unified* to `typing.Union` on 3.14. So on the whole `requires-python = ">=3.11"` range below 3.14, every `Sub | None` field failed to recurse and its children were never derived: **the walker produced 220 leaves on 3.12 vs 261 on 3.14 (a 41-leaf gap across all optional sub-config blocks)**. Both engines' `capabilities.yml` are generated by `aqueduct dev capabilities scaffold` (run on a 3.14 dev machine), so they list the children — which on 3.11–3.13 are **orphan rows**, and `load_declaration()` fails closed on any YAML naming a leaf the walker didn't derive: `CapabilityDeclarationError: … declares a verdict for 'config.agent.budget.max_reprompts', which is not a real capability leaf`. Because `deployment.engine` validation is itself fail-closed, that raised on **every `aqueduct.yml` load** on 3.11–3.13 — for the shipped Spark engine, not merely a hypothetical new one; only the 3.14 dev interpreter escaped it. Fixed by matching **both** union origins (`_UNION_ORIGINS = (typing.Union, types.UnionType)`), so the derived leaf set is now identical on every interpreter (verified: 160 grammar + 101 config = 261 on both 3.12 and 3.14; spark and duckdb both register on both). Added a determinism guard (`tests/test_capabilities/test_walker_determinism.py`) asserting a PEP 604 `Sub | None` field recurses to its children and that the committed engine tables load against the walker on the running interpreter — these fail on the pre-fix code when run on <3.14. The bug is provably undetectable *on* 3.14 (the language unified the two types into one object there), so the guard is only meaningful on a non-3.14 interpreter; CI already runs `tests/test_capabilities/` and `aqueduct dev capabilities check` on **Python 3.11** (`test-suite.yml`'s `capabilities-tests` job, whose path filter includes `aqueduct/config.py` and `config_leaves.py`, and `version-matrix.yml`'s `unit-tests` lane), so this bug class is now caught there. The same `origin is Union` pattern at `aqueduct/parser/fs_path.py:59` was made consistent (accept both origins) — but see the honest note below: for FsPath it is **defensive-only, not a live bug**. (`aqueduct/executor/config_leaves.py`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_walker_determinism.py`)
- **`fs_path.py` union-origin handling made consistent — defensive-only, not a live bug (Phase 78).** `aqueduct/parser/fs_path.py::field_is_fs_path` matched only `origin is Union`, the same shape as the capability-walker bug above. It was flagged as a suspected cross-version divergence in optional annotated path detection — but on investigation it does **not** manifest: `Annotated[str, FsPath()] | None` (the one real optional FsPath field, `RelationalStoreConfig.path`) normalizes to `typing.Optional[Annotated[...]]` with origin `typing.Union` on EVERY Python version, because `typing.Annotated.__or__` normalizes the union. Unlike a plain `AgentBudgetConfig | None`, an Annotated-union never produces a bare `types.UnionType`, so the `types.UnionType` branch is unreachable for FsPath and the pre-fix code detected every real optional annotated path field correctly. The change (accept both origins, matching `config_leaves.py`) is kept for consistency and as insurance against a future non-Annotated path type alias reaching that branch, but it repairs no observable behaviour. The added guard (`tests/test_parser/test_path_resolution.py::test_fs_path_marker_seen_through_optional_on_this_interpreter`) is an honest version-invariance floor for optional-path detection, NOT a "fails pre-fix" regression test — because for this seam there is no cross-version failure to reproduce. (`aqueduct/parser/fs_path.py`; tests: `tests/test_parser/test_path_resolution.py`)
- **An incomplete capability declaration told you to reinstall the package, which fixes nothing (Phase 78).** Two genuinely different states shared one error type. `EnginePluginError` was raised both when an `aqueduct.engines` entry point failed to IMPORT (a broken or half-installed plugin — an install problem, where its "Reinstall or uninstall the package providing it." advice is correct) and when an engine's `capabilities.yml` was incomplete or invalid (a first-party developer added a schema key, so every engine's table is now missing a row — a dev-time build failure, where reinstalling changes nothing). Split: `EnginePluginError` keeps the failed-import meaning and its reinstall advice; the new `CapabilityDeclarationError` (an `AqueductError`, `aqueduct/errors.py`) covers a leaf with no row, a row still on the `undeclared` sentinel, a row naming a leaf that does not exist, an illegal verdict, and a malformed `requires` specifier. It names the offending leaves in the message and carries them structurally (`.leaves`, `.engine`, `.path`), and gives the fix that works: run `aqueduct dev capabilities sync`, then declare a verdict per engine. No reinstall advice. `load_engines()`'s deliberately-broad `except Exception` now re-raises `CapabilityDeclarationError` unchanged rather than re-wrapping it (which would have swapped the leaf names back for reinstall advice), and `aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py::check_capabilities` catches both by TYPE — a declaration error is reported as a `fail` naming the leaves, not misfiled as "blueprint did not parse/compile". Callers branch by exception type, never by message substring. (`aqueduct/errors.py`, `aqueduct/executor/capabilities.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_error_split.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_closure.py`)
- **The capability closure guarantee was a tautology — a new schema key without a per-engine verdict did NOT fail the build (Phase 78).** `docs/specs.md` §10.9 and AGENTS.md both promised that adding a grammar leaf or `aqueduct.yml` key without declaring per-engine support is a build failure. It was not. Spark's capability table was generated by `all_leaves_default(all_leaves(), SUPPORTED)` — derived from the **same** leaf walker the closure test then compared it against. Two sides, one source: they could never disagree, so every newly-added grammar or config leaf was silently swept into `supported` and `tests/test_capabilities/test_closure.py` passed regardless. Verified by planting a field in `parser/schema.py` and another in `config.py`: both passed the closure test. A guard that cannot fail is worse than no guard, since it is trusted. Fixed by making the two sources independent: **engine declarations are now YAML data with one explicit row per leaf** (`aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml` — 261 rows: 160 grammar + 101 config, each carrying a verdict plus the optional `requires`/`hint` the `Capability` model already supported), the default-verdict sweep is **deleted** (`all_leaves_default()` is gone; a regression test fails the build if any equivalent returns), and the closure test now compares the walker against each engine's YAML **read from disk**. `load_declaration()` hard-validates at registration and raises `EnginePluginError` on an unknown leaf id, an illegal verdict, a malformed version specifier, a leaf with no row, or a row still on the new `undeclared` sentinel. `undeclared` is deliberately NOT a synonym for `unsupported` — "not yet decided" and "this engine cannot do it" are different states, and conflating them is how the bug arose. A planted grammar leaf and a planted config key now each fail the build with a message naming the leaf and the fix. (`aqueduct/executor/capabilities.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/capabilities.yml`, `scripts/capabilities.py`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_closure.py`)
- **The engine capability gate was a no-op in production (Phase 78 Step 1).** Nothing on the CLI/compile path imported `aqueduct.executor.spark.capabilities` — only the test suite did. `CAPABILITY_REGISTRY` was therefore empty at runtime, `get_capabilities("spark")` raised `KeyError`, and `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py::check_capabilities` swallowed it into an empty problem list — the live symptom was `aqueduct doctor` reporting `capabilities: skip — no capability declaration registered for engine 'spark'` on every blueprint. Harmless while Spark declares default-`supported` for the whole grammar, but it would have silently disabled the default-`unsupported` gate the first non-Spark engine relies on. Fixed by the `aqueduct.engines` entry-point registration seam above: `get_capabilities()` now populates the registry itself before any lookup and raises `UnknownEngineError` (not a swallowed `KeyError`) for a genuinely unregistered engine, so both the compile-time gate and the doctor check fail loudly instead of degrading to a silent pass. Because that validation is fail-closed, the empty-registry state gets its own diagnosis: an install whose metadata predates the entry-point declaration reports "no execution engines are registered at all … reinstall the package" rather than an alarming and misleading "Registered engines: []" on every `aqueduct.yml` load. (`aqueduct/errors.py`, `aqueduct/executor/capabilities.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/engine.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/capability_check.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`; tests: `tests/test_capabilities/test_engine_registration.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_closure.py`, `tests/test_capabilities/test_doctor_capabilities.py`, `tests/test_config.py`, `tests/test_parser/test_config.py`)

## [2.0.4] — 2026-07-12

### Fixed
- **Spark Connect failure-mode honesty (3 fixes).** Follow-up to the compatibility audit below — hardens the three call sites it flagged as unguarded/misleading, without adding any Connect support. (1) `channel.py::_apply_metrics_boundary` now guards `df.rdd.getNumPartitions()`; on a session without the RDD API (e.g. Connect) the metrics boundary is skipped with a `[runtime_metrics_boundary_skipped]` warning instead of aborting the module — the transform result still flows through unchanged. (2) `patch/explain_gate.py`'s Gate 4: `capture_plan_snapshot` now stamps `plan_available: False` when plan-text capture returns empty (both `_jdf` paths failed); `run_explain_gate` reports `status="skip"` ("plan capture unavailable on this session — gate skipped") instead of comparing all-zero "after" counts against a real baseline, which previously read as a spurious plan regression. (3) `warnings/jar_availability.py::_loaded_jar_names` now returns `None` when JVM introspection fails (e.g. `sparkContext` unavailable under Connect), distinguishable from `[]` (genuinely no JARs loaded); `check()` emits a "could not verify JAR availability" note when a JAR-requiring format is declared and inspection failed, instead of silently reporting an all-clear. `docs/compatibility.md`'s Spark Connect table updated to reflect all three sites now degrading gracefully. (`aqueduct/executor/spark/channel.py`, `aqueduct/patch/explain_gate.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/warnings/jar_availability.py`, `docs/compatibility.md`; tests: `tests/test_executor/test_channel_metrics_boundary_guard.py`, `tests/test_patch/test_explain_gate.py`, `tests/test_executor/test_jar_availability_warning.py`)

### Docs
- **Gallery snippet + doc sync for recent features.** `gallery/snippets/42_hooks_lifecycle/` gains a `when_error:` filter example and an `in_process: true` blueprint-hook example, plus a README note on the `on_patch_pending`/`on_healed` events. `gallery/snippets/28_delta_merge_retry/` gains a per-module `retry:` override example (a module-level block inheriting the blueprint-level `retry_policy:`, distinct from the heal-time `on_failure:` replacement target). `gallery/snippets/39_checkpoint_resume/README.md` gains a note on the `checkpoint_root:` aqueduct.yml override (local paths only). `gallery/snippets/27_self_healing/aqueduct.yml` gains a commented `regression_artifact: true` example with a one-paragraph explainer. `docs/specs.md` §8.10's registry section rewritten to present tense (the MCP server and the agentic ToolBox both exist now, not "a future MCP server and an agentic ToolBox") with an explicit contrast: the internal heal LLM receives tools in-process via `ToolBox`/the provider API request and never speaks MCP; MCP is the transport for external clients only — no specs Version bump (wording fix, no contract change). `SKILL.md` gains a short "Diagnostics" signpost pointing at `aqueduct mcp serve` and the CLI equivalents (`report`/`runs`/`lineage`/`blueprint history`/`doctor`). (`gallery/snippets/42_hooks_lifecycle/blueprint.yml`, `gallery/snippets/42_hooks_lifecycle/README.md`, `gallery/snippets/28_delta_merge_retry/blueprint.yml`, `gallery/snippets/28_delta_merge_retry/README.md`, `gallery/snippets/39_checkpoint_resume/README.md`, `gallery/snippets/27_self_healing/aqueduct.yml`, `docs/specs.md`, `SKILL.md`, `AGENTS.md`)

### Added
- **Spark Connect compatibility audit.** New `skills/aqskill-audit-connect.md` (detection commands, exception-path tracing method, known-legitimate exceptions) plus a verified "Spark Connect" section in `docs/compatibility.md` documenting today's behavior — no runtime changes, no Connect support added. Six call sites reach into JVM/py4j-only internals (`spark._jvm`, `spark._jsc`, `df.rdd`, `df._jdf`, `sparkContext._jsc`); four degrade gracefully behind existing `try/except` guards, one (`patch/explain_gate.py`'s Gate 4 plan-regression check) degrades to a *misleading* result (empty plan text reads as a spurious regression rather than "unavailable"), and one (`channel.py`'s opt-in `metrics_boundary: true` config) breaks outright via an unguarded `df.rdd.getNumPartitions()`. `DataFrame.observe()`/`Observation` (row-count metrics) verified as already Connect-native — no `SparkListener` registration exists anywhere in the tree despite comments referencing it. (`skills/aqskill-audit-connect.md`, `docs/compatibility.md`)
- **Progressive (chained) multi-patch healing (`agent.progressive`).** Fixes a recorded live failure: the pre-existing `max_patches > 1` loop's `full_run` validation discards a candidate patch that still leaves the pipeline failing and re-executes against the ORIGINAL unpatched Blueprint next iteration — with 2+ independent bugs, a correct fix for bug #1 is generated and thrown away on every attempt because the pipeline still fails downstream at bug #2.
