New opt-in `agent.progressive: bool = false` + `agent.max_chain: int = 3` (engine `aqueduct.yml` + per-blueprint override, same `is not None` inheritance shape as `agent.regression_artifact`/`agent.mode`) — `max_patches` semantics are UNCHANGED (N independent retries of the SAME failure). New `aqueduct/agent/progressive.py`: a pure orchestrator (`run_progressive_chain`) that folds a validated-but-still-failing candidate into an accumulating multi-op `PatchSpec` when the NEW failure surfaces at a DIFFERENT module than the one just patched (the advancement test); the same module failing again ends the chain as "stuck". Nothing is written to the Blueprint until the FULL accumulated patch passes the pipeline end-to-end — every intermediate step reuses the existing in-memory apply path (`_apply_patch_in_memory`) re-applied against the original on-disk Blueprint, never a partial write. Progressive healing refuses to run with `agent.sandbox_mode: off` (`ConfigError` at heal-start — each link's advancement test IS the sandbox-backed in-memory validation). Wired into `aqueduct/cli/run.py`'s heal loop for the single-model `agent.approval: auto` (non-cascade) path; cascade-mode progressive interaction is deferred (see specs.md §8.13 scope note). `heal_attempts` gains a `chain_link` column (1-based link index; NULL for non-progressive attempts) via `_HEAL_ATTEMPTS_MIGRATIONS` in `aqueduct/surveyor/ddl.py`. Setting `agent.progressive: true` on a combo the current wiring doesn't chain is never a silent no-op: `approval: human`/`ci` or configured cascade tiers emit one `[agent_progressive_scope]` warning naming the exact disabling condition before falling back to the standard loop, and a replay-cache hit prints an info note (nothing lost — the cached patch served the failure for zero tokens, the chain had no work to do). Bumps specs.md to Version 2.15 (new §8.13). (`aqueduct/agent/progressive.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/ddl.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`, `aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`; docs: specs.md §8.13, SKILL.md, observability_guide.md; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_progressive.py`, `tests/test_parser/test_progressive_schema.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_progressive.py`, `tests/test_surveyor/test_aggressive_telemetry.py`)

## [2.0.3] — 2026-07-12

### Fixed
- **`heal_attempts` schema migration for pre-existing observability databases.** The new `tool_calls_json` column only existed in the `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` DDL, which never adds columns to an already-created table — on an upgraded install every heal-attempt INSERT failed into a swallowed warning, silently dropping all heal-attempt telemetry. Surveyor init now runs idempotent `ALTER TABLE heal_attempts ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` migrations (`_HEAL_ATTEMPTS_MIGRATIONS` in `aqueduct/surveyor/ddl.py`) right after the CREATE; the rule (new column → CREATE **and** migrations tuple) is documented at the constant. (`aqueduct/surveyor/ddl.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `docs/observability_guide.md`; test: `tests/test_surveyor/test_aggressive_telemetry.py::test_heal_attempts_column_migration_on_existing_db`)

### Added
- **Agentic heal — a tool-use loop for self-healing (`agent.mode: agentic`).** Opt-in (default `agent.mode: oneshot` — behaviour unchanged) alternative to the single prompt→PatchSpec turn: the model can call READ-ONLY diagnostic tools before answering — the Phase 73 ToolRegistry (`list_runs`, `run_detail`, `lineage`, `patch_list`, `patch_show`, `probe_signals`, `blueprint_history`) plus three new heal-context tools (`read_blueprint`, `get_source_schema`, `sample_rows`) — via a new per-heal `aqueduct/agent/toolbox.py::ToolBox`. Every tool result is redacted before it can enter a prompt. `agent.max_tool_calls` (default 8) hard-caps tool calls per heal ATTEMPT (a reprompt gets its own fresh cap); exceeding it withdraws tools, forcing a final PatchSpec-only turn. `agent.supports_tools` (`auto`/`true`/`false`, per-blueprint and per-cascade-tier override) resolves `anthropic` as tool-capable without probing; `openai_compat` probes on the first call and degrades to the oneshot path for the rest of that heal session on a tools-related rejection, emitting one `[agent_tools_unsupported]` warning — never a ReAct/text-emulation fallback. `providers.py` gained multi-turn tool-use dispatch for both providers (Anthropic `tool_use`/`tool_result`, OpenAI `tool_calls`/`role: tool`) behind a `ToolCallState` that reports tool-call count/capability back to the loop without changing `_call_agent`'s stable 3-tuple return. Tool-call tokens flow into the existing `BudgetTracker` axes; `heal_attempts` gained a `tool_calls_json` column (per-call name/args/duration/redacted-preview log) so the diagnosis path is auditable post-hoc. Transcript verbose mode renders one line per tool call; terse mode adds a tool-call count. `aqueduct benchmark`/`run_scenario` can run `agent.mode: agentic` for a live oneshot-vs-agentic A/B. `PROMPT_VERSION` bumped 1.6 → 1.7 (the system prompt gains a "Tools available" addendum only when tools are actually offered). Bumps specs.md to Version 2.14 (new §8.12). (`aqueduct/agent/toolbox.py`, `aqueduct/agent/providers.py`, `aqueduct/agent/loop.py`, `aqueduct/agent/cascade.py`, `aqueduct/agent/prompts.py`, `aqueduct/agent/budget.py`, `aqueduct/agent/transcript.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/heal.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/benchmark.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/scenario.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/ddl.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`, `aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`; docs: specs.md §8.12, SKILL.md, observability_guide.md; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_toolbox.py`, `tests/test_agent/test_providers_tools.py`, `tests/test_agent/test_agentic_loop.py`, `tests/test_agent/test_transcript.py`, `tests/test_surveyor/test_scenario.py`)

## [2.0.2] — 2026-07-11

### Added
- **`aqueduct mcp serve` — stdio MCP server over the diagnostics ToolRegistry.** New `aqueduct/mcp/` package + `mcp` CLI group: exposes every registry tool (`list_runs`, `run_detail`, `lineage`, `patch_list`, `patch_show`, `probe_signals`, `doctor`, `blueprint_history`) over the Model Context Protocol on stdin/stdout, so MCP clients (Claude Desktop, IDEs) can query Aqueduct's observability store directly. Local-only in v1 — no network transport, no ports; the client spawns the command as a subprocess. Read-only stays structural (the server imports only the registry, never a store write API — a test greps for write markers) and every invocation goes through `call_tool()` (the redaction chokepoint); handler exception messages are additionally `redact()`-scrubbed before the SDK converts them to structured `isError` tool results, which never terminate the server loop. `--config <aqueduct.yml>` is injected into every tool call that accepts `config_path` (a client-supplied value wins). `name`/`description`/`params_schema` are handed through verbatim as the MCP tool declaration — no translation layer. New optional **`mcp` dev-tooling extra** (`pip install aqueduct-core[mcp]`, `mcp>=1.9`) — same sanctioned out-of-`all` carve-out as `tui`/`dashboard`; the CLI guards on `find_spec("mcp")` with an install hint, and top-level `import aqueduct.mcp` never pulls the SDK. Bumps specs.md to Version 2.13 (§8.10 MCP paragraph); roadmap.md's MCP entry reconciled to "shipped read-only; write tools + network transport still deferred". New `mcp-tests` CI job. (`aqueduct/mcp/server.py`, `aqueduct/mcp/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/mcp.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `.github/workflows/test-suite.yml`; docs: specs.md §8.10, cli_reference.md, roadmap.md; tests: `tests/test_mcp/test_server.py` — pure declaration tests run SDK-free, in-memory client/server session tests gate on `pytest.importorskip("mcp")`; `tests/test_cli/test_cli_mcp.py`)
- **Internal, read-only diagnostics ToolRegistry (`aqueduct/tools/`).** A `Tool` frozen dataclass (`name`, `description`, `params_schema`, `handler`, `read_only: bool = True` — structurally always True in v1, no tool receives a write handle) plus a `REGISTRY`/`register()`/`get_tools()` table and a single `call_tool(name, **kwargs)` call path that runs every result through `aqueduct.redaction.redact()` before returning it. Eight built-in tools, each a thin wrapper over `aqueduct/stores/queries.py` (never inline SQL): `list_runs`, `run_detail`, `lineage`, `patch_list`, `patch_show`, `probe_signals`, `doctor`, `blueprint_history`. This is the enumeration surface a future MCP server / agentic ToolBox will read from (roadmap.md); a reserved `AQ_TOOLS_ENTRYPOINT_GROUP = "aqueduct.tools"` constant names the future custom-tool entry-point group (mirrors `executor/probe_plugins.py`'s probe-signal precedent) but is not resolved anywhere yet. Bumps specs.md to Version 2.12 (new §8.10). (`aqueduct/tools/registry.py`, `aqueduct/tools/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`; docs: specs.md §8.10; tests: `tests/test_tools/test_registry.py`)
- **Remediation-domains framing in specs.md (§8.11) + roadmap entry.** Self-healing is now described as operating in explicit "remediation domains" — today the pipeline-definition domain (PatchSpec ops on the Blueprint) is the only one — each following the same shape: declarative typed operations, per-domain validation gates, never freeform code execution, domain-aware read-only diagnostics. Future domains (engine-config, infra) are sketched in `docs/roadmap.md` only, no promised features in specs.md. (`docs/specs.md` §8.11; `docs/roadmap.md`)
- **`aqueduct blueprint history <id|blueprint.yml>`.** New `blueprint` CLI group (`aqueduct/cli/blueprint.py`) with a read-only `history` subcommand: a chronological remediation timeline for one blueprint merging the observability store's `patch_index`/`healing_outcomes`/`heal_attempts` (heal run starts, `PATCH_APPLY` with confidence, run outcome, `PATCH_REJECT`) with the blueprint file's git commit history when it is git-tracked — a commit with no `---aqueduct---` trailer renders as `manual_edit`. New query functions in `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`: `blueprint_history`, `patch_show`, `git_blueprint_commits` (the only one that shells out to `git log`; handles a non-repo gracefully, never raises). `--format json` via `emit()`; also registered as the `blueprint_history` diagnostics tool (dogfoods the new ToolRegistry). (`aqueduct/cli/blueprint.py`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `aqueduct/tools/registry.py`; docs: cli_reference.md, observability_guide.md cookbook; tests: `tests/test_stores/test_blueprint_history.py`, `tests/test_cli/test_cli_blueprint_history.py`)

## [2.0.1] — 2026-07-11

### Added
- **Hooks v2: `on_patch_pending`/`on_healed` events, `when_error:` filters, in-process `blueprint:` hooks.** Extends `hooks:` (additive, backward-compatible) three ways: (1) two new events mirroring the engine-level `webhooks:` heal-loop vocabulary — `on_patch_pending` fires every time a heal stages a patch for human/CI review (all three staging sites: guardrail-blocked, `approval: human`, `approval: ci`), `on_healed` fires once a heal's re-run succeeds (patch applied AND green again), always before the outer run's terminal `on_success` hooks; (2) `when_error: [ErrorType, ...]` on `on_failure`/`on_patch_pending`/`on_healed` entries filters firing by the run's `error_type` / stack-trace exception class — same exact-match candidate set as `agent.guardrails.heal_on_errors`; setting it on `on_success` (no failure context there) is a schema error, not a silent no-op; (3) `in_process: true` on `blueprint:` entries parses+compiles+executes the target Blueprint in THIS process, reusing the caller's live SparkSession, instead of spawning an `aqueduct run` subprocess — no self-healing loop for the in-process target (out of scope: the heal loop lives in `aqueduct run`'s CLI orchestration, not reusable as a bare library call), falls back to the subprocess path with an info message when the target sets its own `spark_config` (a live-session config merge isn't generally safe), and recursively chains the target's own `on_success`/`on_failure` hooks (same session, explicit in-memory ancestor chain — the `AQUEDUCT_HOOK_CHAIN` env var is process-scoped and doesn't see in-process calls) so cycle/depth guards still hold. `aqueduct doctor`'s static hook-cycle walk now covers all four events. (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/cli/hooks.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`; docs: specs.md §Hooks (bumped to Version 2.11), SKILL.md; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_hooks.py`, `tests/test_parser/test_hooks_schema.py`)
- **Opt-in post-heal regression artifact (`agent.regression_artifact`).** A SUCCESSFUL heal (patch applied AND the re-run succeeded) can now optionally emit an auto-generated `.aqtest.yml` under `aqtests/` next to the Blueprint, run by `aqueduct test`. New `aqueduct/agent/regression_artifact.py::generate()` builds a fixture from the patched module's upstream `schema_hint`(s) and a smoke assertion, wired in after `_write_patch_to_blueprint` succeeds in `auto` mode's full-run success path (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`). This is a **different job** from the signature-memory heal cache (§8.1): the cache resolves the SAME failure RECURRING in production for zero tokens (acts AFTER recurrence); this artifact is a CI regression test that catches the fix being UNDONE — a hand-edit, a SQL refactor, a revert — BEFORE the next production run, with no failure and no heal needed (documented side-by-side in specs.md §8.9, new comparison table). Generation is conservative: it silently skips (info-level message, no file) whenever the healed failure/module shape can't be expressed as a valid aqtest — a multi-module patch, a non-testable module type (Ingress/Egress), or an upstream module with no `schema_hint` to synthesize fixture rows from — and never overwrites an existing file. Default `false`; per-blueprint override inherits the engine default like `block_on_explain_regression`. Bumps specs.md to Version 2.10. (`aqueduct/agent/regression_artifact.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`, `aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`; docs: specs.md §8.9; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_regression_artifact.py`, `tests/test_parser/test_regression_artifact_schema.py`)
- **Heal-cache signature normalization: backtick-quoted identifiers collapse too.** `aqueduct/agent/signature.py::_normalize_message` already collapsed `'…'`/`"…"`-quoted values, filesystem paths, and digits so failures differing only in specifics hash to one signature — it missed Spark 4's backtick identifier quoting (e.g. `` `i`.`bad_col` `` in `UNRESOLVED_COLUMN` messages), so two typo'd column names produced two signatures and duplicate heal runs. New `_BACKTICK_RE` collapses `` `...` `` spans to `` `X` ``, same placeholder style as the quote rules. **Side effect**: existing heal-cache entries re-hash under the new signature and are effectively orphaned — safe, because every cache replay is re-validated through the sandbox gate before it lands. (`aqueduct/agent/signature.py`, `docs/observability_guide.md`; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_signature.py`)
- **Agent prompt rule: `schema_hint` type-mismatch / field-not-found guidance.** The LLM was observed proposing a no-op `set_module_config_key` re-setting a `schema_hint` to its already-current value when shown `schema_hint type mismatch on 'X': expected 'A', actual 'B'` — it didn't realize `expected` IS the current declared value. New curated rule in `_SYSTEM_PROMPT_TEMPLATE` spells out that `expected` = current schema_hint (re-proposing it is a no-op), `actual` = what Spark inferred from the data, and the fix is changing the hint to `actual` (or deferring if `actual` is a genuine data problem); a companion rule covers `schema_hint field 'X' not found in source schema` (align the key to a real column from the listed `Available columns`, never re-type the missing key). `PROMPT_VERSION` bumped `1.4` → `1.5` per the system-prompt-body bump policy. (`aqueduct/agent/prompts.py`, `aqueduct/agent/loop.py`; tests: `tests/test_agent/test_prompts.py`)
- **Docs pack: gate-sequence callout, gate-output polish, TOCs, lineage `--chain` discoverability.** README's "The Healing Flow" section and specs.md §8.7 now both name the 4-gate patch pyramid explicitly (`✓ guardrails → ✓ lineage → ✓ sandbox → ✓ plan-regression → patch applied`) with a one-line description of each gate. `aqueduct patch preview`'s gate-status output now routes through the `style.py` vocabulary (✓/✗/⚠/· instead of a bare `status: <value>` line) for visual consistency with the rest of the CLI. `docs/specs.md` and `docs/spark_guide.md` gain an inline Table of Contents at the top; specs.md §2 gains a one-line note disambiguating the three version spaces (Blueprint grammar `1.0`, specs.md document version, package version). `docs/cli_reference.md`'s `lineage --chain --types` row gains a worked example of the per-hop transform trace; `docs/observability_guide.md`'s `column_lineage` section cross-references it as the deeper on-demand alternative to the stored table. (`README.md`, `docs/specs.md`, `docs/spark_guide.md`, `docs/cli_reference.md`, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `aqueduct/cli/patch.py`; tests: `tests/test_cli/test_cli_patch_preview.py`)
- **Per-module `retry:` override.** Blueprint grammar gains a module-level `retry:` block that overrides the top-level `retry_policy:` field-by-field — any field left unset inherits the blueprint-level value (same per-field inheritance shape as `agent.cascade` tiers). `backoff:` overrides as a whole block, not merged field-by-field. Distinct from the pre-existing `on_failure` field (an internal write target the self-healing agent uses via `set_module_on_failure`/`replace_retry_policy` patch ops); precedence at run time is `on_failure` (heal-time) > `retry:` (authoring-time) > blueprint `retry_policy:`. (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/models.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/executor.py`; docs: specs.md §4 (version bumped to 2.9), SKILL.md, `blueprint.yml.template`)
- **Retry idempotency exposure matrix (docs).** New "Retry idempotency — half-write exposure by module" table in the Production Guide's Delta section: Delta (any mode) = safe, parquet/csv overwrite = safe-ish, parquet/csv append and JDBC append = NOT SAFE (duplicates on retry), Delta MERGE = idempotent by construction. Cross-references the existing `delivery_append_retry_dupes` compiler warning. (`docs/production_guide.md`)
- **Per-endpoint webhook `health_probe` — `doctor` no longer POSTs to your webhooks by default.** New `WebhookEndpointConfig.health_probe: connect | options | full` field (default **`options`** — a **behaviour change**: `aqueduct doctor` used to send a real POST with a synthetic `doctor_probe` payload to every configured webhook, which could trigger consumer-side side effects; it now sends a non-mutating HTTP OPTIONS request by default, any response including 405 counting as reachable). `connect` drops to TCP/TLS-only reachability (no HTTP request sent at all); `full` restores the old real-POST behaviour. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`)
- **`aqueduct validate` runs the static hook-cycle check.** Reuses `aqueduct/cli/hooks.py::static_hook_check` (the same graph walk `doctor` already ran) so a `hooks:` block with a cycle, excessive chain depth, or a missing `blueprint:` target now surfaces at `validate` time too — as a suppressible warning (`[aqueduct:hook_cycle]`, honours `warnings.suppress`), not a hard validation failure. (`aqueduct/cli/diagnostics.py`)
- **`checkpoint_root` engine-config override.** New top-level `aqueduct.yml` key overrides the derived `<store_dir>/checkpoints/` location for module checkpoint/resume state (both a fresh run's checkpoint write and a `--resume` reload). LOCAL FILESYSTEM PATHS ONLY — a remote URI scheme (`s3://`, `s3a://`, `gs://`, `hdfs://`, `abfss://`, ...) is rejected at config-load with an actionable error pointing at the "Remote-Filesystem Checkpoint Root" roadmap item, instead of the previous silent `Path("s3://…")` mangling. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/executor.py`; docs: specs.md §10.4.2, production_guide.md, roadmap.md, `aqueduct.yml.template`)

### Fixed
- **`blueprint.yml.template`'s blueprint-level `retry_policy:` example didn't parse.** The commented example used flat `backoff_strategy`/`backoff_base_seconds`/`backoff_max_seconds` keys, but the schema (`RetryPolicySchema.backoff`) nests them under a `backoff:` sub-block — uncommenting the example raised `Extra inputs are not permitted`. Fixed alongside the new per-module `retry:` template addition. (`aqueduct/templates/default/blueprints/blueprint.yml.template`)

## [2.0.0] — 2026-06-30

> ⚠️ **Breaking changes** (see **Removed** + **Changed**): the deprecated `agent.approval_mode` / `agent.aggressive_max_patches` / `danger.allow_aggressive_patching` keys and the `--allow-aggressive` flag now **error** instead of warning; the inert `stores.lineage` block is removed; and `engine: flink` is now **rejected** at config-load. A `2.0.0`-worthy batch.

### Added
- **`doctor`: cascade tiers get the same reachability proof the main agent gets, plus a model-count + remote-master DuckDB nudge.** A `agent.cascade:` openai_compat tier was only checked for *base_url presence* (so it showed `✓` even when the endpoint was down) — under `--preflight` it now pings `/v1/models` and confirms the tier's model is actually loaded (reachable + N models, else warn). The main agent check now also reports `(N models available)` and **warns** (was a soft note) when the selected model isn't loaded. And `cluster-stores` now nudges at `env=local` when the master is **remote** and DuckDB paths are relative — fine for client mode, but lost under `--deploy-mode cluster`. (`aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`)
- **`doctor --preflight`: store write round-trip + real JDBC connect.** `--preflight` now (c) does a throwaway **write+read** against the observability/depot store (duckdb temp table, postgres temp table, redis SET/GET/DEL) — proving write *permissions*, not just connect; and (d) for `jdbc:` sources, attempts a **real connect+auth** (postgres via psycopg2, using the source `user`/`password`) instead of only the TCP-port probe, degrading to "TCP only" when no python driver matches the subprotocol. Both non-fatal. (`aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`)
- **`doctor --preflight`: deeper opt-in checks — agent auth-ping + UDF import.** `--preflight` now (a) for `agent.provider: anthropic`, proves the API key + endpoint actually work via `GET /v1/models` (free, no tokens) instead of only checking the key is *set* (openai_compat already lists models); and (b) **imports each Python `udf_registry` entry** (`module` + `entry`) so a typo or missing dependency is caught at doctor time, not mid-run (java/jar UDFs skipped). Both are preflight-only (the UDF import executes module top-level code), non-fatal (warn/skip). (`aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`)
- **`doctor`: Spark version handshake is now a real major.minor comparison + a Java-runtime check.** Under `--preflight` the cluster Spark vs client pyspark comparison was major-only, so a `3.4`-driver/`3.5`-cluster split (a common cause of cryptic mid-job serialization/RPC errors) passed silently — it now warns on any **major.minor** difference (extracted as the pure, testable `_spark_version_verdict`). New always-on **`check_java`** reports the JVM Spark will launch (`JAVA_HOME` or PATH `java`, parsed via `_parse_java_major` — handles `1.8`→8 and `17.0.x`→17) so a wrong JDK is visible up front (plain `doctor` builds no session, so a Java mismatch otherwise only surfaces as a cryptic `--preflight` failure), with a single non-matrix nudge (pyspark 4 needs Java 17+). No curated compatibility tables. (`aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/diagnostics.py`)
- **Live token streaming for self-healing (SSE).** Provider calls now stream when run on an interactive terminal: the agent's response (and a reasoning model's "thinking" channel) renders live as it generates, so a slow/local model no longer looks hung. Both provider shapes are supported — OpenAI-compatible (`reasoning_content` + `content` deltas) and Anthropic (`thinking_delta` + `text_delta`) — via `client.stream` + SSE parsing in `aqueduct/agent/providers.py`, threaded through `generate_agent_patch` / `generate_cascade_patch` as an `on_token(kind, text)` sink. Default shows a compact in-place `⏳ thinking… N chars` meter; `-v` streams the actual thinking/answer text under a `┆` gutter. **Streaming is interactive-TTY-only** — piped/CI runs keep the non-streaming POST path unchanged (so existing behaviour, retries, and tests are unaffected). Tokens still come from the provider's usage frame. (`aqueduct/agent/providers.py`, `aqueduct/agent/loop.py`, `aqueduct/agent/cascade.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`; mocked SSE tests in `tests/test_agent/test_streaming.py`)
- **Live healing-conversation display (`-v`).** Turn-by-turn agent transcript powered by `aqueduct/agent/transcript.py` (engine-agnostic, no click/pyspark). Default is terse (`· attempt 2 · tier 1 (deepseek) · patch rejected (sandbox)`); `-v` (verbose) unfolds the full turn — patch details (ops, rationale, root cause, confidence), gate verdict, reprompt reason, tokens/cost per attempt, cascade tier labels, escalation markers. Shared by `run`, `heal`, and the benchmark flow via the `on_attempt` hook; all output routes through `cli/output.py` for colour + redaction.
- **Consolidated CLI output funnel + runtime warning codes.** New `aqueduct/cli/output.py` composes styling + redaction + `--format` rendering into one entry point (`emit(data, fmt=…, redact=…)`, `warn(rule_id, msg, …)`); the `--format json` paths across `report`/`runs`/`lineage`/`benchmark`/`validate`/`lint`/`patch`/`heal`/`drift` route through it (consistent, un-styled JSON), and risky value-interpolating output (config inspector, store paths/DSNs) is redacted at the point of printing. Every runtime probe/assert/executor warning now carries a stable `[runtime_*]` rule_id (greppable, parallel to compile rule_ids), and runtime warnings are **attached to the module that produced them** — printed nested under that module's `✓` line (`↳ ⚠ [rule_id] …`) instead of as loose lines. Per-module collection uses a `threading.local` collector (parallel-component safe), reset at each module boundary.
- **`aqueduct doctor --preflight` verifies cloud object existence.** For `s3a://`/`gs://`/`abfss://` Ingress/Egress paths, preflight now checks the object/prefix actually exists via Spark's Hadoop `FileSystem` — reusing the run's exact s3a/gcs/adls credentials from the (already-built) preflight session — so a typo'd cloud path or bad credentials is caught before a long run instead of failing mid-execution. Default `doctor` is unchanged (only endpoint TCP reachability, no creds/network per-object cost).
- **Webhook hardening — HMAC signing, configurable retries, delivery IDs.** Any `webhooks.*` endpoint gains three optional fields: `secret` (HMAC-SHA256-signs the exact request body and adds an `X-Aqueduct-Signature: sha256=<digest>` header so receivers can verify authenticity/integrity), `max_retries` (default 1; bounded retry on 429/5xx/network, exponential when > 1), and `backoff_seconds` (default 2.0). Every delivery also carries an `X-Aqueduct-Delivery: <uuid>` header for receiver-side dedup. Endpoint auth via header templating (`Authorization: Bearer ${TOKEN}`) is unchanged.
- **`aqueduct/infra/http.py` — shared outbound-HTTP transport.** One source of truth for retryable-status sets, `Retry-After` parsing, exponential backoff, HMAC signing, and fire-and-forget daemon delivery. The webhook + OpenLineage paths (previously near-duplicate inline loops) and the LLM-provider retry now build on it.
- **`not_null` per-row Assert rule.** Quarantine-able — routes null rows to the spillway port with zero extra Spark actions. Companion to `null_rate` (population-level sampled alarm); choose `not_null` when you want to drop null rows, `null_rate` when you want a sampled-gate alarm.
- **Engine-level `agent.cascade` default in `aqueduct.yml`.** A project-wide multi-model healing cascade (blueprint `agent.cascade` overrides). Resolves ISSUE-038 (cascade scope).
- **Configurable `agent.api_key`** (engine, blueprint, and per-cascade-tier). Resolved from `@aq.secret()`/`${ENV}`/literal with env-var fallback. Plaintext literals in `aqueduct.yml` emit an `insecure_api_key` warning and are redacted from logs and LLM payloads. Precedence: per-cascade-tier → blueprint `agent.api_key` → engine `agent.api_key` → env var (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_API_KEY`).
- **`aqueduct doctor`** now accepts `-v` as a short alias for `--verbose`.
- **Per-blueprint depot isolation + named depot mounts (`stores.depots`).** The depot config is now a **name-keyed map**, `stores.depots`, replacing the single `stores.depot` block (legacy mapping auto-migrates to `depots.default` with a deprecation warning; `cfg.stores.depot` stays as a back-compat accessor for the default mount). Every mount is **per-blueprint key-isolated by default** — keys are transparently prefixed with `blueprint_id`, fixing the silent cross-blueprint collision on `_last_run_id` / watermarks when a depot is physically shared. Opt a mount into deliberate **cross-blueprint sharing** with `shared: true` (raw keys), read via `@aq.depot.<name>.get('key')`; the default mount is `@aq.depot.get('key')`. Mounts are dot-addressable for overrides: `--set stores.depots.<name>.backend=postgres`. For parallel writers on a shared mount use postgres/redis (concurrent), not a single DuckDB file. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/stores/base.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/runtime.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `docs/specs.md`, `docs/observability_guide.md`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`)
- **`@aq.*` subject-grouped namespaces + pipeline identity / deployment context.** Tier-1 functions are now grouped by *what the value is about*, not by an internal dynamic-vs-static split: **`@aq.run.*`** (`id()`, `timestamp()`, `prev_id()` — renamed from `@aq.runtime.*`), **`@aq.blueprint.*`** (`id()`, `name()`, `dir()`, `path()` — new), **`@aq.deployment.*`** (`env()`, `target()` — new), **`@aq.version()`** (engine version — new), alongside the existing `@aq.date.*` / `@aq.depot.*` / `@aq.secret` / `@aq.env`. `@aq.blueprint.dir()` is the reproducible "relative-to-this-pipeline" path anchor (e.g. `path: @aq.blueprint.dir()/out`); `cwd`/user/host are deliberately **not** exposed (non-reproducible across laptop ↔ CI ↔ driver ↔ cluster). Resolved pre-job on the driver with full provenance tagging; `blueprint`/`run` always available at compile, `deployment` threaded from the run config.
- **Resolution-scope rule + config guard (specs §5.3.1).** Documented that `@aq.*` resolves at two points with different scope availability: **`aqueduct.yml`** gets only `${ENV}` + `@aq.secret()` (loaded before any Blueprint/run exists), while **Blueprint compile** gets the full `@aq.*` (deployment ⊃ blueprint ⊃ run all in scope — *override-downstream, not propagate-uphill*). `load_config` now **rejects** any non-secret `@aq.*` in `aqueduct.yml` with a clear pointer instead of leaking an unresolved literal. (`aqueduct/compiler/runtime.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `docs/specs.md`, `SKILL.md`)
- **Phase 68 (part 3) — location-only observability routing (DuckDB).** `stores.observability.path` now distinguishes a **directory** from a **file**: a suffix-less custom path (e.g. `/mnt/aqueduct/obs`) is a *base directory* and gets the same per-blueprint routing as the default (`<dir>/<blueprint_id>/observability.db`) — so you get a custom location **and** parallel-safe per-blueprint files **and** DuckDB, all at once. A path ending in `.db` remains a single shared file (one store for all blueprints → not parallel-safe; DuckDB is single-writer). Both the write path and the read resolver honour the rule. New specs §10.4.1 documents the three layouts + the single-writer / same-pipeline-twice caveats + the short-lived-read rule; dynamic path templating (`@aq.date.*`) noted as planned. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/stores/read.py`, `docs/specs.md`, `aqueduct/templates/default/aqueduct.yml.template`)
- **Phase 68 (part 2) — `aqueduct dashboard` (local Streamlit observability viewer).** New `aqueduct dashboard` command launches a **local, read-only, on-demand** Streamlit dashboard (like the Spark UI — never a production server, never required by a pipeline) over the shared read layer: a **Fleet** view (cross-blueprint runs / success-rate / heal-attempts KPIs + table, runs-over-time line chart, failure-category bars), **Runs** (per-store run list → per-run module-metrics table in execution order), **Lineage** (Plotly **Sankey** of `source.column → output_column` + a row table), **Doctor**, and a read-only **Config** inspector (secret values never read). No sidebar, no buttons — **F5 refreshes**: the **Runs** tab is the hub (one table of *all* runs across blueprints, inline blueprint/status filters, native header sort, **click a row → detail below**); status is rendered as **real colored cells** (pandas Styler — no emoji); static tables use `st.table` (full-length, page-scroll, no chrome) while the interactive Runs table uses `st.dataframe`; the lineage Sankey uses high-contrast source/output colors with an inline blueprint picker; Doctor (structured `CheckResult`) is memoised per browser session. Every script run re-reads with short-lived connections (no background polling, no `cache_data`) so the viewer never blocks a pipeline's DuckDB writer. New optional `dashboard` extra (`streamlit`+`plotly`) — a documented dev-tooling carve-out (kept out of `[all]`, like `tui`); the base CLI never imports it. App verified headless via Streamlit `AppTest`. Expanded surfaces: a dedicated **Healing** tab (resolution distribution, gate-rejection counts, heal success-rate by failure category, heal-attempt details), **per-module metric trends** with a metric selector (duration / rows / bytes) on the Runs detail, a **column-quality** section and a **SQL fingerprint changelog** (per-channel canonical-SQL diff) on Lineage, and **gate-rejection** + **heal-coverage** rollups on Fleet. A failed run now shows a **full failure-detail** panel (complete error message + error class + offending object + suggested columns + stack trace from `failure_contexts`) instead of only the 200-char table preview. The fleet query layer is backend-portable and CI-covered on both DuckDB and Postgres. (`aqueduct/dashboard/`, `aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `aqueduct/cli/observability.py`, `pyproject.toml`, `docs/cli_reference.md`, `AGENTS.md`)
- **Phase 68 (part 1) — shared observability read layer + fleet aggregates.** New `aqueduct/stores/queries.py` is the single backend-agnostic read-time query layer behind every observability viewer (the frozen `studio` TUI now re-exports from it; the upcoming Streamlit dashboard and `report --json` will share it — no duplication). Adds **cross-run + cross-blueprint "fleet" aggregates** computed entirely at read time (DuckDB: iterate per-blueprint files + merge in Python; Postgres: group by `blueprint_id`) — `fleet_summary` (per-blueprint runs/success-rate/last-run/heal-attempts), `runs_over_time` (daily status counts), `failure_categories` — never materialised into a second copy. Reads are short-lived (open→query→close) so a viewer never blocks a running pipeline's DuckDB writer. Also: the `studio` resource profile is now in **execution order** (was slowest-first), consistent with the module-results view. (`aqueduct/stores/queries.py`, `aqueduct/tui/data.py`)
- **Phase 66 — `SKILL.md` LLM Blueprint-authoring guide + provider `base_url` docs.** A distilled, signal-dense authoring guide at the repo root (`SKILL.md`) teaching an LLM to write Blueprints + `aqueduct.yml`: top-level structure, the 9 module types with minimal examples, edges/ports + linear-edge sugar, the Context Registry (3 tiers), UDF registry, macros, the `agent:` self-healing block, common gotchas, a validated end-to-end worked example, and the authoring loop (write → `aqueduct validate`/`lint` → fix). Includes a table of known-good OpenAI-compatible `base_url`s (OpenAI, OpenRouter, DeepSeek, Groq, Gemini-compat, Ollama, LM Studio) with the correct auth contract (`anthropic` → `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; `openai_compat` → `OPENAI_API_KEY` for every endpoint). README LLM framing updated to "Anthropic native + any OpenAI-compatible". Registered in AGENTS.md as a documentation surface (doc map + change-trigger matrix) so grammar/agent/provider changes keep it in sync. (`SKILL.md`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `pyproject.toml`)
- **Phase 65 — Table-first addressing (`catalog.schema.table`).** Ingress and Egress modules can now address data by a catalog identifier (`table: catalog.schema.table`) instead of a filesystem `path:`. Ingress reads via `spark.read.table(table)`, Egress writes via `df.write.mode(...).saveAsTable(table)`. `table:` is mutually exclusive with `path:` on both Ingress and Egress — the engine raises a clear error if both are set. The catalog is configured through standard `spark.sql.catalog.*` properties in `spark_config` (external to Aqueduct — Unity Catalog, Hive, Iceberg REST, Glue, Polaris, etc.). `format:` is not required when `table:` is set on Ingress. `register_as_table` is ignored (logged as non-fatal warning) when an Egress already writes to a catalog table via `table:`. `time_travel` is not supported on table-addressed Ingress reads (use a Channel with `TIMESTAMP AS OF` SQL syntax). Doctor checks table existence via `spark.catalog.tableExists()`. Specs, templates, Spark Guide, and production guide updated. (`aqueduct/executor/spark/ingress.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/egress.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `docs/specs.md`, `docs/spark_guide.md`, `docs/production_guide.md`, `aqueduct/templates/.../blueprint.yml.template`)
- **Phase 63 — Execution targets: in-cluster (YARN / Kubernetes).** `deployment.target` is now validated against `master_url` at config-load. Each in-cluster target (`local`, `standalone`, `yarn`, `kubernetes`) enforces a matching `master_url` shape and raises a clear `ConfigError` on mismatch. Remote‑submit targets (`databricks`, `emr`, `dataproc`) are rejected with a "not yet supported (planned: Phase 64)" message — they remain in the `Literal` so pydantic recognises them but the validator blocks them before execution. `aqueduct doctor` now provides target‑specific guidance: yarn warns when `HADOOP_CONF_DIR`/`YARN_CONF_DIR` is unset; kubernetes TCP‑probes the API server and warns if no `spark.kubernetes.*` keys are configured. Specs, production guide, and template updated. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`, `docs/specs.md`, `docs/production_guide.md`)
- **Phase 64 — Remote-submit: Databricks Jobs API target.** `deployment.target: databricks` is now a **remote‑submit** execution path: the CLI packages the Blueprint, uploads it to DBFS, triggers a one‑shot `spark_python_task` job run, and polls to completion. The submitting machine needs no Spark — `aqueduct run` works from a laptop or CI runner. New `aqueduct/deploy/` package (engine‑agnostic, `pyspark`‑free) with a `Submitter` ABC and `DatabricksSubmitter` implementation powered by raw `httpx` (no SDK required). Config lives under the new nested `deployment.databricks:` block (`workspace_url`, `cluster_id` / `new_cluster`, `libraries`). A `databricks` optional extra (`databricks-sdk>=0.30`) is available for richer client features. (`aqueduct-core[databricks]`)
- **Remote‑target heal policy.** LLM self‑healing is **disabled** for remote‑submit targets with explicit stderr messaging. Any Blueprint `agent.approval_mode` setting is ignored. Failures exit `DATA_OR_RUNTIME(2)`. The `Submitter.fetch_failure_context()` hook is stubbed for a future release. (`deploy/base.py`)
- **Doctor: `check_remote_target`.** A new non‑fatal check verifies Databricks workspace URL reachability and `DATABRICKS_TOKEN` presence. Wired into `aqueduct doctor` before the Spark section. (`aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/__init__.py`)
- **Thin‑packaging contract.** Remote‑submit uploads only the Blueprint, `aqueduct.yml`, a bootstrap script, and referenced UDF files. The cluster must have `aqueduct-core[spark]` installed (Databricks library / EMR bootstrap / Dataproc init action). This avoids repackaging the entire engine and its transitive JVM/Python coupling inside the job artefact. (`docs/specs.md` §10.8)
- **Branded banner on the bare `aqueduct` command.** Running `aqueduct` with no subcommand now shows a small wordmark (red `aq` + sand `ueduct`, arch motif, version, tagline) above the help — reserved for the entrypoint only, never per-run. (`aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`)
- **Cleaner `aqueduct run` output.** The JVM/Spark startup banner (incubator notice, log4j profile lines, `NativeCodeLoader` warning) is suppressed by default — `make_spark_session(quiet_startup=…)` mutes only stderr around session creation, so genuine *runtime* Spark warnings still print. Pass `-v/--verbose` to restore the full banner. The run is framed with separators that span the **terminal width** and a tidy header/footer (`▶ <blueprint> · N modules · run <id> · spark <master>` … `✓ complete`); the `run_id` appears once (header), not duplicated in the footer. Compiler warnings are now collapsed into one tidy block — `⚠ N warnings · -v for full text` with a one-line summary per rule (`· [rule_id] <summary>`); `-v` prints the full message text. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/executor/spark/session.py`)
- **Storage-integrity guardrail — local-blob-under-remote-obs warning.** If `stores.observability.backend` is remote (postgres) but `stores.blob.backend` is left unset (defaults to `local`), Aqueduct warns at config load that externalised blobs (manifests/stack traces/provenance) will be written to the *driver's* local disk. Fires only when blob was *not* explicitly chosen — an explicit `stores.blob.backend: local` is silent (informed choice), and unrelated configs never trigger it.
- **`aqueduct studio` — interactive read-only TUI.** A `textual` terminal app (optional `tui` extra: `pip install aqueduct-core[tui]`) for inspecting the observability store: browse runs → module results + resource profile, an ad-hoc **read-only** SQL pane over the embedded DuckDB, a doctor view (`skip_spark`), a config/deployment inspector (structural fields only — no secrets), and a column-lineage view. No server and no production control — a transient local dev surface (works over SSH). The query layer (`aqueduct/tui/data.py`) is `textual`/`pyspark`-free and unit-tested; the `tui` extra is a documented dev-tooling exception to the extras policy (kept out of base and `[all]`).
- **`aqueduct report --format html`.** Self-contained, single-file HTML run report (run status/timing + per-module results + the Phase-62 resource profile), emitted to stdout (redirect to a file). No server and no external assets — renders offline. Pure read-side over existing observability data; HTML-escaped. First slice of the monitor-surfaces work.
- **Per-Blueprint compile-warning suppression.** A Blueprint YAML may now declare its own `warnings.suppress` block, scoped to that Blueprint's compile pass — covering both the modular rule registry (`aqueduct/compiler/warnings/run_all` — e.g. `file_format_no_repartition`, `jdbc_missing_partition`) and the inline compiler checks (e.g. `perf_python_udf_row_at_a_time`, `delivery_append_retry_dupes`). It is unioned with the engine-level `warnings.suppress` (`aqueduct.yml` + `--suppress-warning`), so either side suppressing a rule silences it, but never affects session-startup warnings, runtime (Probe/Assert) warnings, or the process-global default other blueprints see. `"*"` silences all compile warnings for that one Blueprint. On an Arcade sub-Blueprint, the parent's suppress list covers the whole expanded compilation unit — the sub-Blueprint's own `warnings:` block parses (valid Blueprint on its own) but is not consulted. (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `aqueduct/parser/models.py`, `aqueduct/parser/parser.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`)

- **Conditional module execution (`enabled:`).** Every module accepts `enabled:` (default `true`) — a bool or a `${ctx.*}`/`${ENV}` expression, so a context profile can toggle whole pipeline sections (`aqueduct run bp.yml -p dev`). A disabled module compiles but is skipped (⏭ with a reason in the summary), and the disable **cascades at compile time** to every consumer of its output — via edges, `depends_on`, or Probe `attach_to` — transitively and uniformly (a join/union missing one input never runs partially). A disabled Arcade disables all expanded children (`arcade '<id>' disabled`). Disabled modules are excluded from compile-time warnings; disabling every module is a `CompileError`. specs.md §Modules table + SKILL.md + blueprint template updated, specs Version 2.2 → 2.3. (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `parser/models.py`, `parser/parser.py`, `compiler/expander.py`, `compiler/compiler.py`, `compiler/models.py`, `executor/spark/executor.py`, `cli/run.py`)
- **Blueprint lifecycle hooks (`hooks:`).** A Blueprint may declare `hooks.on_success` / `hooks.on_failure` — sequential actions fired after the run's terminal state, each entry exactly one of: `blueprint:` (chain another Blueprint as a fresh `aqueduct run` subprocess — own session/run_id/report, cycle-guarded via `AQUEDUCT_HOOK_CHAIN` with a depth cap of 8), `webhook:` (same endpoint model + payload templating as the engine-level `webhooks:` block; bare-URL shorthand supported), or `command:` (subprocess with shlex argv, no shell, `${run.id}`/`${run.status}`/`${blueprint.id}` interpolation, per-entry `timeout:`) — the latter gated by the new **`danger.allow_command_hooks`** flag in aqueduct.yml (operator-owned, so a Blueprint/LLM patch cannot self-authorize; startup danger warning when enabled; no patch-grammar op can address `hooks:`). Hook outcomes never change the run's exit code — a failing hook warns (`[hook_failed]`) and skips the event's remaining hooks; a rendered hooks section closes with a `✓ run complete` footer. `aqueduct doctor` gained a `hooks` check that statically walks `blueprint:` chains for cycles/missing targets. Engine-level `webhooks:` in aqueduct.yml is unchanged and fires independently. specs.md §4.2 (Version 2.4 → 2.5), SKILL.md, both templates, cli_reference.md updated; 16 unit tests. (`aqueduct/parser/schema.py`, `parser/models.py`, `parser/parser.py`, `compiler/models.py`, `compiler/compiler.py`, `config.py`, `cli/hooks.py` (new), `cli/run.py`, `doctor/__init__.py`)
- **Probe `report: stdout` — signal results in the terminal.** A Probe may declare `report: stdout` in its config: each signal's result then also prints under the Probe's row in the post-run summary as dim `↳` note lines — single-value payloads on one line (`row_count_estimate: method=sample · estimate=1234`), dict/tabular payloads one line per entry, capped at 10 lines unless `-v`. Purely additive: signals persist to `probe_signals` exactly as before, sampling governance and `danger.allow_full_probe_actions` apply unchanged, and notes are never counted in the runtime warning roll-up (new `ModuleResult.notes` field, serialized in `ExecutionResult.to_dict`). specs.md Probe section (Version 2.3 → 2.4), SKILL.md, blueprint template updated. (`aqueduct/executor/spark/probe.py`, `executor/spark/executor.py`, `executor/models.py`, `cli/run.py`)
- **Run preamble surfaces overrides, context, and secrets provider.** `aqueduct run` now prints a dim `· overrides ·` info line (stderr, next to the `· env ·` notice) whenever `--set`, `--ctx`, or `--profile` shape the run — `--set` shows dotted key paths only (values may embed unregistered secrets), `--ctx` shows a key count, `--profile` the profile name — plus a `· secrets · provider: <name>` line when a non-default (`env`) secrets provider is configured. Nothing prints on a plain run. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`)

### Changed
- **End-of-run runtime-warning roll-up.** Runtime warnings (`runtime_assert`, `runtime_probe_blocked`, `runtime_probe_signal_error`, …) still print inline under their module (`↳ ⚠ [rule_id] …`, for locality), and now *also* collapse into a single `⚠ runtime: N warnings · -v for full text` block just before the run footer — so on a busy/long run nothing that warned is buried in the scroll. Same shape as the `⚠ compile:` block (shared `style.emit_warning_pairs`; full text on `-v`); empty → nothing. Aggregated from the structural `ModuleResult.warnings`, not re-parsed from logs. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/style.py`)
- **Spillway-wiring mismatch is now a compile warning, not a mid-run log.** A Channel with `spillway_condition` but no `port: spillway` edge (rows silently routed to main), or a spillway edge with no `spillway_condition` (spillway always empty), was warned at runtime from the executor — a static, Manifest-level condition emitted in the wrong place. It is now the suppressible compile rule **`spillway_port_mismatch`**, so it appears in the grouped `⚠ compile` block before execution and can be silenced via `warnings.suppress`. Executor behaviour (rows to main / empty spillway) is unchanged.
- **Warnings now follow the run's lifecycle around the header divider.** Blueprint/compile warnings (`perf_*`, `file_format_no_repartition`, …) and Spark-session warnings now print **after** the `▶ <blueprint>` header (the header names the blueprint they're about) rather than before it; engine/config-level notices (env loaded, config warnings) stay above the header, and runtime probe/assert warnings come later, during execution. The header reads as a divider: setup/engine context above, this-run details below.
- **Runtime log output matches the CLI's visual vocabulary.** Text-mode logging now renders through a `StyledLogFormatter` — `⚠`/`✗`/`·` icons (yellow/red/dim, colour gated on a TTY) instead of the raw `WARNING: …` prefix — so a mid-run `Assert`/`Probe` warning looks like the compile-warning block. Assert warnings no longer leak the enum `repr` (`<AssertRuleType.FRESHNESS: 'freshness'>` → `[freshness]`). The `.env`-loaded notice is now a dim `· env  ·  loaded N var(s) from …` preamble line consistent with the rest of the header. The self-healing-unreachable notice now colours the whole line yellow (via `style.warn`) instead of a bold-yellow icon with white text. (`--log-format json` is unchanged.)
- **Lint backlog cleared — `ruff check aqueduct/` is green.** Package-wide pyupgrade sweep (`UP037` quoted-annotation removal, `UP017` `datetime.UTC`, `UP035` `collections.abc` imports), import-sort, dead-import/variable removal, and `logger` relocation so module imports sit at the top. No behaviour change. Also fixed a real annotation bug surfaced by the sweep: `executor.py` referenced `ProbeSampling` in a type annotation without a `TYPE_CHECKING` import (`F821`).
- **Clearer agent self-healing CLI messaging.** User-facing heal-loop output in `aqueduct run` / `aqueduct heal` now says **"Agent"** (the product concept) instead of "LLM" — e.g. `Agent patch validated and applied`, `Agent self-healing`, `Agent guardrail blocked`, `Agent rate-limit reached`, `Agent not reachable`. Technically-precise references (LLM-call/token-count metrics, the model endpoint, internal logs, code comments) keep "LLM". Additionally, `aqueduct run` now warns **upfront** (right after the run banner) when self-healing is *enabled* (`agent.approval` is `human`/`auto`/`ci`) but the agent is unreachable (no API key / base_url), so the misconfiguration is visible before a failure rather than only at heal time. It stays silent when healing is `disabled` — the default — so a blueprint with no `agent:` block, or one that only sets `budget`/`memory`/connection without `approval:`, never triggers it.
- **`depot_kv` DDL unified across backends.** The DuckDB and Postgres relational depot stores shared an identical `depot_kv` schema as two separate `_DDL` literals (drift risk); both now bind a single `DEPOT_KV_DDL` constant in `stores/ddl.py`. DDL owned by one layer stays with its owner (`patch_index`, observability tables, `benchmark_results`). No schema change.
- **LLM defaults hoisted to named constants** (`DEFAULT_MAX_TOKENS`, `DEFAULT_LLM_TIMEOUT`, `ANTHROPIC_API_VERSION`, `DEFAULT_LLM_MODEL`) in `agent/constants.py`. Imported at every provider/loop/cascade/prompts/config call-site; values unchanged. The ruamel YAML `width=4096` in `patch/` is deliberately left as a bare literal (different semantic).
- **`cli/run.py` `run()` decomposed into phase helpers.** Three module-level helpers (`_load_engine_config`, `_do_compile`, `_setup_surveyor`) extract config-load, parse/compile, and surveyor/session wiring from the `run()` god-function. Each takes explicit parameters and returns a frozen dataclass; behaviour is unchanged. The remote-submit, sandbox, and heal-loop phases remain in `run()` pending a follow-up extraction.
- **`surveyor/surveyor.py` split for readability.** DDL string constants moved to `surveyor/ddl.py` and structured Spark/Py4J error extraction to `surveyor/error_extraction.py`; `surveyor.py` re-imports both so `aqueduct.surveyor.surveyor.*` names are unchanged for callers and tests. The lazy `pyspark`/`py4j` imports stay inside the extraction function, preserving the `pyspark`-free top-level import. No schema or behaviour change.
- **Clearer compile error for `on_fail: quarantine` on non-quarantineable rules.** The rejection message now distinguishes "no per-row predicate" (aggregate rules: `min_rows`, `max_rows`, `sql`) from "population gate" (`null_rate` — sampled alarm; quarantine would mask the signal and add a full scan). Points `null_rate` users at the new `not_null` rule.
- **Consistent colored error output across CLI commands.** New `aqueduct/cli/style.py` centralizes icons, colors, error/status/warning rendering so all commands share a single presentation vocabulary. `aqueduct doctor` preamble is dimmed and warnings are collapsed into the standard `\u26a0 N warnings` block (no more raw mid-grid `AQ-WARN` lines).
- **`probes.max_sample_rows` and `probes.default_sample_fraction` are now honoured by the probe executor.** `max_sample_rows` caps `sample_rows` `n`; `default_sample_fraction` provides the fleet-wide default for sample-based signals (overridden by per-probe `fraction:`). `default_sample_fraction` default changed from `0.01` to `0.1` to preserve prior probe behaviour (the old inline hardcoded default was `0.1`).
- **Coverage moved out of default `pytest` addopts into a dedicated CI job.** `--cov=aqueduct --cov-report=term-missing` is no longer in `pyproject.toml` `addopts` — default test runs are coverage-free for Spark-session stability. The 80% minimum is now enforced by a dedicated `coverage` job in the CI workflow (`pytest --cov=aqueduct --cov-fail-under=80 -m "not spark"`), which excludes Spark tests (the `--cov`+shared-session combo is the root cause of ~154 flaky Py4JJavaError failures).
- **Downstream layers import boundary types from `aqueduct.models`.** New shared import surface: `Manifest`, `Module`, `Edge`, `ModuleType`, `RetryPolicy` re-exported from `aqueduct/models.py`. Executor and surveyor no longer reach into `parser.models` / `compiler.models` internals. Definitions remain in their original modules — strictly additive.
- **`engine: flink` is now rejected at config-load** with a clear "not yet supported" message (was silently accepted with undefined behaviour). See `docs/roadmap.md`.
- **Backend-aware observability reads.** Read commands (`report`, `runs`, `lineage`, `report --profile`, `report --trend`, the `patch` index lookup, and `aqueduct studio`) now resolve the observability store through a single canonical helper (`aqueduct.stores.read.open_obs_read`) instead of opening DuckDB files directly with hand-built `.aqueduct/observability/...` paths. This fixes commands that previously ignored a non-default `stores.observability.path`, and lets the plain-SQL read commands work against a Postgres observability backend (not just DuckDB). `runs` no longer relies on DuckDB-specific `json_extract_string`. (`heal` still reads DuckDB only — it couples to the store file for blob materialization; `report --trend` still uses DuckDB-specific JSON SQL.)

- **Error-taxonomy hardening — user-facing failures now carry an `AqueductError` type.** An invariant-audit sweep reclassified raw `ValueError`/`RuntimeError` raises that reach the user into the existing hierarchy so callers/CLI can key off the type: parser graph + context resolution → `ParseError`; `@aq.*` compile-time resolution → `CompileError` (and a `SecretsError` is no longer flattened into a bare `RuntimeError`); Databricks config/auth → `ConfigError`, Databricks network/DBFS → a new `DeployError(AqueductError)`; missing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`/`agent.base_url` and budget-config validators → `ConfigError`; LLM response/stream errors → `AqueductError`. Pydantic validators keep raising `ValueError` (required for `ValidationError`), and internal-invariant guards stay raw. `ParseError`/`CompileError`/`ConfigError` now live in `aqueduct/errors.py` (re-exported from their old modules — single class identity, existing imports/`except` clauses unaffected). (`aqueduct/errors.py`, `aqueduct/parser/*`, `aqueduct/compiler/*`, `aqueduct/config.py`, `aqueduct/deploy/databricks.py`, `aqueduct/agent/{providers,budget}.py`)
- **BREAKING: the DuckDB observability path is a routing DIRECTORY only — the single-shared-file mode is removed.** A `.db`-suffixed `stores.observability.path` now fails config load with migration guidance. Why: the explicit-file mode had a silent read/write split-brain — writers dropped the configured basename (data always landed at `<parent>/observability.db`) while readers honoured it, so any basename except the magic default produced "no runs found" against a green pipeline; and one shared DuckDB file was never parallel-safe (single-writer) anyway. The two remaining layouts are unchanged: default → `.aqueduct/observability/<blueprint_id>/observability.db`, custom directory → same split under your dir. For one merged concurrent store use `backend: postgres`; cross-blueprint *reads* over routed files already work (fleet commands aggregate across `<base>/*/observability.db`). `aqueduct signal` gained a required `--blueprint <id>` (duckdb, without `--store-dir`) — previously its override could land in a store no run ever read. Also swept the three conflicting default sentinels (config `None`, the literal `.aqueduct/observability.db` string, suffix heuristics) down to one. specs §10.4.1 (Version 2.5 → 2.6), both templates, production_guide updated. (`aqueduct/config.py`, `cli/run.py`, `cli/observability.py`, `stores/read.py`, `stores/queries.py`, `stores/base.py`)
- **BREAKING: 1.x back-compat shims removed (2.0 cleanup).** The pre-routing flat `.aqueduct/observability.db` read fallback in `runs`; the pre-Phase-53 watermark-sidecar one-time migration (`_migrate_legacy_watermark_sidecar` — the Depot has been the sole watermark store since Phase 53); the `style_heal_line` back-compat alias (use `colorize_line`). A 1.x upgrade that still depends on any of these should upgrade through a 1.x→2.0 run first or reset local state. (`aqueduct/cli/observability.py`, `executor/spark/executor.py`, `cli/style.py`)
- **CI restructured — no more double runs.** The test suite's `pull_request` trigger is gone: a push to a `phase/**`/`feat/**` branch already proves the SHA, and the PR to main re-ran the identical suite on the identical commit. `main` joined the push list so post-merge skew is still caught; the coverage gate now runs on main pushes (+ `workflow_dispatch`) instead of every PR. The slow full-run `snippets` job moved from the per-push suite into the Compatibility Matrix workflow (main pushes + dispatch) — snippets are end-to-end compat checks; the fast `gallery-tests` parse/compile guard still runs on branch pushes. Release notes: the GitHub Release step gained an **opt-in** LLM polish (repo var `LLM_RELEASE_NOTES=true` + `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` secret) that rewrites the extracted CHANGELOG section highlights-first — with `continue-on-error` and empty-response guards, so any failure publishes the raw CHANGELOG section unchanged (no hard dependency). (`.github/workflows/test-suite.yml`, `version-matrix.yml`, `release.yml`)
- **`maintenance.optimize` on a non-Delta Egress is now a compile ERROR** (was warning `maintenance_optimize_non_delta`, no longer suppressible). The failure is deterministic — format is known at compile time and `OPTIMIZE` cannot succeed on a non-Delta table — so the run is never allowed to start. (`aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `docs/spark_guide.md`)
- **Warning-rule audit — five rules had stale claims or unsafe advice; all corrected and pinned by tests.** `delivery_append_retry_dupes` no longer suggests bare `mode=overwrite` (destroys history on incremental sinks) — advice is now Delta `txnAppId`/`txnVersion` idempotent writes or downstream dedup. `perf_incremental_watermark_scan` described a "second DAG scan" from an older implementation — the executor reads the WRITTEN output (Delta ≈ txn-log statistics, near metadata-only), so the message now states the real cost, the bogus upstream-checkpoint exemption is gone, and all-Delta downstream outputs are exempt entirely. `perf_python_udf_row_at_a_time` is skipped when the Blueprint enables Arrow-optimized Python UDFs (`spark.sql.execution.pythonUDF.arrow.enabled`, Spark 3.5+) and the message names that escape hatch. `perf_delta_append_no_partition` no longer double-signals when the same Egress has `maintenance.optimize`. `perf_multi_consumer_no_cache` and `nondeterministic_fanout` said "add a Checkpoint *upstream*" while their code checks the Channel's OWN checkpoint flag — advice now points at the channel itself, with a cache-tradeoff caveat. `docs/spark_guide.md` sections rewritten to match. New `tests/test_compiler/test_warning_rules_audit.py` pins each load-bearing message fragment so future claim-drift breaks a test instead of rotting silently. (`aqueduct/compiler/compiler.py`, `aqueduct/compiler/warnings/nondeterministic_fanout.py`)
- **Run preamble: info lines and the danger block no longer interleave.** The `⚠ danger: N warnings` startup block used to print between the dim `· env ·` and `· overrides ·`/`· secrets ·` info lines (emission order followed code path, not presentation). `_load_engine_config` now returns the danger pairs instead of emitting them, and `run()` prints the block after the info preamble — order is now `· env ·` → `· overrides ·` → `· secrets ·` → `⚠ danger` → `▶` run header. Same content, same rule_ids, still above the header. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`)
- **Arcade modules render as a tree in the run summary.** Arcade-expanded children (`{arcade}__{child}`) no longer print as flat rows with mangled ids — they nest under a synthetic parent row (`├─`/`└─` glyphs, child name only), the parent showing the worst child status (any ✗ → ✗, else any ✓ → ✓, else ⏭). Display-only: runtime logs, observability rows, and the `failed_module=` footer keep the full flattened id so error correlation stays joinable. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`)

### Removed
- **BREAKING: deprecated agent/danger aliases removed.** Gone: the `agent.approval_mode` YAML key (use `agent.approval`), `agent.aggressive_max_patches` (use `agent.max_patches`), `danger.allow_aggressive_patching` (use `danger.allow_multi_patch`), and the `--allow-aggressive` CLI flag (use `--allow-multi-patch`). These now error (`extra=forbid`) instead of parsing with a deprecation warning. NOTE: `approval: aggressive` is still a **valid value** (it selects the multi-patch heal path); collapsing it into `auto`+`max_patches>1` is a separate heal-loop change, not done here.
- **BREAKING (2.0): legacy `stores.lineage:` and flat `stores.depot:` blocks now error.** Both were tolerated by load-time shims (`_strip_removed_lineage_block` stripped `stores.lineage:` with a warning; `_migrate_depot_block` auto-migrated a flat `stores.depot:` mapping into `stores.depots.default`). Those shims are **deleted** — a `stores.lineage:` block or a flat `stores.depot:` mapping now raises a `ConfigError` (`extra=forbid`). Column lineage lives entirely in the observability store (`bundle.lineage` aliases it); depot mounts use the name-keyed `stores.depots:` map (`depots.default:` for the implicit mount). All bundled examples/templates already use `stores.depots:`. (`aqueduct/config.py`)
- **BREAKING (2.0): `cfg.stores.depot` shorthand property removed.** The back-compat `@property` is gone; call `cfg.stores.default_depot()` (synthesizes the implicit DuckDB default when no `default` mount) or index `cfg.stores.depots["default"]`. (`aqueduct/config.py`)
- **BREAKING (2.0): deprecated `benchmark` flags removed.** `--provider` / `--base-url` / `--timeout` / `--no-persist` / `--store-path` / `--gate-on-regression` on `aqueduct benchmark` now error (click "no such option"); use `--set agent.*` / `--set stores.benchmark.*`. (`benchmark-diff` / `benchmark-stats` keep `--store-path`.) The flag-only `--timeout 0` "unbounded" sentinel is gone (config `agent.timeout` is `gt=0`; unbounded = `null`). (`aqueduct/cli/benchmark.py`)
- **BREAKING (2.0): renamed-symbol aliases removed.** `execute_sql_channel` → `execute_channel`, `MAX_REPROMPTS` → `BudgetConfig.max_reprompts` (default 3), `StoreBackendConfig` → `RelationalStoreConfig`. All were internal re-exports. (`aqueduct/executor/spark/channel.py`, `aqueduct/agent/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`)
- **BREAKING (2.0): schema-migration DDLs dropped — 2.0 assumes a fresh DB.** The `healing_outcomes` `_PHASE45_MIGRATION_DDL` ALTERs and the `benchmark_results` additive ALTERs are deleted; the `CREATE TABLE`s already declare every column, so a fresh observability/benchmark store is complete, but a **pre-2.0 DB is no longer auto-migrated** and must be recreated (pair with an ops-side wipe). (`aqueduct/surveyor/ddl.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/surveyor.py`, `aqueduct/surveyor/benchmark_store.py`)

### Fixed
- **Webhook/OpenLineage delivery no longer prints raw URLs past the redaction layer.** `infra/http.py` logged delivery attempts/failures with bare `print(url, file=sys.stderr)` from daemon threads — bypassing both the `click.echo` wrapper and the root logger's redacting filter, so a URL carrying a registered secret could surface in clear. Now routed through `logger.warning(...)`, which the `_RedactingFilter` covers. The `doctor` Redis check also masks the password in the DSN it echoes (matching the Postgres path). (`aqueduct/infra/http.py`, `aqueduct/doctor/checks_io.py`)
- **`import aqueduct.dashboard` no longer pulls `pyspark` at module import.** The early-init `import pyspark.sql` (a narwhals/plotly circular-import workaround) was module-level, violating the "pyspark only under `executor/spark/`" rule and loading Spark for a headless viewer; it's now lazy inside the entrypoint. (`aqueduct/dashboard/app.py`)
- **Fixed a circular import surfaced while hardening the error taxonomy.** Moving `ConfigError` consumers to import it caused `config → agent.constants → agent.budget → config` to fail on a partially-initialised module; `ConfigError` now lives in `aqueduct/errors.py` (re-exported from `aqueduct.config`), breaking the cycle with no change to `from aqueduct.config import ConfigError` call-sites. (`aqueduct/errors.py`, `aqueduct/config.py`)
- **`benchmark-diff` now resolves its store from `stores.benchmark`, and the dashboard finds blueprints outside its CWD.** Two more "hardcoded-local vs configured" slips found by the 2.0 regression sweep: (a) `aqueduct benchmark-diff` ignored config entirely — it read a hardcoded `./.aqueduct/benchmark.duckdb` (only a `--store-path` override), so a configured `stores.benchmark.path` was missed; it now loads config (`--config`/env) + resolves via `BenchmarkStore.from_config`, like `benchmark`/`benchmark-stats` (a postgres benchmark backend is reported unsupported for diff rather than mishandled). (b) the dashboard's `_find_blueprint_file` searched only the CWD's `blueprints/`, so a dashboard launched from elsewhere (systemd/container) couldn't find blueprints — it now also derives the project root from `AQ_DASH_CONFIG`/`AQ_DASH_STORE_DIR`. Minor: `runs` uses the `_OBS_ROUTING_ROOT` constant instead of a duplicated literal (it already honoured `--store-dir`/configured path), and the sandbox `run_id` is a full uuid (was truncated to 8 hex when unnamed → unqueryable). (`aqueduct/cli/benchmark.py`, `aqueduct/dashboard/app.py`, `aqueduct/cli/observability.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`)
- **`aqueduct heal` and `aqueduct drift` now stage patches to the configured `stores.blob` backend, not a hardcoded local dir.** Both called `stage_patch_for_human(...)` without a `patch_store=`, so `agent/loop._patch_store_for` fell back to `make_patch_store("local", …)` — on a non-local backend (s3/gcs/adls) the patch body landed on the **local FS** while `patch list`/`apply`/`pull` (which read the configured store) never saw it. They now build the store from `cfg.stores.blob` and pass it (the `run` command already did this via `surveyor.patch_store()`), and the `heal` success hint points at the backend location + `patch apply <patch_id>` rather than a local path. (`aqueduct/cli/heal.py`, `aqueduct/cli/drift.py`)
- **`patch apply`/`reject` now move the patch body *in the configured store* (not just a local copy), and the heal-cache index follows — no more pending/index drift on remote backends.** The CLI lifecycle did raw local-FS writes (`write_text`/`unlink`) and called `set_status` **without** an `object_key`. So on a non-local `stores.blob` (s3/gcs/adls): `apply <id>`/`reject <id>` mutated a *materialised local copy* while the remote `pending/` body stayed put (→ `patch list` still showed it pending), and the index `object_key` kept pointing at the stale `pending/` key (→ the heal cache's `find_replay` would fetch the wrong location). Now `apply_patch_file`/`reject_patch` take a `PatchStore` + pending key and do a real store move — `write_applied`/`write_rejected` + `delete(pending/<key>)` (a local rename for `fs`, an object move for `s3`) — and `set_status` records the new `object_key`, so the relational index (which the heal cache reads) stays consistent with the store. `patch_id` is the model's **non-unique** semantic label, so `patch list` now shows the unique timestamped **`file`** and `apply`/`reject` accept either a bare `patch_id` (when unique) or the full filename (required, with the colliding files listed, when several share an id). `commit`/`discard` stay git-on-local. (`aqueduct/patch/apply.py`, `aqueduct/cli/patch.py`)
- **Patch commands (and `compile`) now auto-discover `aqueduct.yml` from CWD + load `.env`, like every other command.** They called `load_config(None)`, which does **not** walk up from the working directory to find `aqueduct.yml` and does **not** load `.env` — so running `aqueduct patch list` from a project dir silently fell back to scanning the local `./patches` (and required `--config aqueduct.yml` to actually see a remote store). Now `patch list` / `apply` / `pull` and the shared `_patch_index_obs_store` (→ `reject`/`discard`/`commit`) and `compile` resolve config through `_load_config_with_env` (the same `_resolve_project_root` CWD walk-up + env loading `run`/`heal`/`doctor` use). Audited: no `load_config(None)` remains in `aqueduct/cli/`. (`aqueduct/cli/patch.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`)
- **CLI status output is coloured at one choke-point (no more per-line "uncoloured `✗`" leaks), and duplicate `AQ-WARN`s are gone.** Three recurring, whack-a-mole classes of bug fixed structurally rather than line-by-line: (1) **colour** — `click.echo` is wrapped once at top level (text mode) with `style.colorize_line`, which colours the icon vocabulary (`✓` green / `✗` red / `⚠` yellow / `ⓘ◆↻▸` cyan; `│ ├─ └─ ┆ ↳ ↑` dimmed) on any line that *starts* with a status/tree glyph — so every status line (current and future) is styled automatically, while `--format json` and prose stay untouched and already-styled lines aren't double-processed; (2) **warnings** — after the blueprint's compile warnings are shown once (grouped) under the run header, `AqueductWarning` is suppressed for the rest of the run, so the many re-compiles (heal re-runs, zero-token replay, sandbox/explain gates) stop leaking the raw `AQ-WARN [rule_id] …` fallback mid-execution; (3) **replay UX** — a zero-token heal-cache *replay* no longer prints the full agent-self-healing scaffolding (header / model ceremony / "waiting for first token" / `└─ ✗ replayed`) for a run where no agent is called — it shows just the cache announcement + the apply result. (`aqueduct/cli/style.py`, `aqueduct/cli/__init__.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`)
- **Heal output polish: no raw `AQ-WARN` mid-heal, coloured apply lines, clearer tokens, agent header.** Four UX gaps in the self-healing display: (1) the patched **re-compile** inside the heal loop re-emitted the blueprint's compile warnings through the raw `AQ-WARN [rule_id] …` fallback formatter (they were already shown, grouped, under the run header) — those duplicates are now suppressed on the re-compile; (2) the `✓ Agent patch validated and applied → …` / `✗ … did not fix` lines were raw uncoloured `click.echo` — now green/red; (3) per-turn token usage reads `11,866 in → 268 out` instead of the cryptic `11866→268 tok`; (4) the heal block now opens with an agent "ceremony" line (`◆ <model> · <provider> · ≤N reprompts`, or `cascade · N tier(s) · m1 → m2`) so the model on the job is always visible up front. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/agent/transcript.py`)
- **`aqueduct patch list` now reads the configured patch store, and the patch index stays in sync on remote backends.** `patch list` scanned the local `<blueprint-dir>/patches/` directory via `glob`, so with a non-local `stores.blob` backend (s3/gcs/adls) — where self-healing stages the patch body — it reported "No pending patches" even though one was pending. It now lists the **patch store directly** (local *or* object backend) via `PatchStore.iter_payloads` — the source of truth — resolving the backend from `aqueduct.yml` (`--config` / CWD) **plus `.env`** (so configs/blueprints that reference env or `@aq.secret()` resolve instead of silently failing to the local dir). The same `.env` fix in `_patch_index_obs_store` means the lifecycle commands' best-effort `patch_index` status updates (`apply`/`reject`/`commit`/`discard` → `set_status`) actually reach a remote obs store, keeping the index consistent. (`aqueduct/cli/patch.py`) `aqueduct patch apply`, `reject`, and `import` now also accept a bare **`patch_id`** (not just a local file) and **auto-fetch the body from the configured store** (via `_materialize_patch_by_id`) — so the remote workflow is `patch list` → `patch apply <id>` / `patch reject <id>` with no manual `patch pull`. `commit` and `discard` are git operations on the *local* blueprint (they finalise/revert what `apply` already wrote + archived locally), so they need no store fetch — only the config/env auto-discovery above.
- **Explain-gate plan regressions are a standard `rule_id` warning, on both heal paths.** The "patched plan gained N shuffle node(s)" notice was a raw `click.echo` printed only on the LLM-apply path — so a zero-token *replay* of an archived patch silently dropped it. It is now `⚠ [explain_regression] …` through the output funnel (greppable, suppressible) and emitted on **both** the LLM-apply and the replay paths. (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`)
- **Benign response recoveries no longer block auto-apply.** A self-healing patch was downgraded to human review whenever the parser applied *any* mechanical recovery — including the purely structural locators that strip a ```` ```json ```` fence, a `<think>` block, or leading prose (deterministic, content-preserving). Local/reasoning models fence their output by default — and on the streaming path Aqueduct cannot request `json_object` — so `approval: auto` heals were *always* downgraded. Now only **content-reinterpreting** recoveries (`json_repair`, comment stripping, wrapper unwrap) downgrade to human; structural strips auto-apply (the parsed patch is byte-identical to the model's, minus the wrapper). (`aqueduct/cli/run.py`)
- **Budget-deadline heal timeouts now explain the right knob, and a progress cue shows the agent is working.** When a provider call hit the total-heal `agent.budget.max_seconds` budget (default 300s) it timed out with a loose `⚠ LLM API call timed out on budget deadline 300.0s` line interleaved above the tree and **no guidance** — so raising `agent.timeout` alone (a per-call socket timeout, a different knob) never helped. That warning is demoted to DEBUG; the turn now reads `✗ budget exhausted — hit the 300s heal budget mid-call` with an `ⓘ` hint naming `agent.budget.max_seconds` (and showing the current `agent.timeout`). A `⏳ contacting agent…` cue prints before the synchronous provider call so a slow local model doesn't look hung. (`aqueduct/agent/loop.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`)
- **Heal conversation tree is now coloured, and the misleading cost estimate is gone.** The tree glyphs take the CLI's semantic colours (`✓` green, `✗` red, `⚠` yellow, `ⓘ` cyan) with the rails (`│ ├─ └─ ┆`) dimmed so structure recedes and content stands out (colour gated on a TTY, via a CLI-side `style_heal_line` so the `TranscriptWriter` stays engine-agnostic). Per-turn usage now shows tokens only (`<in>→<out> tok`, input prompt → output patch); the `≈$` dollar estimate was removed — a hardcoded price table is plain wrong for self-hosted models (an Ollama call is free, not the cloud deepseek/qwen rate) and only approximate for cloud ones, i.e. false precision. (`aqueduct/cli/style.py`, `aqueduct/agent/transcript.py`, `aqueduct/cli/run.py`, `aqueduct/cli/heal.py`)
- **`aqueduct run -v` now shows the model's raw response per heal turn.** Each turn in the verbose transcript prints the model's verbatim output under a `┆` gutter (capped at 40 lines) alongside the parse/gate verdict, the parsed ops/rationale/root-cause, and the reprompt fed back — so when a patch won't parse you can see *exactly* what the model returned (e.g. prose wrapped around the JSON, a trailing comma, a hallucinated op) and judge whether the prompt or the blueprint needs more guiding context. The raw text is captured transiently per turn and never persisted to `heal_attempts`. (Live token-by-token streaming + reasoning "thinking" remain a separate follow-up.) (`aqueduct/agent/loop.py`, `aqueduct/agent/transcript.py`)
